# Ply — Architecture

## What it is

A workspace shell for macOS and Windows. One window, multiple workspaces, each bound to a project directory. The repository, Rust crate, and some intentionally retained internal identifiers still use `atelier`; the shipped product, bundle, and user-facing name are Ply.

Workspace layout (`NewWorkspaceShell`): a navigation sidebar on the left, a mode-switched center stage, and contextual Knowledge on the right. The center hosts Inbox, Board, Code, Terminals, and Activity. Opening a card reuses the Board center for the card lifecycle. Warmed Terminals and card-lifecycle hosts stay mounted and use CSS visibility so switching modes or workspaces never kills their PTYs. The pre-v0.5 shell and the old right-tab navigation model are retired.

**Navigation sidebar (v1.82.0, Part 2 P2-4):** Ply's primary navigation. The global zone contains Inbox, Activity, and Commands. Each workspace has one row; only the active workspace expands the scoped Board, Code, and Terminals modes beneath it. The footer owns environment health, drain/ship state, the contextual-Knowledge toggle, and Settings. The earlier multi-expand rows, two-tier switcher/detail pane, and top AppToolbar are retired.

**Terminals mode / tile grid (v0.5.0, re-hosted center-stage v1.67.0):** reads the per-workspace tile list and auto-arranges it via `gridLayout(N)`. A tile body is `Terminal` (xterm + portable-pty) for shell/Claude tiles or `AgentRunView compact` for agent-run tiles. The warmed tile-grid stack remains mounted and becomes CSS-hidden when another center mode owns the stage. Drop targets accept paths from FilesPanel and external Finder drops (v0.7.3), shell-quoted into the active PTY.

**Knowledge and liveness (GH #95, ADR 0005):** the pinned right panel and the Live rail are retired; the centre runs to the window's right edge. Knowledge is a temporary centre mode (`CenterMode` `"knowledge"`, sidebar entry, palette, ⌘⇧E) until the Library replaces it. Live sessions (Builder sessions of cards, then the grid's tiles) are listed in the sidebar under the active workspace's Terminals entry through the pure `lib/sessionRows.ts`. Persisted historical `pinnedRightTabs` values still decode and sanitize to `knowledge`; the frontend ignores them.

**Agents (v0.3.0+):** first-class persisted configs. System prompt, prompt template, runtime mode, allowed tools; an optional cron schedule and file-glob / agent-finished triggers still load from old files but, since GH #171, nothing acts on them — an agent runs only when someone starts it. Workspace-scoped (`agents/workspaces/<wsId>/<id>.json`) or project-scoped (`<cwd>/.atelier/agents/<id>.json`). v0.7.4: card-driven runs use card content as the prompt (bypassing the agent's stored template). v0.9.0 G1: oneshot runs spawn with `--output-format stream-json --verbose`; the PTY data listener parses NDJSON to extract text + tokens + cost + model, persists usage on the run record. Since the `claude -p` follow-up to PR #374 a Claude oneshot run is Claude's interactive TUI collected through its transcript (`PtyRegistry::begin_collect` + `CollectRun::wait` on its own thread), whose JSON lines the same parser reads; no cost is reported. v0.9.0 F1: pre-spawn snapshot of the cwd; post-exit diff written to `snapshots/<runId>/changes.json`.

**Board and card lifecycle (v0.6.0; current hierarchy v1.81.0+):** the per-workspace Board has three columns: Todo, In progress, and Done. The Done column groups cards under Review and Done according to review/ship state rather than persisting a fourth `in_review` column. Cards carry title, notes, Knowledge/context, optional agent or swarm bindings, and run/review/ship state. Moving Todo → In progress can start the bound card run; selecting any card opens its lifecycle in the warmed center-card host, where intake, execution, verify, review, and ship actions live.

**Prompts library (v0.8.0 D1; re-homed by Part 2):** saved system + user prompts reusable across agents. Prompt and agent/template management now live in Settings and workflow-specific pickers; there is no Prompts right-panel tab.

**Custom tile layouts (v0.8.0 D3; re-homed in UX-1):** users can save preset session lists (`label`, `description`, `tiles: { kind: "shell"|"claude" }[]`). They are optional actions inside Terminals, available from both its empty state and toolbar; creating a workspace never spawns one automatically.

**Command Blocks (v0.10.0):** opt-in OSC 133 detection in terminal panes. Toggle in Settings → Behavior; user pastes a shell integration snippet into `.zshrc`/`.bashrc`. Once enabled, terminal panes track command boundaries (start, end, exit code) and surface a `✓ N · ✗ M` indicator with a popover listing recent commands.

**Quick Open (v0.7.0 A1, ⌘P):** fuzzy file finder per workspace. Backend `fs_list_files_recursive` walks cwd respecting noisy-dir excludes; frontend caches per workspace, fuzzy-filters via `lib/fuzzyScore.ts`.

**Terminal search (v0.7.0 A2, ⌘F):** xterm `addon-search` overlay; intercepts ⌘F via `attachCustomKeyEventHandler` so it doesn't reach the PTY.

**Inline image preview (v0.9.0 C2):** xterm `addon-image` enabled with sixel + iTerm IIP support (each capped at 25 MB).

**Diff reviewer (v0.9.0 F1):** `RunChangesPanel` mounted as a collapsed `<details>` section inside `AgentRunView` for finished runs. Loads `snapshot_get_changes(runId)`, renders changes as a list with click-to-expand inline diff (CodeMirror `@codemirror/merge` for modified, single-pane for added/removed).

**Token + cost tracking (v0.9.0 G1):** `AgentRunView` header shows a compact `1.2k in · 8.4k out · sonnet-4-7 · $0.0042` badge when usage data is present. Tooltip shows full breakdown.

Tauri 2 desktop app, React frontend in WKWebView, Rust backend talking to the OS and `portable-pty`.

## Hard constraints (don't break these)

1. **Claude Code runs as a real PTY subprocess. Never via the Anthropic SDK with an API key.**
   The user pays for Claude through a Max subscription. The Max session token lives in `~/.claude/`. Spawning the `claude` CLI via `portable-pty` with `HOME` passed through inherits that auth. Calling the Anthropic SDK directly with `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` would silently bill per-token and bypass the subscription. Any feature that needs Claude must spawn the CLI in a PTY — that includes manual agent runs, swarm roles and Builder/Challenger sessions.

2. **PATH probe via `bash -lc` is mandatory before any PTY spawn.**
   macOS GUI apps inherit only `/usr/bin:/bin:/usr/sbin:/sbin`. They do not read `.zshrc` / `.bash_profile`. `claude` lives in `~/.npm-global/bin` (or similar). `shell_env::init()` runs once at app start, captures the user's interactive PATH, and every PTY spawn injects it into the child env. Skip this and `claude` won't be found.

3. **Atomic JSON writes for persisted state.**
   `workspaces.json`, `settings.json`, `files-order.json`, every agent config, and every run record are written via tempfile + `rename(2)`. A crash mid-write must not corrupt any of these. See `WorkspaceStore::save_atomic` and `agents::write_atomic_json`.

4. **PTY cleanup on quit, including agents.**
   `run_exit_cleanup` (lib.rs) runs once on the first of `RunEvent::ExitRequested` (`app.exit`) and `RunEvent::Exit` (a quit macOS starts itself — the Dock's Quit, logging out — is `-[NSApp terminate:]` and raises only `Exit`). Every quit Plyloft starts (Plyloft › Quit and Cmd-Q, a custom menu item since QoL 1.168 S1-2; the tray; Ctrl+Shift+Q; the Windows close) first goes through `request_quit`, which asks the page (`app://quit-asked` → `quit_ack`/`quit_answer`) and calls `app.exit` only once nothing would be lost or the person said quit; nothing is prevented inside `ExitRequested`. QoL 1.168 G1: on the Mac, an update scheduled with "Install when my work is done" and already downloaded holds the ack too (`quit_ack` with `ask: true`, `QuitGuardSheet.tsx::installsAtQuit`): the page installs it (at most 90 s, `installAtQuitWithin`; the sheet says "Installing the update…") and only then answers `quit_answer("quit")`, so the exit cleanup runs after the install as on every quit. Never on Windows (the installer would reopen Plyloft). The Mac menu's Check for Updates… and Help items, and the Windows tray's Check for Updates…, emit `menu://help` with what to open. It calls `AgentRegistry::kill_all` first (so live agent runs are marked `killed` before their PTYs are torn down), then `PtyRegistry::kill_all`. Without this, `claude` orphans accumulate.

5. **Window-close hides instead of quits, dock-click reopens.**
   `WindowEvent::CloseRequested` on the main window calls `window.hide()` + `api.prevent_close()`. `RunEvent::Reopen` (macOS) re-shows it. Cmd-Q is the only thing that fully exits.

6. **`dragDropEnabled: false` on the main window. (v0.6.1.)**
   Tauri's default is `true`, which on macOS makes WKWebView intercept drag events at the OS layer **before** they reach the WebView's HTML5 DnD pipeline. With the default, internal JS-initiated drag-and-drop (kanban cards, files-panel-to-tile) is silently broken — drag images follow the cursor but no `dragenter`/`dragover`/`drop` events fire on JS handlers. Trade-off: external Finder→Atelier drops bypass `WindowEvent::DragDrop` too. v0.7.3 restores that path via HTML5 drop listeners on terminal panes (`Terminal.tsx::handleHtmlDrop`) which materialize external file bytes through the `fs_save_dropped_file` IPC since the WebView File API doesn't expose `.path`.

7. **Self-write suppression for `fs_write_text_file` only.**
   The file watcher's `SelfWriteTracker` is registered exclusively when the editor saves through Atelier's IPC. **Agent writes go through `claude` directly**, do not register, and therefore correctly trigger the external-write reload UX in the editor — that's a feature, not a bug.

## Tech stack

| Layer | Choice | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Native shell | Tauri 2 | Real `.app`, small binary, Rust backend, web UI |
| UI | React 19 + Vite + TypeScript | Mature ecosystem for editor/terminal components |
| State | Zustand | One store per concern, no Redux ceremony |
| Editor | CodeMirror 6 | Lighter than Monaco, modular, Compartment-based language/theme swap |
| Terminal frontend | xterm.js + addon-fit | De facto standard, integrates with PTY backend |
| Terminal backend | `portable-pty` (Rust) | Real PTYs from Rust, cross-platform |
| Layout | `react-resizable-panels` | Resizable splits; per-workspace `autoSaveId` for size persistence |
| File watcher | `notify` + `notify-debouncer-full` | 250 ms debounce, cross-platform, OS-level events |
| Search | `ripgrep` subprocess via streamed `--json` | Reuses user's `rg`; no in-process index |
| Cron parsing | `cron` 0.12 + `chrono` 0.4 | Standard 5-field POSIX support |
| Glob matching | `globset` 0.4 | Swarm owned-glob checks (`snapshots.rs`, `swarms.rs`). GH #171 retired the agent file triggers and the `cron` crate with the scheduler. |
| Markdown preview | `marked` 14 + `dompurify` 3 | Sanitized HTML rendering for the editor preview overlay |
| Native notifications | `tauri-plugin-notification` 2 + `@tauri-apps/plugin-notification` 2 | macOS Notification Center for agent completions |
| Process FFI (macOS) | `objc2` + `objc2-foundation` | App Nap disable via `NSProcessInfo` activity token |
| Persistence | JSON files in `~/Library/Application Support/com.mbadv.ply/` | No SQLite needed; first launch migrates the legacy `com.matteo.atelier` namespace |

## File map

### Rust workspace crates (`src-tauri/crates/`)

| Crate | Responsibility |
|---|---|
| `ply-memory` *(agent collaboration Phase 1A)* | Shared accepted-Knowledge core used by the Tauri host and the standalone MCP sidecar. Owns the canonical `Memory` model, markdown/frontmatter codec, scope paths, slug rules, lexical ranking, and stateless layered reader (workspace personal → team shared → global personal). Its production source exposes no filesystem-write or Knowledge-mutation API; encoding is pure, and host writes remain atomic in `src/memory.rs`. |
| `ply-collab` *(agent collaboration Phase 2)* | Shared mutation-free coordination model and reader. Owns schema-versioned session/handoff records, validation, workspace filtering, live-warning derivation, bounded recent-handoff reads, and the canonical `coordination/state.json` path. It contains no persistence mutation or process-launch API; the Tauri host is the sole writer and `ply-mcp` is a stateless reader. |
| `ply-mcp` *(agent collaboration Phases 1–2)* | Minimal provider-neutral newline-JSON stdio MCP server. Launch context fixes app config, workspace id, and project cwd; protocol 2 exposes exactly four read-only tools: `knowledge_search`, `knowledge_get`, `coordination_status`, and `handoff_recent`. None accepts an alternate workspace/path or exposes mutation. `scripts/build-sidecar.sh` creates the ignored target-triple input used by Tauri `externalBin`; the app carries it as an executable sibling of the host, and `--version` exposes protocol compatibility before provider injection. Signed releases explicitly sign and verify the nested executable in both the app and updater archive. |

### Rust backend (`src-tauri/src/`)

| File | Responsibility |
|---|---|
| `main.rs` | Tauri entrypoint. Calls `atelier_lib::run()`. |
| `lib.rs` | Builder, plugin registration, state management (Pty/Watcher/Search/Agent/Workspace/Settings/FilesOrder registries). Window-event override (close → hide for `main` & `settings` windows; native drag-drop → `window://drop` event). `RunEvent` shutdown sequence (agents → ptys → watchers → search). Starts the 30 s runtime tick (`agents::start_runtime_tick`: swarm wall-time, App Nap release) after setup; GH #171 removed the scheduled-agent firing and the trigger listeners. |
| `codex_app.rs` | Owns structured `codex app-server` JSONL subprocesses for interactive Codex tiles. The registry launches with the cached GUI-safe PATH, emits typed protocol messages to React, and terminates every process group on tile close or Cmd-Q. Its separate `CodexApprovalStore` atomically persists exact file roots in `codex/approvals.json`, bound to both workspace id and canonical cwd; root/home/workspace-ancestor grants are rejected and every rule is revocable. Command-rule persistence remains Codex-native through the protocol's proposed exec-policy amendments. Card chat (part 1): `start` is now `start_with(session, cwd, codex, sink)` with a sink that emits the same three events; a card chat passes its own sink and speaks the protocol in Rust. The exit event now waits (at most 2 s) for the stdout reader, so a session's last notification is handed on before its exit; `kill_all` closes the registry (a later `start_with` is refused). |
| `chat.rs` *(card chat, part 1)* | `ChatRegistry` (managed; also installed as the global `end_card_session` reaches): one session at a time per card, at most `ChatLimits::max_live` (3) across cards (the least recently used Ready one is suspended to make room), Ready ones idle for 10 min suspended by a 30 s sweeper; a suspended or closed chat resumes on its next send. Both vendors run in `<config>/claude-sessions/chats/<ws>/<card>` (`chat_dir`, always computed; a plain folder). **Claude**: the interactive TUI in a hidden PTY (`PtyRegistry::spawn_chat`: streamed as `pty://data`, interactive for the member's own keystrokes ("Show terminal", part 2), cached PATH, coordination intent `Intake`/human-attended/read-only, child-session markers removed, refused in a git root under the session root) with `claude_chat_argv` = the read-only profile v3 of the reviews (a test holds it to `read_only_provider::restriction_argv_profile`) + `--add-dir` (attachments, project) + `--append-system-prompt` (the card); a new session gets the first message as positional prompt, a `--resume` (while the transcript exists) none — the message is held and pasted once `composer_idle`. A watcher thread per session reads only what the transcript grew by (`chat_transcript::ClaudeFold`), merges it into `chat.json` (a transcript prompt takes the oldest waiting message, else one with the same words and no source), decides Ready from the transcript, and reads the screen through `chat_screen` (an emulated grid fed every PTY byte) every poll for the session's lifetime: the trust question (`FOLDER_TRUST_REFUSAL`), a blocking screen on every frame (but for words quoted above a composer box) and — until the session writes past its catch-up length — the startup net stop the chat, never answered; another numbered menu sets `attention`; a paste happens only at `chat_screen::composer_idle`, else the send is refused; an open turn at an idle, unchanged composer for 10 s is closed; a switch to another transcript in the project folder (`/clear`, `/resume` in the terminal) is followed and the conversation's session id updated. Everything Claude is sent starts with one space (`composer_text`) and carries no control character but `\n`/`\t`. Stop = Esc, only while a turn is open, debounced. **Codex**: `codex app-server` via `CodexAppRegistry::start_with` (`thread/start`/`thread/resume` with `sandbox: read-only`, `approvalPolicy: never`, vendor-specific `developerInstructions`, `config` turning the member's top-level MCP servers off (`codex_chat_config`; names quoted when not bare keys) and Windows' `windows.sandbox=unelevated`; `turn/start` with `sandboxPolicy: readOnly` and `clientUserMessageId`); server requests refused; Stop before `turn/started` queued. Messages a stopped session took with it are marked not sent. Events `chat://state`, `chat://messages`. `kill_all` (exit cleanup, before the PTYs), `forget_card` (card delete), `forget_workspace` (workspace delete, on a thread), `end_card_session` (from `verify_logs::card_worktree_removed`: every worktree removal). IPC: `chat_open`, `chat_messages`, `chat_send`, `chat_interrupt`, `chat_close`, `chat_attachment_add` (raw body, read in place), `chat_attachment_list`, `chat_attachment_remove`; each refuses a card that doesn't exist. |
| `chat_screen.rs` *(card chat)* | A Claude chat's screen as a terminal shows it: `ChatScreen` feeds every byte the PTY wrote (its capture, drained) into a `vt100` grid of the PTY's size (40 × 120), and the matchers read its rows. `composer_box` (two rule rows with an input line `❯ ` between them — never a numbered `❯ 1.` menu row), `composer_drawn_last`, `composer_idle` (only the footer below the box, no "esc to interrupt" / queued / "ctrl+b" there, no menu or dialog), `menu_waiting`, `blocker` (`claude_session::blocking_screen` on every frame, from the composer box's top when one is drawn), `tail`; `set_size` (from `PtyRegistry::resize` through `spawn_chat`'s resize hook) and `state` (`chat_screen` IPC: "Show terminal"'s first paint — `state_formatted`, the screen and its input modes; `offset`, the bytes fed, counted under the grid's lock, which drains the capture itself so readers never feed it out of order; `rows` × `cols`). The rows read a no-break space as a space, and on the composer's input line (chevron first, no numbered menu row) only plainly drawn characters: Claude Code 2.1.300's suggested prompt — faint, bright black, 256-grey or RGB grey after the chevron — is ghost text, not a draft (`may_hold_a_suggestion`, `without_ghost_text`; Mac walk F1). Tests feed real VT streams (cursor moves, erase-line, a footer-only repaint). |
| `chat.rs` lanes *(Plan with two AIs, part 1)* | Every slot is keyed `ChatKey { workspace, card, lane }`, `Lane::Chat \| Lane::Plan { exchange, role: Proposer\|Critic }` — by role, never vendor, so one vendor can hold both roles (owner Q5). A plan lane runs in `chats/<ws>/<card>/plan-<12 hex>/<role>` (its own Claude project folder and transcripts), its Codex key is `plan:{ws}:{card}:{exchange}:{role}`, its record is one `Conversation` saved through a sink into `plan.json` (never `chat.json`), its events are `plan://messages` (state goes to the driver, which emits `plan://state`), its new Claude session gets no positional prompt (the message is pasted at `composer_idle`), and its next start can carry a re-seed used only if the session comes back fresh. Every status change goes through `Inner::set_status` (and `take_proc`), which tells a plan lane's driver `LaneSignal::TurnEnded` when a turn leaves Working/Starting; `SessionStarted { fresh }` at each start. A running exchange reserves 2 of the 3 slots (`reserve`/`release`): its lanes are never evicted by `make_room` or `sweep_idle`, and the card's Chat send is refused while it runs. `end_card_session`, `forget_card`, `forget_workspace` and `kill_all` cover every lane. `is_plan_pty` makes a plan lane's terminal view-only in the IPC `pty_write`/`pty_resize`/`pty_kill`. `ClaudeFold` records `TurnClose` (Ended, Interrupted, Settled, Stuck, Open). |
| `plan_exchange.rs` *(Plan with two AIs, part 1)* | `PlanExchanges` (managed): the one running exchange's driver thread (P2), reading its two lanes every poll: relays each side's final answer (the text after the turn's last tool call, cut at 16,000) only at a relay point (Claude: the turn closed by the transcript, an interrupt or the stuck closer, or settled with the composer idle and still for `stuck_turn`; the composer idle; the text read the same twice), parses the critic's verdict, runs 2 rounds (3 at most; early stop on `Verdict: ready`), re-seeds a fresh session, sends `retry`/`reseed`/`full` on Retry, pauses on any screen (never answered), Stop, the 20-minute net, an archived card, a session ended; quit saves `paused{quit}` (`interrupt_active`, before `ChatRegistry::kill_all`). The line-up: both vendors ready → the card's Builder vendor proposes, the other critiques; one ready → that vendor twice; none → refused. Accept copies the chat files the plan used into the card's attachments (Q4) and writes the card through `KanbanStore::apply_two_ai_plan` (one write: plan, edited mark, `intake_status: Ready`, attachments, `IntakeRecord` with `twoAis`). Guards for others: `build_refusal` (P1, in `advance_card_to_in_progress_staged`), `intake_refusal` (in `intake_start`). No plan gate (Q1: Free). Test env through the `PlanEnv` trait. |
| `plan_store.rs` *(Plan with two AIs)* | `plan.json` per card beside `chat.json` (schema v1, atomic, serde-default, v0 read as v1, a newer version read and never written, every enum with an `Other`; a stored `running` reads as `paused{quit}`; at most 10 exchanges, the oldest finished go with their `plan-*` folders). |
| `chat_store.rs` *(card chat)* | `chat.json` per card (schema v1, atomic, serde-default everywhere, v0 read as v1, a newer version never written over): attachments, one `Conversation` per vendor (session id/folder, messages, token usage — never a cost). |
| `terminal_sessions.rs`, `session_titles.rs` *(Terminals block, unreleased)* | **The record of a workspace's Claude and Codex tiles** (persistence map: `projects/<ws>/terminal_sessions.json`). `commands::pty_spawn` gives a Claude tile `--session-id <uuid>` unless its args name a conversation (`claude_args_with_session`; `--resume <id>` and Codex's `resume <id>` are taken as the id, both interactive — `pty::refuse_print_mode` still guards argv) and calls `record_start` (a conversation the record had continues its row: `resumedFrom`, its name); `pty.rs`'s exit thread calls `record_end`. One process-wide lock (the live PTY → row map); a CLI's files are read outside it. `refresh_title` (IPC `terminal_tile_title`, polled by `store/tileTitles.ts`): follows a `/clear` to the new conversation by Claude Code's own `sessions/<pid>.json` (read only, undocumented — a hint), takes up the one a `--continue` tile is in, gives a Codex tile its session only when no other waiting tile in that folder could own it (`codex_claim`), and takes the best title `session_titles` reads (Claude's `custom-title`/`ai-title`, Codex's `session_index.jsonl` name, else the first message's words — head and tail only, never kept beyond one ≤ 60-character line; never generated: Hard rule 1). A member's name (`terminal_tile_rename`) wins until "Use the automatic name" (`terminal_tile_auto_name`). **Resume…** (`terminal_conversations`): every conversation of the project folder from the last 28 days — Claude by its folder key (`claude_project_key`, every non-alphanumeric character a `-`) and the transcript's own `cwd`, Codex by `session_meta.cwd` and `source: cli` — card worktrees and intakes (`intake::opens_an_intake`) left out; `terminal_resume_check` before a tile opens (a tile already in it, any workspace; another live Claude in it; its folder or transcript gone). **History › Sessions**: `terminal_sessions_history_all`. 1.179.0: a row records the Claude `account` it ran on (`None` = Main); titles, the session watch, Resume and `terminal_resume_check` read that account's folder (`CliHomes::claude_for`), a conversation of a removed account offers no Resume, and `terminal_conversations` reads Main's folder and every account's, each conversation tagged with its account. A fresh Codex tile whose member picked an older session with Codex's own resume claims that session only when it is the only Codex tile in its folder, the tile itself took an Enter and the session grew after it, never one a closing tile was still writing, an intake session or one another tile runs. |
| `chat_transcript.rs` *(card chat)* | `ClaudeFold`: a Claude TUI transcript folded line by line into chat items, on the top-level `type` only (`api-request-blob` would double messages); unwraps `<pasted_content>`, skips `<synthetic>` resume entries, sidechain/meta/compaction lines; an interrupt ends the turn (no `turn_duration` follows Esc); `<bash-input>` shows as a shell item; images never copied; token usage per message id. |
| `chat_attachments.rs` *(card chat)* | `prepare` (no write: name — last part only, no control character —, kind by extension + file signature (PNG/JPEG/GIF/WebP magic, `%PDF-`, zip for .docx, UTF-8 text), size limits (image 5 MB, PDF 32 MB, docx 20 MB, text 2 MB), the text read) then `commit` (under the card's lock, which also holds the 50-per-card cap: atomic no-clobber storage via `commands::save_dropped_bytes`, the extracted text). `clean_text` (ESC and other controls out). The Claude message (quoted absolute paths + "Read each one"; the extracted text for a PDF over 10 pages and a .docx) and the Codex input (`localImage` only for real images, re-checked at send; documents inline, capped). |
| `chat_extract.rs` *(card chat)* | Plyloft's own text extraction, Mac and Windows. **PDFs in a child process**: this binary with `--ply-extract-pdf <in> <out>` (`dispatch`, from `main.rs` via `chat_extract_dispatch`, before the app starts; both paths must be in one `ply-pdf-*` temp folder, the answer written `create_new`; leftover folders removed at launch, `clean_leftovers`), 20 s wall clock and 1 GiB (Windows: a job with a process memory limit, `platform::cap_memory`; Linux: `RLIMIT_AS`; macOS: the child's `ri_phys_footprint` watched through `proc_pid_rusage`), the child bounding itself too (`RLIMIT_CPU`, an alarm, an exit when its parent is gone), its own process group, killed at quit (`kill_active`); the child reads the page count first (`lopdf`) and extracts the first 2000 pages in one pass (`pdf_extract::output_doc` into `PageText`, an `OutputDev` that records page boundaries and stops at the cap; a panic keeps the pages before it), 2 MB of text. .docx with `zip` + `quick-xml` (`word/document.xml`, 64 MB inflate cap), in process. Plain text (UTF-8, no NUL). |
| `mcp_launch.rs` *(agent collaboration Phase 1B)* | Resolves and caches the validated bundled `ply-mcp` sibling, canonicalizes typed workspace launch context, and injects it ephemerally: Claude receives final inline `--mcp-config` JSON; Codex receives two additive `-c` values before its subcommand. It never writes provider or repository MCP config. |
| `launch_inventory.rs` *(1.175.0)* | What the boards name at launch, read once in `setup` for the three launch sweeps: the cards (`discussion::DiscussionRegistry::new`'s retention), the runs they name (`snapshots::gc_orphans`) and the swarms they link (`swarms::gc_orphan_swarm_dirs`). `LaunchInventory::read` also says whether that is the whole picture. It is not when `workspaces.json` was set aside this launch (`WorkspaceStore::set_aside`), when the project list is empty while board files exist, when a board fails to read, or when a board was set aside or opened from its last saved copy since the app started (`KanbanStore::first_not_whole`, the store's session flag). Round 2: boards the list doesn't name are read too, where they are (`kanban::peek_board`), and what they link is kept; a project list that was refused (`WorkspaceStore::refused`) and a coordination state set aside this launch (`CoordinationRegistry::set_aside`: its retained handoffs protect snapshot folders) are not the whole picture either. Round 4: nor is it while a board file or a coordination state that was set aside is less than 30 days old by the `<ms>` in its name (`recent_set_aside`). Then none of the three prunes (`live_cards()` is `None`, `sweep_snapshots` and `sweep_swarms` return `None`) and one log line says why. |
| `atomic.rs` → `load_store_or_set_broken_aside` *(1.175.0, round 2 R4)* | The one read of the five launch stores whose parse error used to stop `setup`: `codex/approvals.json`, `coordination/state.json`, `coordination/reviewer-capabilities.json`, `reviews/state.json`, `discussions/state.json`. A file that doesn't parse is renamed to `<name>.broken-<ms>` beside itself (never written over, never deleted) and the store opens with its empty state, whose meaning each store states beside its call (no remembered Codex folder: ask again; no capability proof: not eligible until the probe passes; no review record: the next review is a whole one). A newer Plyloft's file, and one that can't be renamed, are still the constructor's error, with the file left as it is. |
| `card_claim.rs` *(1.175.0, moved out of `commands.rs`)* | The claim of a card's issue on a GitHub Issues board: `ClaimOutcome { claimed, holder, card, raced }`, `ClaimMode` (ReadOnly, Claim { confirmed }), `claim_card` (the live read of who holds the issue, `@me` assigned, the re-read, D1's `second_claim::after_claim`, the computer's marker through `device_claim::claim_on_issue`, the author and assignees recorded) and `device_claim_card` (`kanban_card_device_claim`'s work). The commands (`kanban_card_claim`, `kanban_card_claim_state`, `kanban_card_device_claim`) stay in `commands.rs`. |
| `leave_issue.rs` *(1.175.0, D2)* | Leaving a card on a GitHub Issues board takes the member's **own name off its issue**. `leave(store, repo, ws, card)` — called by `kanban_card_put_back` (Move back, Stop during the start) and by `kanban_card_archive` when it isn't the auto-archive — writes `github::issue_remove_assignee_me` (`gh issue edit --remove-assignee @me`, no login parameter) only for a card that `applies` (an issue, no pull request, not shipped) and whose `gh_assignees` hold the current login; the outcome (`NameOff::Off` / `Refused { details }`) is kept per card until the gesture reads it once (`card_name_off_outcome`, wrapper and toasts in `src/lib/leaveCard.ts`, words in `src/lib/cardClaim.ts`). A refusal sets `KanbanCard.unassign_pending`; `retry_pending` runs in `github_sync::reconcile` before the reads and first out of `LABEL_WRITES_PER_SYNC`, reading each card again under `NAME_LOCK` before its write; `taken_up` (Start build's claim gate, `card_claim::claim_card` in Claim mode — each before its read of the issue, on the same lock) clears it; an issue GitHub says is gone (`github::is_issue_not_found`) is dropped. `applies` is false for a card in Done. Also the two places a copy goes outside the card's own gestures: `merged` (from `after_merge::after_merge_and_record`) and `marked_owner` + `copy_deleted` (from `worktree_delete`, off its thread) release this computer's claim marker. |
| `second_claim.rs` *(1.175.0, D1)* | Two people on one card of a GitHub Issues board. **Claim:** `after_claim` — called by `commands::claim_card` after it assigned `@me` to an issue nobody held and re-read it — reads the assignment times (`github::issue_assignments`, GraphQL `timelineItems` ASSIGNED/UNASSIGNED, only when another name is on the issue), decides with the pure `second_to` (earlier time first; same second or no times: the login earlier in the alphabet first), and for the second takes the member's own name off again (`github::issue_remove_assignee_me`; refused → `unassign_pending`), returning `Raced { first, name_off }` in `ClaimOutcome.raced`; the window's claim step asks with `cardClaim.raceSheet` (`kanbanStore.claimStep`, `confirmAfterRace`). **Ship:** `ship_refusal` (before `push_branch_for_pr` in `card_ship`, GitHub Issues boards only) reads `github::issue_delivery` (the issue's state and its closing pull requests with their head branch) and refuses on a teammate's open pull request, or a merged one once the issue is closed (`teammate_work`, pure: a teammate is an author inside the repository — `authorAssociation` OWNER, MEMBER, COLLABORATOR — or anyone whose pull request comes from the card's own branch name); `name_who_pushed` turns a push refused as moved into a sentence naming the teammate who wrote the branch's newest commit (`github::branch_tip_author`), only for a card with no pull request of its own. A timeline that doesn't show this member's own assignment yet is read once more after `TIMELINE_WAIT`. The refusals are sentences ending their first part with `NOTHING_SENT` and the page to open; `src/lib/teammateWork.ts` reads them into the card's error box (`cardPage.cardActionError`), and Now shows no retry (`cardLifecycle.ShipFailure: "teammate"`). |
| `issue_edit.rs` *(1.175.0, D3)* | A card edited here while its issue changed on GitHub: only the part touched is written. `gh_sync_card_edit(store, repo, ws, before, card, body, labels)` (moved out of `commands.rs`; `before: Some` for the member's edit in `kanban_card_patch`, `None` for Plyloft's own whole-body rewrites) → `write_touched`: one `issue_view`, each touched part judged against its baseline (`GhLastRead { title, brief (hash), knowledge, updated_at }` on the card while an edit isn't on GitHub yet, else the card's value before the edit), then `github::issue_edit_title` alone and/or `issue_edit_body` alone (this computer's notes; GitHub's current knowledge, outside text and claim marker — read through `issue_text::parse_body` / `claim_in`, B3's rule); a part both changed becomes an `EditConflict { part: title|brief, theirs, who, at }` in `KanbanCard.edit_conflicts` (`github::issue_last_change` says who and when, asked only then). `push_pending` is the same rule for a Sync's push (`github_sync::push_pending_card`); `kept_on_pull` is what the Sync plan does with an asked part (`ReconcileAction::Patch.edit_conflicts`); `resolve` / the `card_edit_conflict_resolve` command answer a question against the value shown (`Resolved`: kept_github, kept_mine, changed_again, gone); `inbox_detail` words the Inbox row. Window: `src/lib/editConflict.ts` (words, `markedLines`), `src/lib/editConflictActions.ts` (the command and its toasts), `src/components/EditConflictNotice.tsx`; Needs you through `boardSemantics.attentionColumnFor` (`editConflict`) and `cardPresentation` (`editAsks`, `recordInboxGroup`). |
| `fix_round.rs` *(1.175.0, F5)* | Finish and check by itself after a fix round Plyloft sent: the card's two fields (`card_fix_round_check_set` → `KanbanCard::fix_round_check`, `card_fix_round_self_checks_set` → `fix_round_self_checks`, refused on a review card) and `card_fix_round_changed` (a bounded local `git rev-parse HEAD` / `status --porcelain` against the last snapshot; `None` when it can't tell). The rule is the window's: `src/lib/fixRoundCheck.ts` (which rounds, clean finish, cap of two, words), driven by `src/store/fixRoundStore.ts` (rounds noted by `builderStore.noteFixRoundSent` at the findings' delivery, Ask the Builder to fix them, Fix with the Builder; `cancelSelfCheck` for Stop); `CardLive.fixRound` → `cardPresentation`; Now's quiet press in `components/fixRoundNow.ts`, the card's row in `components/FixRoundPick.tsx`, the setting `behavior.checkFixRoundsByItself`. |
| `issue_text.rs` *(1.175.0, B3)* | An issue's text is somebody else's text. **Hidden parts:** `segments(text)` splits a text into what GitHub shows and what sits in an HTML comment (every `<!--` opens one, nothing exempt; an unclosed comment runs to the end; every kind of line break is `\n` first, `plain_lines`; held with `src/lib/issueText.ts` to `src/lib/issueText.fixtures.json`), `hidden_parts`, `with_hidden_shown` (each line `[hidden on GitHub: …]`, `]` escaped, a typed look-alike's bracket escaped). **What an AI gets:** `issue_of(card)` is the one rule for "this card's text is an issue's"; `quoted_for_agent` (the preface, then "Title: …" and the text quoted line by line) under `for_agent(card)` (notes and knowledge — what `kanban::card_dispatch_prompt` returns for an issue card with no `dispatch_prompt`), `for_planner(card)` (`intake.rs`), `brief_for_ai(card)` (`chat::chat_context`, `pr_fix::brief_section`), `problem_for_ai` / the `card_issue_problem` command (a Plan challenge's question, `src/lib/issueProblem.ts` → `DiscussionModal`), `material(card, text)` (`pr_draft::prompt`, marked inside its own fence) and `name_for_ai(card)` ("GitHub issue #N" in Plyloft's own sentences: the Builder's and Build-both preambles, the planner's title line, the Reviewer, the Chat). `issue_text_tests::every_composer_of_a_cards_text_for_an_ai_is_listed` holds the list of readers. **Who wrote it:** `Origin { association, editor }` read by `github::issue_origins` (GraphQL aliases, `ORIGINS_PER_READ` = 100 a read; off in tests unless `set_test_origins_read(true)`) and put on each open `GhIssue` by `github_sync::fill_origins` (a per-run memo keyed by the issue's `updatedAt`); recorded on the card as `gh_author_association` / `gh_last_editor` through the Sync plan's `origin` (Create, Patch, SetAssignees; `KanbanStore::set_gh_origin`). `outside(association)`, `written_outside(author, association, editor)` / `card_written_outside(card)`. **Whose marks count:** `parse_body(body, from_outside)` and `claim_in(body, from_outside)` — used by `reconcile_plan_with` and the write-back, `commands::claim_card`, `device_claim::claim_on_issue(…, from_outside)` and `rebuilt_body`. Window: `src/lib/issueText.ts` (the scanner, the words), `src/components/IssueTextNotes.tsx` (`HiddenParts` under the brief, `withOutsideNote` in Now), the Board card's line in `KanbanBoard.tsx`, Created by in `CardTechnicalDetails.tsx`; icons `outside` and `hiddenText`. |
| `publish_filter.rs` *(1.175.0, lane d X2)* | What an AI wrote, before Plyloft posts it on GitHub. `for_github(text, project)` replaces with `[removed by Plyloft]` what has the shape of a secret (vendor token prefixes, JWTs, the token after `Bearer`, private key blocks, the value of a secret-named `NAME=value`), shows a path inside the card's copy relative to it and any other home path behind `~`, and counts what it removed (`removed_line`). By shape, not by meaning: not a guarantee. Applied in three places: `builder::verdict_comment_body` (the Reviewer's notes in the pull request comment), `builder::line_for_github` (the dismissed-findings line in the pull request body) and `pr_draft::take_for_ship` (the Builder's title and description). The card keeps the Reviewer's notes whole. The filter itself is pure: `for_github(text, &Places { project, session_roots, home })`; `for_github_here` hands in this computer's home folder and Plyloft's session roots (a Reviewer's read tree under one is shown relative to the project). No regex crate: hand-written scans over ASCII starts, linear in the text. |
| `unreadable_notices.rs` *(1.175.0, round 2)* | What Plyloft couldn't read when it opened, said once in the window. `board_unreadable_notice(workspaceId)` hands out `KanbanStore::take_notice` after the Board's first load: the board file was opened from its last saved copy (`restored`) or set aside and started empty, with where the unreadable file is kept (`src/lib/unreadableNotices.ts` words the toast). The load itself succeeds, so the cards are shown with the notice. Round 4: `board_unreadable_notices()` hands out every trouble not said yet at launch (the launch inventory reads every listed board), with the project's name and whether it is a GitHub Issues board, so a restored board is said and read from GitHub without its project being opened. Round 6: it answers `{ notices, awaitingRead }` (the GitHub Issues boards still waiting for their read, also ones said at an earlier launch); a notice is only handed out until the window says it was on screen (`board_unreadable_said`), and `board_unreadable_reveal(workspaceId)` shows the kept file for the notice's Show in Finder (the path is the store's own). `project_list_notice()` hands out `WorkspaceStore::take_launch_notice` once per launch (asked by `App.tsx` beside the welcome toast): `workspaces.json` was set aside (`.broken-<ms>`) or refused and left as it is (a newer Plyloft's, or a value this build can't read: the store then reads as no projects and every save answers the refusal). `project_list_notice_reveal()` shows that file in Finder / File Explorer; the path is the store's own. |
| `instruction_files.rs` *(agent collaboration Phase 1C; GH #101)* | Read-only diagnosis and deterministic repair proposals for the root `AGENTS.md` / `CLAUDE.md` convention. Requires an exact `@AGENTS.md` first non-blank Claude import, guards size/UTF-8/file type/symlinks, fingerprints both reviewed inputs, and atomically writes only fixed workspace-root targets after stale-preview validation. No model or persisted Ply state participates. **GH #101 (BOARDS.md §5):** the same doctor answers the Library's Instructions page. `inspect(root)` runs over **either** a checkout or the integration branch's mirror (which now carries both files), so a shared workspace's page is about the branch and an unshared one's about the checkout, judged by one reader. `procedure` turns every status into one sentence and at most one step — the doctor's own **first** proposal, so no repair can be offered that this module would not make; `manual-only` gets the reason and no step. `plan` is what the flow draws (title, lede, the compared file's before/after, `The N lines move to AGENTS.md, unchanged.`, the `What changes on disk` lines, the reach line, the two fingerprints and the branch blob ids). `file_rows(source, checkout)` are the Value rows, and only a regular file in the member's **own** checkout gets a path for Code. `channel_files(root, id, expected)` is the shared path and **reads only** — the proposal is recomputed from the mirror and refused on a fingerprint mismatch. `symlinked_on_branch` / `manual_on_branch` stop the page offering to create a file the branch keeps as a symlink (the mirror omits it, so the doctor would read it as absent). IPC: `library_instructions_view`, `library_instructions_set_up` (the **one** entry point: it writes the checkout through `apply` only when the workspace has no shared definition, and otherwise queues one change for the channel and touches nothing in the checkout), `personal_instruction_reveal`. The Phase 1C `instruction_files_check` / `instruction_files_apply` commands are gone with the panel. |
| `coordination.rs` *(agent collaboration Phases 2–3)* | Sole coordination-state writer. A cloneable registry wraps one process mutex, resolves canonical Git common-dir/worktree or non-Git cwd identity, atomically persists versioned JSON, reconciles prior-active records to interrupted at startup, prunes bounded retention, records advisory overlaps, and creates snapshot-backed handoffs after agent-run finalization. Phase 3 makes an exclusive writer's `starting` record the atomic same-worktree reservation and returns a typed stable busy envelope without creating a record on conflict. Human-attended and read-only sessions remain advisory. There is no lease, heartbeat, expiry, or terminal resurrection. |
| `shell_env.rs` | One-shot PATH/SHELL/claude-path probe via `bash -lc` at startup. `OnceLock<ShellEnv>`. |
| `claude_auth.rs` *(GH #169)* | Whether Claude Code has a credential on this Mac, for Diagnostics' `claude` row: the login keychain's generic-password item `Claude Code-credentials` (plus, when Ply's process has `$CLAUDE_SECURESTORAGE_CONFIG_DIR` / `$CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, the CLI's suffixed `Claude Code-credentials-<sha256(NFC(dir))[:8]>`), asked for its **attributes only** through Security.framework (`security-framework`, no `kSecReturnData`, authenticated items skipped), and `.credentials.json` in `$CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` / `~/.claude`, `stat`ed and never opened. No process, no network (`the_probe_runs_nothing` holds the file to it). `doctor::claude_auth_status` maps it: either present → signed in; neither → Ply could not see, a **neutral** row (`optionalStep`: counts as ready, `Sign in` only secondary), **never** signed out. Looked at by every read with `withAuth` (Diagnostics and its watch); other reads only reuse an answer under 90 s old. |
| `doctor_reads.rs` *(onboarding OB-2)* | What the dependency doctor reads around the shell probe, handed to the pure `doctor::build_report_with` as `DoctorFacts`: **Codex's sign-in** from file presence only (`auth.json` in the real `CODEX_HOME`, read beside `config.toml`'s `cli_auth_credentials_store`; `keyring`/`auto` or an unreadable file = Ply can't tell, neutral; missing from a file store = not signed in); the **git identity** (`git config --global --get user.name` / `user.email`, read only, only through a git that works); and each tool's **newest release** (D6) from the vendor feeds MEASUREMENTS §4 measured (`downloads.claude.ai/claude-code-releases/<channel>`, `releases.openai.com/codex/channels/latest`, the gh `releases/latest` redirect) with bounded `curl`, fetched only by a read with `withAuth` (Diagnostics), never at launch, cached 6 h (an empty offline answer 10 min). Never runs `claude auth status` or `codex login status` (Setup's AI step only, OB-5); `nothing_here_runs_a_status_command` and `doctor::tests::diagnostics_never_runs_claude_or_codex_status` hold it to that. The report's required rule (§13.1): `allRequiredOk` = git works (resolved **and** answering `--version`; on macOS `xcode-select -p` is asked first and the `/usr/bin/git` shim is never run without the CLT, `shell_env::choose_git`) and Git Bash on Windows **and** `aiReady` (Claude Code or Codex found, usable, not signed out). Every `git` spawned by name goes through `platform::git_command` (`shell_env::git_program`): unchanged wherever the CLT are there or off macOS; on a Mac without them the working git by path with the probed PATH, or a spawn that fails — never the shim (`no_git_is_spawned_by_bare_name_outside_git_command`); `doctor::require("git")` refuses a git that doesn't work. |
| `pty.rs` | `PtyRegistry`: `Arc<Mutex<HashMap<String, PtySession>>>`. `spawn` builds a `CommandBuilder` with cached PATH + HOME + USER + LANG + TERM, opens a PTY pair, spawns the child, drops the slave, spawns a reader thread that emits `pty://data` events, and spawns a wait thread that emits `pty://exit`. `spawn_with_capture` tees PTY output for intake; `spawn_collect` runs bounded synchronous internal collectors — `begin_collect` (spawn, register, writer admission; synchronous) then `CollectRun::wait` (the child's exit, a collected Claude session's end of turn read from its transcript through `SessionWatch`, a blocking screen, or the timeout; an optional observer gets the transcript's lines as they grow). An Agent's Claude oneshot run calls the two halves itself and waits on its own thread; the sessions map is an `Arc` so the wait can end its session there. All three require a typed `SpawnIntent`, obtain the Phase 3 coordination reservation before child creation, and retain an armed cleanup guard until `running + ptyId + processId` is durable. They accept typed optional provider Knowledge context and call the shared Phase 1B injection helper immediately before command construction. Registered sessions remain under `kill_all` ownership on Cmd-Q. **Ending a session (unix, 1.169.0):** each session is ended whole — its leader's group and every process whose session it is (a shell's `cmd &` jobs have groups of their own; macOS reads the process table) — SIGHUP, then SIGTERM, then SIGKILL (`end_sessions`): at the quit all at once within `QUIT_GRACE` (2 s, as `COLLECT_KILL_GRACE`), waited for; at a tile's close within `CLOSE_GRACE` (5 s) on a thread of its own, listed in `ending` so a quit right after waits for it too. A session whose child was reaped (`PtySession.reaped`, set by every exit thread and a collected run's wait) is signalled nothing — its pid may be another process's — nor is Plyloft's own group. `PtySession.process_id` backs `shell_prompt_idle` (#47): a shell tile's prompt is idle when the shell's pid is the terminal's foreground process group (`tcgetpgrp`) *and* canonical input is off (`tcgetattr`), i.e. its line editor is editing the prompt — a builtin `read` owns the foreground in canonical mode and does not qualify. |
| `dictation.rs` *(#47)* | Ply's dictation for Codex and shell tiles (Claude tiles keep `/voice`). Model files in `whisper/models/` (`ggml-small.bin` / `ggml-base.bin`, present only as ≥10 MB ggml files), `curl` download to `<file>.part` with progress and rename-into-place, `whisper-cli -m -f -l -nt -np` on a temp WAV (removed on every path) under a duration-scaled timeout, and the transcript parser (one line, annotations and control characters dropped). Every child runs in its own process group in `JOBS`, keyed for cancel; `ExitRequested` calls `JOBS.shutdown()` (Hard Rule #4). |
| `writer_queue.rs` *(agent collaboration Phase 3)* | Bounded in-process FIFO for standalone unattended-writer intent. Cap 100, caller-key dedupe, cancellation prefixes, one collapsed pump, and `coordination://changed` wakeups. Busy items remain queued; independent keys progress; invalid items are removed with a bounded event. Entries contain no persisted session/run data and are cleared on app exit. |

**v1.16.0 (Cycle E2) frontend update:** `RunHistoryAccordion` in `src/components/KanbanRunHistory.tsx` now renders inline diffs (`RunChangesPanel` + CodeMirror MergeView) on row expand, replacing the v1.5.x toast-summary "View changes" button. Coordinator-mode swarm aggregates lazy-fetch their leg run_ids via `swarmGet` and render one panel per leg with a `Leg k/N · <runId-head>` label. No backend or schema changes; consumes existing `snapshot_get_changes` + `swarm_get` IPCs. Shared between KanbanBoard inline card view and CardDetailPanel.

**v1.20.0 (Cycle E5) workspace dashboard expansion:** `WorkspaceDashboard.tsx` (since deleted with `BrowserPopover.tsx`; the empty state is `CodeOpener.tsx`, telemetry lives in Activity) extended (~140 LOC added) with four new sections aggregating across the workspace:
- Recent ships (top 5) — flatten `card.shipHistory[]` across cards, sort by `shippedAt`.
- Recent verify (top 5) — flatten `card.verifyHistory[]`, sort by `endedAt`.
- Cost rollup (4 weeks) — bucket `agentRun.totalCostUsd` by week-of-startedAt; oneshot-only (REPL has no cost).
- Recent intakes (top 5) — flatten `card.intakeHistory[]`, sort by `startedAt`.

No new IPCs; pure FE aggregation against existing `useAgents` + `useKanban` state. Force-reloads kanban on mount via `loadFromDisk(wsId, /*force*/ true)` so backend-driven mutations (verify/ship) reflect immediately.

**Cycle E close (2026-05-10):** All 6 phases shipped (E1, E2, E.5, E3, E4, E5).

**v1.19.0 (Cycle E4) intake history + versioned dispatchPrompt:**
- `kanban.rs` schema bump v4 → v5: new `IntakeRecord` struct + `KanbanCard.intake_history: Vec<IntakeRecord>` (`#[serde(default)]`). New `KanbanStore::append_intake_history` with FIFO cap `MAX_INTAKE_HISTORY = 10` (evicting deletes the transcript file).
- `pty.rs` adds `spawn_with_capture` — variant of `spawn` that tees PTY bytes into an optional `Arc<Mutex<Vec<u8>>>` while still emitting `pty://data` events.
- `intake.rs` extends `IntakeSession` with `intake_id` (UUID) + `transcript_buf`. New `IntakeStore::transcript_path` / `transcript_relpath` / `finalize_transcript` (atomic, ANSI-stripped) / `read_transcript`. `verify::strip_ansi` promoted to `pub(crate)`.
- `commands::intake_apply` and `intake_cancel` finalize the captured transcript and append an `IntakeRecord` (applied=true / applied=false respectively) before the existing PTY-kill + draft-delete cleanup.
- New IPC `intake_read_transcript(relativePath) -> Result<Option<String>>`. Frontend `IntakeHistorySection` (in `CardDetailPanel`) lazy-loads on row expand.

**v1.18.0 (Cycle E3) project-config starter pack:** `project_config.rs` adds `ProjectConfigTemplate` struct + `builtin_templates()` (5 templates: Rust crate, Tauri app, Python package, Node monorepo, Astro/Vite) + `ProjectConfigStore::apply_template(workspace_id, template_id) -> ProjectConfig`. Two new IPCs: `project_config_list_templates` (pure read) and `project_config_apply_template`. `ProjectConfigPanel.tsx` (since deleted, GH #94; Settings › Repository is `RepositorySettings.tsx`) not-onboarded state surfaced a `<select>` template picker alongside the Detect button. Templates stamp opinionated defaults (target_branch, version_files, install_script, verify_pipeline) — user edits the saved JSON or uses Detect to refine.

**v1.17.0 (Cycle E.5) hardening pass:**
- `snapshots.rs` adds `gc_orphans(live_run_ids) -> (kept, deleted)` for launch-time orphan-dir reaping. Live set is assembled in `lib.rs` setup from `<config_dir>/agents/runs/*.json` filenames + every kanban card's `run_history[].run_id` and `linked_run_id`. Mirrors `worktrees::gc_trash`'s original once-at-launch pattern (gc_trash itself now also runs daily and after Sync).
- `intake.rs` adds `IDLE_INTAKE_TIMEOUT_MS = 15 * 60 * 1000`, `is_idle_for_cancel(now_ms, started_at_ms, is_ready)`, `INTAKE_SWEEPER_TICK_SECS = 60`, and `idle_sweeper_tick(...)`. A new sweeper thread spawned in `lib.rs::run` ticks every 60s, walks `IntakeRegistry::list_active()`, and auto-cancels stale non-Ready intakes.
- `App.tsx` adds `WARMED_CAP = 8` + `lastActiveAtRef` + LRU eviction in the `activeId` warmed-tracker effect. When activating a workspace would push warmed past the cap, the LRU non-active workspace evicts (file watcher stops; per-workspace components unmount, killing their PTYs via `Terminal.tsx`'s mount/unmount lifecycle).
| `app_nap.rs` *(macOS only)* | Acquires an `NSProcessInfo` activity token via `objc2` and holds it for the app's lifetime. Prevents PTYs from stalling when the window is hidden. |
| `log_retention.rs` | 14-day prune of files in `~/Library/Logs/com.mbadv.ply/`. Called once at startup. |
| `workspace_definition.rs` *(GH #79, ADR 0003)* | The shared workspace definition: `.atelier/workspace.json` (identity, board, project config, `schemaVersion`; unknown keys kept). `refresh_definition(app, workspace, moment)` is the one read — `git fetch` of the integration branch, then the file at its tip, never the checkout — run at workspace open and every 10 min while warmed (frontend `DefinitionRefreshScheduler`), at every board Sync (`kanban_github_reconcile`) and before every Start build (`builder_start`); and, with moment `pageRead`, which only reads (`RefreshMoment::only_reads`: no queue drained, no library or Next improvement direction pushed), on entering a Settings › Project page and, on a shared project and a computer that isn't solo, every 60 s while one stays open with the app in front and input in the last 15 minutes (`hooks/useProjectPageReads.ts`; at most once per 30 s of a read that went through, `store/definitionReadStore.ts`; known limits: whether a repeated fetch over an SSH remote can raise a prompt is an open question, and each read holds the workspace lock in `refresh_definition`, so an edit or Sync of that workspace waits behind a slow one; what a read finds can start the Board's existing automatic steps, as any read does), on Team's *Read it again*, and before the press that moves a board. Repo wins: differing local values in `workspaces.json` / `projects/<ws>/config.json` are replaced and reported as `FieldChange`s on `workspace-definition://refreshed` (the frontend toasts author + field) — except the Name of a workspace its person named (`workspaces.json` `nameChosen`, `keeps_own_name`; owner, 2026-10-20), which is never replaced. `workspace_default.json` is converted in the cache when it is the only file. Cache `projects/<ws>/workspace_definition.json` keeps the last good copy, an `unreadable` record (Doctor row) and per-field `pending` local edits, queued for `shared_config` (GH #80) — never written to the checkout. `refresh_definition` (`refresh_and_publish`) reads the branch, drains that queue (`publish_pending`) onto the branch the definition names, then reads again; an edit (`edit_shared`, `app_save`) spawns the same read with moment `publish`. `RepoSource` / `Publisher` traits keep the logic testable without git. |
| `move_shared_setup.rs` *(1.175.0, C5)* | The shared setup's branch gone from GitHub, and a member who can't write. `move_to_renamed(stores, repo, ws, to)` records this computer's "Cards go to" as the new name GitHub gave the gone branch (`DefinitionCache.branch_gone.renamed_to`, read by the refresh through `RepoSource::renamed_to` — `gh api repos/…/branches/<old>`) and the publish takes it there (`workspace_definition::publish_branch`: a pending `integrationBranch` on a branch that is there, while the old one is gone). `use_team_values(stores, ws)`: only where GitHub says this account can't push, empties the queue (Stop sharing's removal kept) and `pending`, returns the labels dropped and this computer's settings pull requests left open (named, never closed). Commands `workspace_move_shared_setup`, `workspace_use_team_values` (both `(async)`). |
| `put_on_github.rs` *(1.175.0, F3)* | *Put it on GitHub…* for a project only on this computer (no `origin`). `preview(tools, dir, branch)` reads only — refuses an `origin`, counts the target branch's commits and the unsaved files, and gives the name (`project_setup::slugify` of the folder's). `create_and_send(tools, dir, login, account, name, branch, token, on_progress, ws)` refuses an `origin` or a name GitHub wouldn't take, checks the branch for a file over 100 MB (`large_file_in`: `rev-list --objects` + `cat-file --batch-check`; nothing created then), runs `gh repo create <login>/<name> --private --source . --remote origin` (bounded, the cached PATH, the account's token), then `git push -u origin refs/heads/<b>:refs/heads/<b>` through `project_setup::network_git` (the account's sign-in answers git) in `worktrees::run_bounded_output_stoppable` — its own `PUSHES` registry, so `stop()` (Stop) kills only these pushes and `kill_active_fetches` kills them at exit. Outcomes `Done`, `NameTaken`, `Stopped`, `NotCreated`, `NotSent` (`large` when GitHub's 100 MB limit refused it). `send_again` pushes again only while `ls-remote --heads origin <b>` is empty. `sent_already(tools, dir, shas)` (R13) says which shipped commits a branch of `origin` contains, from local refs only. Never forced, never `gh --push`. Commands `put_on_github_preview`, `put_on_github`, `put_on_github_send_again` (all `(async)`), `put_on_github_stop`, `put_on_github_sent`; progress on `put-on-github://progress`. Frontend: `src/lib/putOnGithub.ts` (every line of the sheet, card, Shipping and Add a project), `src/lib/putOnGithubIpc.ts`, `src/store/putOnGithubStore.ts` (the sheet asked, the repository made this session until the next Ship it), `src/components/PutOnGithubSheet.tsx` (the sheet, `CardShipNote` under a card's Ship it, `PutOnGithubPress` in Settings › Cards › Shipping); Add a project's tick in `ProjectFromFolder.tsx` (`ProjectInspection.hasOrigin`, `githubName`). |
| `cli_health.rs` *(1.176.0, R1)* | A **read-only** look at one AI CLI (`claude` or `codex`) for the guided Repair. `inspect(tool, inputs, machine)` is pure over `Inputs` (the PATH string, home, `%LOCALAPPDATA%`/`%APPDATA%`, `$CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, `codex_home::real_codex_home`, npm's prefix from `NPM_CONFIG_PREFIX` or `~/.npmrc`, PATHEXT, a `windows` flag) and the `Machine` trait (`look`, `folder`, `run_version`; the real one is `SystemMachine`, tests script a fake): every copy on PATH in order plus the known folders (`~/.local/bin`, `~/.claude/local`, npm's bin, `/opt/homebrew/bin`, `/usr/local/bin`; on Windows Codex's `Programs\OpenAI\Codex\bin`, `%APPDATA%\npm`, WinGet's Links), one copy per real file, each with its real path or broken link, PATH rank, executable, owner (unix uid; Windows unknown), `InstallChannel` (Codex's rules are `doctor::codex_is_npm`/`codex_is_standalone_windows`) and a bounded `--version` (8 s, `worktrees::run_bounded_output`, in parallel; `shell_env::parse_version_output`; failure kind and a trimmed tail). The folders an install or update writes (config home, the official installer's, each copy's npm or Homebrew folder) are tested for owner and writability by creating and removing one uniquely named file — a missing one through its nearest existing parent, never created. A broken link is classified by where its chain stops (`linkEnd`); a `--version` past 8 s is tried once more with 30 s and then is inconclusive, not a failed start; a signal or a malformed/wrong-architecture spawn is `badBinary` (R0 C4). Folders and copies outside the home and config folders are `systemFolder` (never "not yours"). `runsToday` is what a tile runs: the `ShellEnv` snapshot's cached path (`Inputs.cached`), checked against the disk (`cached`, `CachedPathStale`); `shouldRun` (first that starts), `findings` (tagged `Finding`, most severe first) and `healthy` (only copies on PATH, in the install folder or run by a tile count). Command `cli_health(tool)` (async, `spawn_blocking`, the snapshot's PATH and cached path); wrapper and types in `src/lib/cliHealthIpc.ts`. No UI yet. |
| `cli_repair.rs`, `cli_repair_run.rs`, `cli_repair_sessions.rs`, `cli_repair_report.rs` *(1.176.0, R4)* | The guided Repair's engine; its sheet is `components/RepairSheet.tsx`, opened from Settings › Setup and account › Diagnostics (`components/settings/RepairGroup.tsx`), Setup's *Your AI*, a tile that didn't start and the sidebar through `lib/openRepair.ts`. `cli_repair::plan(health, context)` is pure: from one fresh `cli_health` look and a `RepairContext` (sessions in use — the window's tiles plus `cli_repair_sessions`' backend ones: active coordination records of the provider, Codex's App Server sessions, the hidden Codex update — free space on the home's volume, the installer host's reach, `setup_last_run`'s record, the npm-removal tick, the step kinds already run) it returns tagged `Situation`s, `Note`s (what stays), ordered `Step`s (`closeSessions`, `fixOwnership` = one `sudo chown -R <user>` of the tool's own in-home folders typed in the Setup terminal, `fixPermissions` = owner bits added by Rust, `removeBrokenPayload` = Claude's corrupt `versions/<v>`, `removeDanglingLauncher`, `runInstaller` = the official installer, `removeNpmCopy` = `npm uninstall -g --prefix`, opt-in, only when writable, `reread`, `verify`), a `Block` (nothing to repair / healthy enough, offline, disk under 1 GiB, could not repair — with the loop guard: a step kind run already and asked for again stops with the last run's evidence) and the by-hand commands. Never `claude update`/`codex update`; Homebrew/WinGet/bun/pnpm copies only named; a working copy of another channel is not migrated unless it can't update itself or the person ticked the npm removal. `cli_repair_run`: IPC `cli_repair_plan(tool, request)`, `cli_repair_run(tool, stepId, request, cols?, rows?)` (fresh look and plan; only the plan's next step — ids hash what a step acts on — or `verify`; terminal steps through `SetupRunner` as action ids `repair-install.*`, `repair-chown.*`, `repair-npm-remove.*` built in `setup_actions::repair_*_plan` from paths re-checked on the disk (`CheckedPath`: inside the home, no link out of it, never the home, `/`, `~/.claude`, `~/.codex` or a sign-in file); removals are one `remove_file` each, never recursive; a re-probe after anything that changes the tools), `cli_repair_in_use(tool)`, `cli_repair_report(tool, includeTail?)` (plain text, home as `~`, user name removed). Attempts are kept in memory per tool (`request.startOver` forgets them). Review (B1): `chown -R -P --` only for folders that are only the tool's (`own_roots`: `~/.local/share|state/claude`, `~/.cache/claude`, a config folder inside the home named for the tool); the shared install folder, `.claude.json` and the tool's launcher entries get `chown -h --` (`fixSharedOwnership`); never root, never with a home that isn't the user's. B2: `shell_env::choose_copy` passes over a first copy on PATH whose `--version` certainly fails for the first later one that starts (never an Unknown, never under a PATH override: `*_path_skipped` reports it), and `cli_health` applies the same rule, so tiles run the working copy; Setup's Update refuses a copy whose updater can't write its folders (`setup_actions::update_refusal`). A Repair's chown keeps no output (`setup_outcome::keeps_tail`); `tool_run_active(app, tool)` tells R5's updater a Setup run of the tool is under way. Types and wrappers in `src/lib/cliRepairIpc.ts`; Setup's screens leave `repair-*` runs alone (`src/lib/repairAction.ts`). |
| `setup_outcome.rs` *(1.176.0, R2)* | What an install or update run really did, pure. `version_change(before, after)` (versions compared as numbers) judges an update on the version, not on the tool answering afterwards; `classify(tail, exit, timed_out)` reads the output's tail into a `Cause` (permissions with the refused folder, disk full, file in use, network, time limit) for the error block's plain words (`classify_for` per action); `RUN_IDLE_LIMIT` / `RUN_LIMIT` bound a hanging run (`limited(watch)`; a slow download that still prints is not cut); `keeps_tail` / `kept_tail` decide what `setup_last_runs.json` keeps (a Repair's chown keeps none). `setup_run.rs` runs and logs; `setup_last_run.rs` stores. |
| `cli_fresh.rs` *(1.176.0, R3)* | Where `claude` and `codex` are, checked on disk when something starts them. `cli_fresh_check` (a tile's own check, on a blocking worker) and the other starts (`pty_spawn`, `codex_app_start`, `doctor::require`) test the `ShellEnv` snapshot's path through its link; a stale one triggers one shared re-probe (`commands::re_probe_and_forget`) and the window gets `shell-env://changed`. Only a thread that opted in waits for the look; every other start judges the snapshot and leaves the look to a background thread. A tool still missing fails with the typed `UNAVAILABLE_PREFIX` (read by `src/lib/cliUnavailable.ts`). A timeout of `--version` is "unknown", never "not starting". |
| `codex_auto_update.rs` *(1.176.0, R5)* | The automatic Codex update, owned by the backend. A thread looks once a minute and runs the update only when it is due (a day after the last counted attempt), Codex has been idle `IDLE_QUIET` (3 min: no Codex tile, App Server session, headless run, Setup or Repair run) and the channel is one Plyloft may drive unattended (standalone, or npm through its own prefix's npm; Homebrew/winget skipped); opening a tile never starts it. The `Gate` meets every Codex start (PTY coordination reservation, `codex_app::start_with`, the tile's `cli_fresh_check`, which waits up to `START_WAIT`); on Windows the update's process tree is stopped for a start. Bounded by `TIMEOUT` (15 min), killed by `shutdown` at quit (Hard rule 4), judged by `setup_outcome` rules, recorded as the `codex-auto-update` entry of `setup_last_runs.json`, told once per version and cause (`codex-update://done`). The cross-process lock is a file, `plyloft-codex-auto-update.lock`, in the user's temp folder (`lock_path`, removed on drop, stale after `LOCK_STALE`). |
| `codex_update_prompt.rs` *(1.176.1, A)* | Codex's own "Update available" question off in every Codex Plyloft starts in a terminal: `pty::pty_command` (the last step before every PTY child: tiles, `resume`, Builder, intake, reviewer and probe `codex exec`, agents, swarms) puts `-c check_for_update_on_startup=false` first, once (`for_program`). Not the App Server or `codex debug models`, which have no screen to ask on. Plyloft owns Codex updates: Diagnostics' Update row and the automatic update. |
| `program_folders.rs` *(1.176.1)* | Reads a Program folders value (`shell.pathOverride`) as it was typed, never rewriting it: per entry (folder, missing folder, file, `~`/`~name`, variable, relative, empty, a `:` in a Mac name) and for the list (which of claude, codex, git, gh, and on the Mac curl, bash, sh, it finds; which system folders it leaves out; whether it is a fixed copy of the shell's list). Pure over a fake disk, with Mac and Windows forms. IPC `program_folders_check`; used by the Settings row, Diagnostics, the support report and `cli_repair` (which adds a folder or empties the setting, through the setting's own save). |
| `setting_check.rs` *(1.176.1)* | The same reading for the other free-text settings that can stop work: the tile shell, the two typed project commands (a hint for *How to start your app*, a warning for *Before the Builder starts, run*) and the team clone folder. | `cli_seen.rs` *(1.176.1, B and C)* | Says once, as an info toast, when `claude` or `codex` changed version or folder since Plyloft last looked (`cli-seen.json`). Observes every snapshot `shell_env` publishes (`shell_env::set_observer`: launch, Look again, a start's re-read); quiet at the first look, for an unreadable version or a missing tool, and while an `OwnChange` is held for that tool: Setup's or Repair's step on it, the automatic Codex update, the person's Program folders in Settings (both tools) — dropped when that look ends, changed or not, and never counting past 10 min. The state is in memory for the session whatever the write does; a notice is never queued twice in a session, and a failed write is logged once. The reviewer's background re-check is said only right before its real request (`AutoProbeHost::starting` → `probe_starting`), once per tool and version per launch. Pure core: `Core::observe_with`. The window drains `cli_seen_take` at start and on `cli-seen://pending`. |

| `stop_sharing.rs` *(1.175.0, C1)* | *Stop sharing* and a removed shared setup. `file_gone(stores, repo, ws, cache, fetched, now)` — called by the refresh when it finds neither file — marks a setup that was on the branch (`source == Repo`, with its commit) as removed when that branch answers and `RepoSource::carries` says the file isn't at its tip: the definition is dropped from the cache and the queue emptied (so `record_local_edit` and `publish_pending` do nothing), the stores keep their values; writers get `Removed` in the cache (the Board's notice, `AdoptionAction::SetupRemoved`), a read-only member `Sharing::KeptLocal` and a toast (`SharingNote::StoppedBy`), this computer's own merged removal `Sharing::StoppedHere`. `ignores_older_file` keeps the refresh from converting `workspace_default.json` there. `stop(stores, remover, publisher, ws, now)` is the press: `remove_from_branch(git, gh, request, before_push)` commits the removal of `.atelier/workspace.json` alone on origin's tip under the integration lock and pushes `<sha>:<branch>` (never forced, once more on a branch that moved); a refusal walks `shared_config::push_fallback` from `atelier/config-stop-sharing-<ts>` and opens the pull request, kept in the queue (`removalPullRequest`) and asked about by `settle` before each refresh that isn't a page's. `keep_local` records "Keep it on this Mac". Round 2: `older_file` and `named_branch` are asked by the refresh before it takes the older file, or a definition read from a branch other than the one it names — `RepoSource::deletion` (the branch's history deleted the file, or can't be read whole) gives the removed state through `let_go`, on a computer that never read the setup too; `forget` copies the team's suggested models into this computer's blank rows and returns the numbers of its settings pull requests left open; `keep_for_reader` keeps the setup for a member found to have no push. `workspace_definition::publish_pending` passes `create_from` only for a queued creation or a move to a branch whose history never deleted the file. Round 4: `RepoSource::deletion(branch, path, since)` has three answers (`Never`, `Deleted`, `CantSay`; bounded, `-m`, a deletion that is an ancestor of `since` doesn't count, kept per repository/tip/path/since); `CantSay` never changes local state — the copy is used, nothing is created, and an older file found alone is left for the Share question (`DefinitionCache.history_unread`). Commands: `workspace_stop_sharing`, `workspace_keep_setup_local` (both `(async)`: git and `gh`). *Share it again* is `workspace_adoption::share`. Round 5: this computer's settings pull requests are closed by `close_own` through `Publisher::close_after_stop` (still in origin's repository, same head branch, open; its own comment) — at the direct commit, or by `close_deferred` after a read, once `Sharing.removal` reads merged. C2: `workspace_adoption.rs` holds the declined conversion (`ConversionDeclined` in the cache, set by `publish_pending` when the older file's conversion pull request is closed unmerged; `may_convert_by_itself`, `propose_again`, `conversion_notice_seen`; commands `workspace_propose_conversion_again`, `workspace_conversion_notice_seen`). Frontend: `src/lib/sharedSetup.ts` (the row's, sheets', notice's and toasts' words), `src/lib/sharedSetupIpc.ts` (the two calls), `SharedSetupRow` in `RepositorySettings.tsx`, the shared presses in `AdoptionBanner.tsx`. |
| `definition_text.rs` *(1.175.0, C6)* | What a publish of the shared definition writes back that this build's types don't know. `keeping(new, known, written)` takes what the types write now, what they write for the file as it is on the branch, and that file's bytes: a value the publish doesn't change is written as it was (every key a later Plyloft added inside an entry of `versionFiles`, `verifyPipeline`, `reviewChecklist`, `library.plySkills`), an item of a changed list the types write the same as before is the branch's, an edited or added one is the types' own, and a changed object keeps the keys the types don't write. Key order: the types' for what they write, the file's for what is kept. Called only by `workspace_definition::file_text`, which writes the types' own text whenever the kept one wouldn't read as exactly the same definition; `shared_config::prepare` hands it the branch's bytes. A file Plyloft wrote that nothing changes comes back byte for byte. |

A command differs from this Mac's `approvedSetup` → not run, `approvalNeeded`; `card_worktree_setup_retry(allow)` allows then runs; `_skip` is Start anyway. Also the one-time `.env` proposal (`worktree_env_proposal` / `_answer`). |
| `checks_consent.rs` *(1.175.0 B2)* | This computer's OK for the two shared values that run here with nobody watching: `verifyPipeline` ("Checks before shipping") and `installScript` ("After a release, run"). `worktree_setup.json` keeps `approvedVerify` (a hash of the normalized pipeline, a Claude step's arguments included, and a readable copy), `approvedInstall` (the path), who changed the value that waits (`verifyChanged`, `installChanged`) and a release whose install step waits (`installWaiting`). A refresh that is about to write a different value from the branch first writes down what ran until then as the allowed one (`before_repository_apply`, called by `workspace_definition::refresh_with`); a local edit allows itself (`edit_shared`, a page's Publish); nothing recorded means allowed, so an update asks nothing. The verify worker asks before the first step (`verify::run_pipeline` → `hold_for_consent`: the card keeps its last result and gets `KanbanCard.checksHold`); every install launch asks first (`ship::install_held`, `hold_install`); a release's own checks are refused (`release_checks_refusal`). | `skill_asks.rs` *(1.175.0 B1)* | What a skill's **text** asks to be allowed to do (review SEC-1, proof P1): `allowed-tools` naming a command tool (`Bash`, `Bash(…)`, any tool not known to be read-only) or a writing tool (`Write`, `Edit`, `MultiEdit`, `NotebookEdit`), `hooks` in the front matter, and a command run when the skill loads (`` !`…` `` or a fenced ```` ```! ```` block), each with its line. Pure (`analyse(bytes) -> Asks`); a front matter that can't be read, or an `allowed-tools` of an unknown shape, counts as asking. Round 2: it reads the text as the installed CLI does — the front matter ends at the first `---` anywhere (`cli_front_matter`), a YAML that fails is parsed again after the CLI's quoting step (`cli_requote`), markers are looked for in the whole file, and the keys are an allow-list (`HARMLESS_KEYS`; any other key is an ask, `Asks.other`). `skill_scripts::classify` makes a `SKILL.md` that asks a fourth file kind (`asks`) that is in the scripts id by its blob, so the existing holds, sheet, Install and Publish paths ask for it by its exact bytes and sessions keep the approved version (`library_snapshot`). Read-only, bounded `gh api` reads with the cached PATH off the UI thread; nothing is written to GitHub. | `team_leave_seat.rs` *(1.175.0 D5)* | **Leave this team** for a member who holds a seat, and **Give up the seat…** (Settings › Plan) for a seat on a team this computer never joined (review SRV-2). One action, in order: `billing_client::seat_leave` (`DELETE /team/seat`, the session's own seat; `200 {left}` in both cases) and only then `commands::leave_team` (the computer stops following the team). A refusal or no answer changes nothing here; a server older than 1.175 answers `404`, which is a failure (`unsupported`), never "already free".
