Commands `team_leave_seat`, `seat_give_up`; the owner, a member without a seat and a team without seats keep `commands::team_leave`. |
| `worktree_ports.rs` | A port block per card worktree: 10 ports from `PLYLOFT_PORT_BASE` in 41000–48999 (blocks of ten, above the usual dev-server defaults, below macOS's ephemeral 49152+). `KanbanStore::ensure_port_base` hands one out when a card's worktree is recorded (`advance_card_to_in_progress_staged`), before its setup (`finish_card_setup`, also lane `b`), or lazily on the first spawn in an older worktree; unique among the cards (and undecided Build-with-both lanes `b`) of every workspace that have a worktree, under the one cache lock, other boards read from disk without caching; a free block with a port in use is skipped (at most 32) — Unix: bind on 127.0.0.1 fails or a connect to 127.0.0.1 / [::1] succeeds; Windows: an exclusive bind (`SO_EXCLUSIVEADDRUSE`, no listen) of 127.0.0.1, 0.0.0.0, [::1] or [::] fails — probed per port in parallel with no lock held, then re-checked under the lock before the write (`ensure_port_base_probed`, 4 attempts, then unprobed); picking lane `b` moves its block to the card (`build_both::apply_pick`); the card keeps its number after the worktree goes (a removed worktree frees it without a write) and gets it back unless another card took it. Persisted as `KanbanCard.portBase` / `BuildLane.portBase` (serde default, not written when absent). Exposed as `PLYLOFT_PORT_BASE` + `PLYLOFT_PORT_COUNT=10` through `env_for_cwd(cwd)` (a resolver over `KanbanStore::port_base_for_cwd` installed at startup: one read of the boards answers a worktree whose block nobody else holds; one handed out then emits `kanban://changed`): `PtyRegistry::{spawn, spawn_with_capture, spawn_collect}` pass it to `session_env` as caller variables (tiles, Builders, reviewers, discussions, verify slash steps); the worktree setup command (`prepare_worktree`'s `extra_env`), Verify's shell steps (`VerifyJob.build_env`) and the Codex app-server get it too. Nothing outside a card worktree does. |
| `shipped_changes.rs` *(QoL 1.168 block 5.3, C8)* | What a shipped card shipped, once its copy is in the Trash. IPC `card_shipped_changes(workspaceId, cardId)`: the card's landing (`landing_commit`: its earliest `shipHistory` entry, else `commitSha` — a later release overwrites `commitSha`), read-only in the workspace folder — `git diff --numstat -z -M <sha>^1 <sha>` (a merged pull request whose head is here: `merge-base(head, <sha>^1)..head`, so a rebase merge reads whole; a root commit: `git show --numstat`), the subject, at most `MAX_FILES` (200) files with their line counts, and the total. A commit other cards shipped too (`cards_sharing` on the Board's records, or its `Card:` trailers) lists nothing and returns `sharedWith: N`. Only a hex commit id is read; one not in this computer's history says so in words. Nothing is fetched or written. Drawn by `ShippedResult.tsx` in the card's The result. Tests in the module. |
| `shared_config.rs` *(GH #80, ADR 0003)* | The one write channel for the shared definition. `publish(git, gh, request)`: `git fetch origin <integration>`, re-apply the queued fields onto the tip field by field (a field changed upstream since its `base` is held for the person's choice — `setting_conflict.rs` — unless they chose *Use this Mac's* over that value, which goes in with its author named in the message: `config: <field> — <old> → <new> (overrides X's value from 2 min ago)`; every commit carries a `Plyloft-Device: <computer>` trailer), commit with plumbing (scratch `GIT_INDEX_FILE`, `hash-object`, `write-tree`, `commit-tree` — the checkout, its index and branch are never touched; `check_path` refuses anything outside `.atelier/`), `git push origin <sha>:<branch>` (bounded, registered with the fetch registry for exit cleanup). A non-fast-forward retries once; a remote refusal or second non-fast-forward pushes `atelier/config-<field>-<ts>` (named once, saved in the queue first; a retry links an existing PR from it; never forced — `push_fallback`, shared with `shared_library_sync`, leaves a branch that ends in another commit as it is, closes its PR and takes the next `-2`, `-3` name; the replacement of a superseded PR takes a fresh name and its body says `Replaces #N`, with the body on stdin; 1.175.0) and opens `Settings: <field>` with `gh --repo <origin's repository>` (bounded, exit-registered). Queue `projects/<ws>/shared_config_queue.json` (atomic). `integration_lock(repo, branch)`, keyed by git's common directory, serializes publishes with Ship's merges (`land_card_local`, `ship_card`, `ship_batch`). |
| `setting_conflict.rs` *(1.173.0, C3)* | A shared setting both sides changed: `shared_config::prepare` holds a queued field whose branch value changed since its `base` (`QueuedField.conflict`: their value, author, the computer from the commit's `Plyloft-Device` trailer, when) and publishes the rest; this module turns it into the question's view (`conflicts_of` → `DefinitionView.conflicts`, only the difference: lists item by item, areas by id) and the Inbox row (`shared_setting_conflict`, `inbox_title`/`inbox_id`). Answered by `workspace_definition::resolve_conflict` (`workspace_definition_resolve_conflict`): *Keep theirs* drops the pending edit, *Use this Mac's* sets `QueuedField.use_mine` so the next publish goes over exactly that value. Frontend: `lib/settingConflict.ts`, `store/settingConflictStore.ts`, `SettingConflictHost`. |
| `workspace_adoption.rs` *(GH #81, ADR 0003)* | Adoption of the shared definition. `decide(facts)` → `AdoptionStatus { githubRepo, hasDefinition, boardRepo, repoBoard, action }`: `ask` (github.com `origin`, no definition on the default/target branch or any origin branch, not declined in `settings.sharing.notOnThisMac`), `convertLegacy` (a converted `workspace_default.json` not published), `switchBoard` (the definition's board is GitHub Issues and this Mac's is still Local: switched here, nothing to publish; R14, 1.169.1: a definition that says Local is never switched or offered and puts up no banner on any plan — `repoBoard: "local"` lets Settings › Board move it, on a press confirmed with "changes it for everyone who opens this repository", published as one config change), `migrateCards` (the board is GitHub Issues here and cards made while it was local have no issue), `boardHeld` (the licence gate refuses GitHub Issues here; `Stores::github_board_allowed` keeps a Local board unapplied and unrecorded), `none`. Automatic steps are offered only where `repo_access` doesn't say this account can't push. `share` seeds (`seed_definition`: this Mac's name, colour and project config, integration branch absent unless the local config names another, version files from the config or the root manifests on the branch), caches it `source: seeded` and queues `createDefinition`; `shared_config::prepare` adds the `.gitignore` fix (`fix_gitignore`: `.atelier/` → `.atelier/*` + exceptions) to the creation commit. Frontend: `AdoptionBanner` on the board (1.174.0 A4: `useAdoptionNotice`, a note in `BoardNotices`); local cards → issues through `kanban_github_push_local` (brief in the notes markers, column as status label), serialized with reconcile by `github_sync`'s per-workspace board-pass lock. |
| `shared_library_sync.rs` *(GH #82, ADR 0003; GH #101)* | Shared Knowledge, project skills and the root instruction files on the `shared_config` channel. A Shared · Project note (`.atelier/library/knowledge/<slug>.md`) or a project skill (`.claude/skills/<name>/`) is a `LibraryChange` (whole item, or `None` to remove) queued in `projects/<ws>/shared_library_queue.json` and laid over the mirror at once (`enqueue`). `sync` (run by `refresh_definition` after the definition's drain, under the workspace lock) publishes each change with `publish_change` — fetch, `commit_tree_changes` onto the tip (writes, removals, executable bits), push, one non-fast-forward retry, PR `Knowledge: <title>` / `Skill: <name>` on refusal — messages `knowledge: <title>` / `skill: <name>` (`(removed)`; for a note, `(overrides X's version from …)` when its object id moved since the edit's `base`); then rebuilds `projects/<ws>/shared_library/` (the tip's two directories plus the queue, `manifest.json` with item object ids and the checkout's cwd) and reports items changed upstream (`LibraryUpdate`, author from `git log`). Readers of the Project layer use the mirror when it has a manifest (`ply_memory::project_layer_root[_for_cwd]`: `MemoryStore::list_shared`, `AcceptedKnowledgeReader` in the sidecar); at each Claude launch `session_plugin_dir` builds plugin `project` — the branch skills the session's own directory (checkout or card worktree) lacks or has differently — into `projects/<ws>/shared_skills_session_plugins/<content digest>/`, never changed or removed while it could be in use (pruned after 30 days without a launch), and passes it as a `--plugin-dir` (`mcp_launch`). **GH #156:** the Codex half — `branch_codex_skills::session_marketplace` builds the mirror's loadable `.agents/skills` the session's directory lacks into `projects/<ws>/shared_codex_skills_session_plugins/<digest>/` as marketplace `ply-project` (plugin `project`, so Codex lists `project:<name>`), and `mcp_launch` folds it into the same `CODEX_HOME` overlay as the team's and `ply-app` (`codex_home::build_all`: a collision costs only that marketplace); `LibraryState.branchCodex` (`branch_codex_skills::delivery`, a `codex_home::preflight` that builds nothing) lights the Library's Codex chip. Read-only collaborators: `DefinitionCache.writeAccess` (`gh api repos/<o>/<r>` `permissions.push` at every refresh, or a refusal no PR could carry) refuses shared-field edits (put back), the editor, and Shared · Project writes (`app_library_change`). IPC: `knowledge_project_state/save/delete`, `project_skill_publish/unpublish`; `knowledge_promote_project`, `knowledge_demote_project` and a Project → Team promote go through the channel in a shared workspace. **GH #101 (BOARDS.md §5):** `ItemKind::Instructions` carries the root `AGENTS.md` / `CLAUDE.md` — same queue, same mirror, same pull-request fallback, message `instructions: <what changed>`, and one change may write **both** files so the `Set up` flow is one commit. They are **never overridden**: `LibraryChange.bases` records each written file's blob id on the branch as the flow was read, `prepare` compares it against the freshly fetched tip, and any difference sends the same commit into a pull request `Instructions: <file>` instead — `git.push(…, branch)` is never reached (a source-order test keeps the check above it). A change that writes one file never removes the other, `check_item_name` admits only the two names, and `shared_config::check_path` admits them only as the whole repository-relative path. `items_at` records each file as its own item (`instructions/<file>`), `build_mirror` writes both from the tip and refuses a symlinked one as it refuses a symlinked skill, and `sync` records every path an instructions publish wrote so this Mac's own commit is not announced back to it. **Skills group B §2:** a skill is never overridden either. `LibraryChange.bases` holds, per root the skill occupies, the tree id the change began from — for `Update on <branch>` `checkout_skill_bases` (this Mac's own last publish of the root from this checkout while the tip still holds it untouched and `HEAD` descends from that checkout, else the version a refresh last saw the checkout's copy equal to (`observe_in_sync`, same checks), else the tree at `merge-base(HEAD, origin/<branch>)`, else the mirror's); for `Remove` and `Align` the skill id the row showed (`MirrorSkill.id`, sent back as `shown`); otherwise the mirror's manifest at `enqueue`. `prepare` compares every root the change **claims** against the tip (`moved_skill_roots`: whole tree, so an edited, added or removed file counts) and on any difference opens the same commit as a pull request `Skill: <name>` (marker `SKILL_NEVER_OVERRIDE_MARKER`, `blocked` when no PR is possible). A removal, an `Align` (the one copy with `replace`) and an approved proposal take the same rule; a plain `Make available to both` copy cannot override (source read on the tip, a filled destination skipped). Older queued skill changes (no `bases`) are judged by `base`. An Update that edited one copy of a skill whose two copies were one tree at base publishes the edited copy to both roots (`follow_the_edited_copy`, `SkillPublish.follows`); both edited differently is refused. |
| `library_snapshot.rs` *(Skills hub H1, `docs/plans/SKILLS_HUB.md` §7.2, R13)* | **What a session is given from a skills library.** At every launch (`KnowledgeMcpContext::new`) the team library's — and, with H10, My library's — accepted plugins are delivered as content-named snapshots built from the git objects of the clone's `HEAD` (`git rev-parse`, `git ls-tree -r -z -l`, one `git cat-file --batch`, bounded and in the exit registry), never its working tree and with no `git_lock`: one per Claude plugin (`--plugin-dir`), one per Codex marketplace (the `CODEX_HOME` overlay's source), named by tree id under `skills_hub/sessions/` (persistence map). Built as `.build-<uuid>` and renamed in, never changed, stamped at most hourly, pruned by `shared_library_sync::prune_session_plugins` (30 days unstamped, a 512 MB cap per library that never removes a build stamped within 24 h). A symbolic link or an unsafe path refuses its plugin; a plugin over 64 MB is refused; a skill whose path would pass 240 characters is left out on Windows. H6a's held skills are built from their approved tree. The per-session record (PTY id → commit and delivered skills, in memory) feeds `session_skill_drift`, the *Skill updated after this session started* line (the UI asks every 7 s). A snapshot that can't be built costs that session the library, never the launch. Card chat and `codex app-server` get no library plugins. |

A pull's range is read per skill for the toast (`skills-hub://pulled`, one per pull, never for the member's own changes). Failures are counted; GitHub refusing the repository three times over at least 30 minutes asks (Inbox row and section notice) before `skills_hub_library_switch` switches it off on this Mac; the manifest no longer listing the account (`Membership::NotListed`) is leaving: the clone goes to the Trash / Recycle Bin. `skills_hub::config_dir` is the one directory every Skills hub store resolves through. `skills_hub_state::SkillsHubStore` owns `skills_hub/state.json` (one mutex, write-through, atomic, unknown keys kept at every level, an unreadable file set aside) — approvals, pull records, proposals, the twin ledger, local links and copy origins (persistence map). `stop()` in `run_exit_cleanup` before the fetch registry is killed. | `skill_upstream.rs`, `skill_bases.rs`, `skills_hub_decisions.rs` (a child module of `commands`), `team_proposal.rs` *(Skills hub H4, R3, R7, R8)* | **The decisions about a linked copy.** `skill_links::link_state` is H4's state machine: here = upstream now → *up to date*; origin unreadable → *modified here* or *not available*; read and gone → *removed*; moved → *renamed*; modified + moved → *both changed*. `skill_upstream::read(clone, origin, removal_trusted)` reads git objects of the clone's `HEAD`; *Removed* / *Renamed* only when the caller trusts the clone (`removal_trusted`: no rebase/merge under way, not ahead and behind; a library switched off or left has no clone — `team_origin_clone`) and the link's commit is an ancestor of `HEAD` (a clone behind the link is *not available*; a link commit the clone never saw reads content but never *Removed*); a rename is the same tree id under a new name, or `diff --find-renames=90%` over `<link commit>..HEAD` moving the skill's files to one new directory, with the other half (`library/claude/<p>/skills/<n>` ↔ `library/codex/plugins/<p>/skills/<n>`) agreeing. **Decision cards** (`SkillVerb::{KeepMine, Detach, Remove, Rename, Relink}`, `SkillCard.renamed_to`, `link_before`/`link_after` blob ids): one plumbing commit on the target tip (`skill_card::decision_changes`), Ship's gate `decision_only` (only the record written / removed, only the copies removed, or the copies and records moved), `decision_preflight` (target holds the result → up to date; copies or record changed since → PR; never a refresh). Commands (`commands::skills_hub_decisions`): `skills_hub_compare3` (Pro: base from the base store, else the clone at the link's commit; per file `both`/`here`/`upstream`/`same`, 2-way `differs` without a base), `skills_hub_keep_mine` (Pro; only when *both changed*), `skills_hub_update { takeUpstream }` (Take upstream), `skills_hub_propose_upstream` (Pro + team gate + `promote_authorization`), `skills_hub_removed_choice` / `skills_hub_renamed_choice` (free). `team_proposal`: a team-library change as a pull request — committed in a temporary detached worktree of the clone at the default branch's tip (or this Mac's open proposal branch), pushed only to `plyloft/skill-<name>-<yyyymmdd-hhmm>`, `gh pr create --repo <team>`; the clone's checkout is never touched. **Promote (R7)**: `promote_into_library` compares the skill's two halves at the clone's `HEAD` before and after the write's own pull (`half_ids`); moved → nothing pushed, a pull request instead ("<author> changed it meanwhile — opened pull request #n instead."); a push refused by branch protection → a pull request; refused as read-only → R7's sentence. A push GitHub explicitly refused takes its own commit back in `publish_library_paths`; any other failed push may have landed (`team::push_may_have_landed`: stopped at its bound, killed at quit, a connection lost, anything unrecognised) and keeps it, and `drop_refused_commit` drops one that such a push left. Inbox: `InboxEventKind::SkillDecision` (`skill-decision:<ws>:<skill>:<both|removed|renamed>:<about>`), one per skill per workspace, not while a skill card carries it or this Mac's proposal of its content stands. |

Records: the link record's `base` (both copies), this Mac's `twinLedger` (`skills_hub_state::record_aligned`, written by `skills_hub_links` whenever it reads a checkout's copies aligned), and on a branch the copies at the card's base when they were aligned there (`records_at`). **Finish and check** (`builder::record_build_done`, after the worktree's edits are committed and before the snapshot is read): `align_card_branch` aligns every skill the card changed since its base whose copies follow one another — one plumbing commit on the card branch, fast-forwarded in — so the Challenger, Verify and Ship read one tree; `KanbanCard.twins_aligned` names them and the Checks section says *"Codex's copy of x updated to match Claude's."* (never on a pull request review card). **Align by card** (`skills_hub_align`, free; a workspace without a shared channel — a shared one aligns on its branch, GH #105): `SkillVerb::Align` + `SkillCard.align_winner`, one commit on the target tip replacing the losing copy file for file (`align_changes`: the winner's files written with their mode, files it lacks removed, the loser's own vendor-only files kept); with no choice the rule's winner, both changed refused with the question. Ship's gate `align_only` (only plain files in the losing copy's folder), preflight `align_preflight` (target already the card's copies → up to date; either copy changed since → pull request), never a refresh. `skills_hub_links` → `twins` (`TwinRow`: state, line, legacy `.codex/skills` disagreeing, committed). Promote reads the branch's copy that changed alone (`project_skills::branch_twins`); Keep mine and Propose wait for an Align (`ALIGN_FIRST_MARKER`), Propose and the 3-way Compare read the copy that changed alone. `.codex/skills` is never written. |

Ship: `skill_only` gate, preflight up to date by its copies alone, never a refresh (`card_behind`, `skills_hub_card_refresh`). A file without a choice aborts exactly as before (`Collision`, every file named). `team_library_reveal_aside` shows the folder. |
| `skills_index.rs`, `skills_index_hash.rs` (its child module `hash`) *(Skills hub H7, R4, §5.7, §7.3)* | **The index behind All skills.** Facts only: per workspace (`WorkspaceStore` list, warm or cold) one row per skill of `.claude/skills` · `.agents/skills` · `.codex/skills` (depth 2, never a whole checkout; a shared workspace reads, as its Library does, the checkout's skills and links plus the skills only its mirror build `shared_library_sync::mirror_build` has when its manifest is there, `source: branch`; with no mirror the checkout, `source: checkoutShared`), the team clone (`team_skills::read_library`; ids from one `git ls-tree -r -d HEAD -- library`; `needs_ok` from `skill_scripts::needs_ok`), this Mac (`personal_skills`) and Plyloft (`ply_skills::read_bundle`, `off_here`). Each row: CLIs, each twin's content id, `twins_differ` (Codex's `VENDOR_ONLY` file left out), `legacy_codex`, `changed_at`, `too_many_files`, the link (`skill_links::read_checkout` of the checkout) with its state. **Ids in process** (`hash`): SHA-1, git's blob and tree formats and order, each file stored as `git hash-object --path` would store it (`hash::Eol` from `eol_for`: `autocrlf` from the repository's config, else `~/.gitconfig` / the XDG file, else the platform default; the `text`/`eol`/`crlf`/`binary` attributes of the global file, `.gitattributes` from the repository's top down to the file and `info/attributes`; git's exact binary guess for `auto`; `filter=`/`ident`/`working-tree-encoding` → no id; where `core.fileMode` is false — always on Windows — `HEAD`'s modes from one lazy `git ls-tree -r -z` per workspace, only on a cache miss), files over 1 MB streamed; a symlink/junction, a noisy directory (`watcher::is_noisy_dir_name`), over 500 files (or 2,000 entries) or a cloud-only file (iCloud dataless, Windows offline) → no id. `DirCache` reuses a directory's id while its files' (path, size, mtime, mode) fingerprint holds. **Links:** `quick_up_to_date` (recorded twins = base in process and the library still at the version, or = the library's id now) → *up to date* without git; otherwise `LinkJudge::full_state` = the Library's own judgement (`skill_card::checkout_twin_ids` + `skills_hub_decisions::link_upstream` + `link_state`). **Running:** `refresh(app, only)` on its own thread, one at a time (a second ask merged and run after), `read_workspaces` on ≤ 2 workers yielding between workspaces; streams `skills-hub://index` (`started` · `team` · `thisMac` · `plyloft` · `workspace` · `done`), keeps the in-memory copy current per workspace, writes `skills_hub/index.json` atomically at the end and prunes directory facts under what it re-read (a whole refresh keeps only what it read and a missing folder's, so a removed workspace's facts leave the file). A missing folder keeps its last rows (`state: missing`). Commands: `skills_hub_index(refresh?)` → the cached `IndexView` at once (first call reads the file), `skills_hub_index_refresh(workspaceIds?, libraries?)` (`Ask`: workspaces, or none with the libraries alone — an OK given to a team skill). The cached view leaves out a workspace no longer listed and shows each one's name as it is now (`list_as_now`). `stop()` in `run_exit_cleanup`. Frontend: `src/lib/allSkills.ts` (pure: rows, Used in by link then name, counted by repository; filters; groups capped at 50 with Show all; the virtual window; the muted count), `src/store/allSkillsStore.ts` (the stream; derived values computed once per index change; refreshes on page open, skill-root `fs://changed` of warmed workspaces, the editor's own saves (`noteFileSaved`, the watcher drops self-writes) and definition refreshes whose sync moved a skill (`definitionMovedSkills`) debounced 2 s, a team pull whose record moved (`libraryKey`: commit, clone, switch, access — not every 10-minute background pull), the libraries alone on `skills-hub://decision` not echoing a pull, hourly while visible; the cache only, 4 s after launch), `src/components/allSkills/AllSkillsPage.tsx` (the page and a skill's hub page), `uiStore.allSkillsOpen` (closed by `setCenterMode` and a workspace switch) and `.center-stage--app-page` in `NewWorkspaceShell` (every host hidden by visibility, none unmounted). |
| `my_library.rs` *(Skills hub H10, R6, §5.3, §5.8, §9)* | **My library**, the member's own private GitHub repository of skills, in the team library's layout (`library/claude/mine/` a Claude plugin `mine`; `library/codex/` the Codex marketplace `plyloft-mine` with the plugin `mine`; a README; never `library/knowledge`, §14 Q22). Per Mac `settings.library.myLibrary { repo, account }` (`SettingsStore::set_my_library`, backend-owned); the clone `<config>/my-library/<owner>__<name>/`. Every `gh` and network `git` runs in the scope of `account`, the login that owns the repository (`scope` → `github_account::enter_for_team_with`, My library's own words); network git is `worktrees::run_bounded_network_git_in` in the exit registry, `core.longpaths`. **GitHub behind a seam** (`Hub`: `login`, `scopes`, `repo` → Missing/Empty/Library/NotLibrary, `create`, `url`; `RealHub` = `gh repo view` / `gh api repos/<slug>/contents/library` / `gh repo create --private`). `create_with`: name check, `repo` scope (missing → `GRANT_MARKER`, *Grant it* = Setup's `refresh.gh`; unknown scopes → try, a 403 maps to the same words), refuse a taken name (`ALREADY_A_LIBRARY_MARKER` when it is a My library — *Use it on this Mac*), seed in a scratch `.new-<uuid>` (git init, layout, chosen skills of this Mac copied into both halves), `gh repo create`, `git push -u origin main`, rename in. `use_with` clones aside and renames in (offline: `OFFLINE_WORDS`, nothing left). `pull_with` (`team::library_pull_in`) → Pulled/Offline/Held/Failed, and **Gone** when the fetch says *not found* and `gh repo view` confirms; `record_pull` folds it into `state.json` → `libraries["library:<owner>/<name>"]` (`goneAt`). `recreate_with` re-creates the private repository and pushes this Mac's clone. **Publish** (`publish_with`, Promote → My library): pull first, the skill's id at `HEAD` vs this Mac's base (`state.json` → `myLibraryBases["library:<repo>/<skill>"]`, written at every publish and *Keep the other's*); moved → `Published::Compare` (`MyCompare`: other id/author/time, 2-way without a base, `skills_hub_decisions::compare3_files`), nothing written; an answer (`Choice::KeepThis` / `KeepOther`) counts only with the other id the compare showed (`expected`); else both halves written, `git add/commit -- <paths>`, then `push_branch`: `git push origin HEAD:refs/heads/<branch>` (an explicit refspec without `+`, so a member's own `remote.origin.push` or `push.default` never widens or forces it; a refused push is undone with `reset --hard` to the commit before and retried once from GitHub's tip on a race). This is the scoped push exception (like `card_ship`): only the member's own My library — `use_with` refuses a repository not under the account's login or the team library (`ownership_problem`), and `check_origin` refuses a push unless the clone's fetch *and* push URL name `settings.library.myLibrary.repo` (`names_repo`) — and only the skill they pressed for (or, *Create it again*, the clone back into the repository it was). A pull or publish whose account can't sign in is `Pulled::SignIn` (`lastError: "signIn"`), never *gone*; `RealHub::repo` returns the scope's own words. **Delivery** (`KnowledgeMcpContext::with_my_library`): `library_snapshot::deliver` on the clone (H1 snapshots, H6a holds — `skill_scripts::library_id` is `library:<owner>/<name>` for a clone under `my-library/`), one more `--plugin-dir` for Claude, the `plyloft-mine` marketplace in the one `CODEX_HOME` overlay, recorded for drift; beside a team plugin `mine` delivered as `mine-<login>` from a renamed, content-named copy of the snapshot (`renamed_snapshot`). Not plan-gated (a lapsed Pro keeps the last copy). **Pulled** by the scheduler after the team's (Startup/Timer/Wake; background only on Pro). **Linked copies** (R6 AC): `skills_hub_install { library: "mine" }` writes a card whose link origin is `{ kind: library, source: <owner/name>, plugin: mine }` (`TeamSkillSource.kind`, `my_library_for_card`); `team_origin_clone` resolves `library` to this Mac's clone when it uses that repository, else *not available on this Mac*. IPC: `my_library_status`, `my_library_find(account)`, `my_library_create(name, account, seed)`, `my_library_use(repo, account)`, `my_library_forget`, `my_library_recreate`, `my_library_pull_now`, `my_library_publish(source, choice?, expected?)` (all but status/forget Pro-gated `ProFeature::MyLibrary`); `skills_hub_scripts_review` / `skills_hub_approve` / `skills_hub_install_review` take `library: "mine"`. Index: `HubIndex.myLibrary` (event `myLibrary`). Inbox: `skill_needs_ok` rows for My library (`skill:library:…`) open its All skills page. Frontend: `src/lib/myLibrary.ts` (words, markers), `src/store/myLibraryStore.ts`, `src/components/allSkills/MyLibrary.tsx` (the section, create/use sheets, the compare, Add to My library, Install in, Review). |

PPVP's owner-check step runs `ply_skills::tests::every_change_to_a_skill_is_listed_with_its_version`: the newest entry's `contentId` is the bundle's git tree id, versions descend and never pass the next minor after the app's (`release.sh` refuses one past the version it releases), and each history copy hashes to its entry. **The switch** (`ply_skill_switch::Inputs::decide`): the workspace's explicit `on`/`off` (`projects/<ws>/ply_skills.json`, the store a definition refresh keeps in step with `.atelier/workspace.json` → `library.plySkills { off, on }`, field `library` in `workspace_definition::FIELDS`; compared by the Plyloft choices only, `overlay` keeps `library`'s other keys) → a fork in the session's directory (`.atelier/skill-links/<name>.json` with `origin.kind = plyloft` beside its copy, `forks_here`) → the team default (`team.json` → `library.plySkillsOff`, read from `TeamManifest::extra`; applies only where the team library reaches the session, `mcp_launch::team_clone_dir`) → this Mac (`settings.library.plySkillsOff`) → on. `KnowledgeMcpContext::new` reads it at every launch and builds the `ply` plugin from what is on, for Claude (`--plugin-dir`) and Codex (the `ply-app` marketplace in the `CODEX_HOME` overlay). IPC: `personal_skills_view`; `personal_skill_promote` copies one into the repository (`project_skills::install_into_repo`, no commit) or into the team library (`write_promoted_skill` inside `TeamStore::publish_paths`, behind the team gate), resolving the source by name through the reader so the window never names a path. **GH #100:** `listed_skill_dir(home, path)` is the guard behind `personal_skill_reveal` (`open -R`, the way `worktree_reveal` spawns it — no Tauri plugin, so no new capability): it accepts only a **visible, real, direct** child of one of the three roots under this Mac's home, refusing a root itself, anything deeper, a relative path, a `.`/`..` segment (compared on the raw segments, because `Path::components()` normalises a `.` away), a dot child, a symlink and another home's skill — each in words, each with a test. Still nothing opened for writing. |

The writable `Open in Code` is deliberately absent — a file Ply must never write is not handed to an editor that saves — so the row offers `Details` with the path and `Reveal in Finder`, and (**GH #174**) a `›` that opens the file in Code **read-only** (place `this-mac`), its bytes from `read_listed_instruction_file(home, path)`, which runs the guard below before reading. `listed_instruction_file(home, path)` is the guard behind `personal_instruction_reveal` (`open -R`, spawned the way `personal_skill_reveal` spawns it — no Tauri plugin, so no new capability): it compares the path **literally** against `home.join(rel)` for those two names and refuses a relative path, a `.`/`..` segment, a symlink, anything that is not a regular file, and another home's file, each in words and each with a test. No `canonicalize`, which would follow the links it refuses. IPC: `personal_instruction_reveal`, `personal_instruction_read` (GH #174); the files themselves come with `library_instructions_view`. |

CRUD includes create/delete/activate/rename, editor-file state, appearance/layout, verification configuration, and the legacy `pinnedRightTabs` compatibility field. **v1.61.0 onboarding:** `import_default(repo_path)` pre-registers a workspace from a committed `.atelier/workspace.json` (GH #79), else `.atelier/workspace_default.json`; `import_pending(queue_path)` drains `pending_imports.json`. `nameChosen` (owner, 2026-10-20): `create` sets it when the name differs from the folder's, `rename_by_user` (the sidebar) always, `import_default` and `create_named_by_team` (Settings › Team) never; `rename` (the shared Name applied) leaves it. `pinnedRightTabs` remains `serde(default)` for old files, but the current sanitizer retains only `knowledge`, and the frontend default is `[knowledge]`. Tier-2 tests cover every persisted-field migration. |
| `settings.rs` | `SettingsStore` over `settings.json`. `schemaVersion: 1`; `serde(default)` for backward compat. #74: `behavior.models` holds a Builder and a Challenger row per vendor — `RoleModels::for_role(vendor, role)` returns a `ModelChoice` and `model_for(&app, …)` reads it from the live store; blank fields push no CLI flag, and Ply never writes to `~/.claude/settings.json` or `~/.codex/config.toml`. Invalid JSON moved to `settings.json.broken-<ts>` and defaults are used. GH #175: `experimentalLayout` is no field any more — an old file's key is ignored on load and dropped on the next write; `swarmArtifactRetentionDays` is owned by this side (the frontend shape does not carry it) and `SettingsStore::replace` keeps the stored value through every save. `#[cfg(test)] mod tests` covers the v0.4.0-fixture migration and the GH #175 round trip. 1.179.0: `aiAccounts` (`AiAccountsSettings`, absent = Main alone, left out of the file while empty, an unreadable value read as Main; unknown vendor keys and unknown account fields kept) is owned by `ai_accounts` and kept through a window save; unknown top-level keys (`Settings.extra`) survive a load and a save. |
| `retry_on.rs` *(1.169.0 A3)* | "Retry on…": a round run again **once** on another model and effort; the card's own `models` never change. Offered (`retryable`, mirrored by `retryOn.ts::retryOffered`) for a Builder session that ran and is over — closed, ended on its own while the app ran, or a failed resume — with real work (a snapshot, or changes in the card's copy, which only Rust checks); never one lost when Plyloft quit or restarted (`BuilderState.lostOnRestart`, set by the launch cleanup: Resume's), never a record no session ran in, never while built by both — and for a settled Challenger review of the latest snapshot; never on an archived card or a pull request's card. A Challenger retry whose vendor can't review now (decided as `review_snapshot` does, `builder::challenger_choice_now`) is cancelled with the verdict kept; a review that starts without the retry's choice (a plain one, or one restarted after a quit) settles an open retry as "interrupted"; the launch cleanup settles an open Builder retry as "ended". The choice is an explicit argument of the round's own start, nothing is held elsewhere: `builder_start(…, retry)` → `builder::start` (a new session on the work already in the card's copy), and `builder_review_retry(ws, card, vendor, retry)` → `review_again_with` (the same snapshot again even after a verdict; `ChallengerJob.retry`; `reviewer::run_review`'s `model_override`). The Challenger's vendor is Rust's (`planned_challenger`, IPC `card_retry_plan`); a review that runs on another drops the choice and emits `retry-on://dropped`. A `ModelRetry` (`KanbanCard.modelRetries`, bounded 20, unstamped, never synced: the attempt it retried — start, model, outcome — and the retry's model, its own outcome settled when its session ends or its review settles) is recorded only once the session or review started. `ReviewRound.model` records each round's model; Activity lists both attempts. Frontend: `components/RetryOnPick.tsx`, `lib/retryOn.ts`. |
| `team_models.rs` *(1.169.0 A2)* | The models and efforts the team suggests: `.atelier/workspace.json` → `models` (`{claude,codex}: {builder, challenger, builderReasoning, challengerReasoning}`, every key optional, unknown keys kept at both levels, a wrong type read as no suggestion — the definition stays readable). A shared field like the others (`workspace_definition::FIELDS` "Suggested models"): kept in step on this Mac in `projects/<ws>/team_models.json` (atomic) by every refresh, edited through `workspace_definition_save` and published by the `shared_config` queue by whoever can push; read-only otherwise (GH #82). Resolution: `settings::resolve_model_with_team` / `resolve_card_model_with_team` — card > my workspace > team > my app > the tool's default, field by field (a forced workspace field still passes nothing); at spawn `resolve_card_model_fitted` walks the effort layers against the catalog, so an effort the model doesn't take falls to the next layer's. `model_for_workspace` / `model_for_card` read it through `team_models::for_workspace`, which ignores a leftover file in a workspace that shares no definition. Publishing merges `models` per vendor and key (`shared_config::merge_models`): different keys both land, the same key changed on both sides is named as an override. Clearing every suggestion removes the key. IPC `team_models_get` (`{shared, readOnly, models}`, from the store and the cache's flags) — what menus, chips and lines read. An older Plyloft keeps `models` as an unknown key. Frontend: `store/teamModelsStore.ts` (from the definition view), `workspaceModels.ts::withTeam` / `AppRows`, the **Team** label (`TEAM_LABEL`). |
| `areas.rs` *(1.173.0 B1)* | Areas: one repository, several products. `.atelier/workspace.json` → `areas` (`[{id, name, path, targetBranch?, versionFiles?, tagPrefix?, label?, lookAt?}]`; `path` from the repository root, `versionFiles` from the workspace's folder; unknown keys kept), a shared field like the others (`workspace_definition::FIELDS` "Areas", not a project field), published as a whole list. Absent or empty = one implicit area with the workspace's values, addressed as `None` (`IMPLICIT_AREA_ID` "default" where a file needs a name); nothing is written for it, and an older Plyloft ignores the key. **Rules** (`check`, `check_in` with the workspace's prefix): unique slug ids (≤ 40, not `all`/`default`), at most one root area (`path` "" or "/"), no overlapping folders, unique case-blind labels (default `area:<id>`, ≤ 50, no commas, not `status:`/`plyloft:`), unique tag prefixes git takes, branch names git takes, http(s) `lookAt`. **Lenient:** the field is `AreasField::Valid | Invalid {raw, problem}` — a broken list never makes the definition unreadable and is kept as a JSON value (every key; formatting and key order are Plyloft's); the refresh holds it (`hold_bad_areas`, `DefinitionCache::field_problems["areas"]` = message + the last good value), applies every other field, and keeps this Mac's last good copy; with none the workspace is *held* (`areas::held`). The editor refuses a list with errors. **Tags:** an explicit `tagPrefix` wins at any position; default "" for the first area, `<id>-` for the others; `release_version(prefix, tag)` reads `^<prefix>v\d+\.\d+\.\d+$`. **Resolution:** `area_of_path` (longest folder, else the root area, else none), `resolve(areas, workspace_branch)` → `ResolvedArea` (with `branch`), `resolve_config` / `project_config::config_for(store, ws, area)` → `AreaConfig::{Implicit, Area, Unknown, Held}` (B3/B4 refuse on the last two). This Mac's copy: `projects/<ws>/areas.json` (atomic; 1.174.0 CH-6: one writer at a time behind a mutex, a file that isn't JSON set aside as `areas.json.unreadable-<ms>` through `atomic::load_or_set_aside`), kept in step by every refresh. **1.174.0 (C10, D6):** `source: "local"` (absent = `shared`, every older file) — saved by Settings › Areas where the workspace shares no definition (`save_local`, IPC `areas_save_local`), and then it decides (`for_workspace`, the view's `keptHere`); a `shared` file there (a setup no longer shared) still decides nothing. Share's seed and a legacy conversion carry the kept list (`kept_here`) in their one `chore(config)` commit of `.atelier/workspace.json`; a refresh that finds a definition reconciles (`reconcile_shared`, called from `refresh_with` after `hold_bad_areas`): the same list becomes `shared`, another one (the repository's areas, none, or a broken list) is set aside — `areas.set-aside-<ms>.json` plus `setAside {areas, file, at}` in `areas.json`, out of the list this Mac runs with — and nothing is written to the repository. The Board's sheet (`AreasChoiceSheet`) answers through IPC `areas_answer_set_aside(publishMine)`: false clears `setAside` (the file stays), true is one `save_changes` of the shared `areas` field. A Share after someone shared without areas takes the kept list as that press's edit (`ShareOutcome.areasShared`). Commit messages and toasts name the areas that changed (`change_words`). IPC `areas_get` (`AreasView {shared, readOnly, defined, areas, workspaceBranch, check, problem, held, keptHere, setAside}`) and `areas_check(areas, workspaceId?)`, both `command(async)`: only when an area has version files they ask git once per folder for the workspace's prefix (`workspace_prefix`, cached; "" when git can't say, which only affects warnings). Held is one rule (`areas::is_held`), used by `config_for`, the view and the Doctor's Shared workspace definition row (`kept` warning / `held` error, appended after any pending edit). Frontend: `ipc.ts` types, `repositoryPage.ts` `AREA_FIELDS` (B5's page), `definitionRefresh.ts` `areaChangeWords`. |
| `release_scope.rs` *(1.174.0 DR1, D1(b))* | What a release of one area carries, read before the person presses anything (release.html states 1–4, 10); nothing persisted, nothing written to GitHub. IPC `release_scope(workspaceId, areaId?)` → `ReleaseScope` and `release_scopes(workspaceId)` → every area's, in order (DR5: one fetch for every target and tag prefix, the Board, ready cards and record read once); both async on a worker. `ReleaseScope {area, name, tagPrefix, targetBranch, throughGithub, head, fetchProblem, countProblem, partial, folder, sinceTag, sinceAt, cards: ScopeCard[] (newest ≤ 100), cardsTotal, otherChanges: OtherChange[] (newest ≤ 100), otherTotal, readyCardIds, localAhead, localBehind, diverged, currentVersion, versionSource, fileVersion, tagVersion, versionOnGithub, versions: VersionChoice[], refusal, inFlight, busy}`. On a GitHub `origin` it fetches the target (`worktrees::fetch_origin_branch`) and the areas' `<prefix>v*` tags (`fetch_origin_release_tags`, never forced), shared per (repository, target) for 30 s behind a mutex (`shared_fetch`; a failure for 5 s at most; a branch GitHub doesn't have — `shared_config::classify_fetch_error` — is the no-target refusal, any other failure `fetchProblem`), and counts `origin/<target>`; elsewhere this computer's target. `git log --first-parent <prefix>vX.Y.Z..<target> -- <folder> [:(exclude) other areas]` (latest strict tag, never `-unverified`; the whole target before the first): the root area is the repository minus the other areas' folders and the workspace's `.atelier`, an area leaves out areas nested in it (`areas::area_folders`); without areas the workspace's folder (git's prefix); `:(top,literal)` pathspecs, `icase` on Windows. Each commit goes to a card by `shipHistory`/`commitSha` or its pull request's merge commit, then its pull request number (`(#N)` of a squash, `Merge pull request #N`), then a `Card:` trailer; a rebase merge's earlier same-second, same-committer commits go with it only for a card whose `pr.mergeMethod` is rebase or unknown, never over a commit with its own pull request number or another card's trailer. Release commits and shared-config commits (`chore(config): …`, `atelier/config-…` merges, `Settings: … (#N)` squashes) touching only `shared_config::check_path` files (from the workspace's folder) are skipped; the rest are other changes (title, pull request number, author — a merge's from its second parent, all read in one `git log --no-walk=unsorted --stdin`). Versions: the later of the version file at the counted target and the last tag; patch first, the file's own version after it when ahead of the tag (and free), minor, major; never released: the file's version as is then 0.1.0, or 0.1.0 first with no file; a taken default puts the next free patch first. `refusal` (never empty; a refused area of `release_scopes` keeps its name, tag prefix and branch from `areas::resolve`): not set up, held/unknown area, no version file of its own, no target branch. `countProblem` when git can't count; `partial` on a shallow clone (never deepened). `Err` is raw (no workspace or repository) and mapped by the callers. Known gap: a renamed area folder counts only its new folder. Tests in `release_scope_tests.rs` (git fixtures). |
| `area_discovery.rs` *(1.173.0 B2)* | Does the repository hold more than one product? `detect(files, read)` (pure, tested on Lyra-like, single-product and nested-package fixtures): a product at the root (a project file — `package.json`, `pyproject.toml`, `requirements.txt`, `Cargo.toml`, `go.mod`, … — or a deploy file — `Dockerfile*`/`Containerfile*` case-blind, `railway.*`, `vercel.json`, `fly.toml`, `render.yaml`, `firebase.json`, `nixpacks.toml`, …; a monorepo's root — `workspaces` in its package.json, `pnpm-workspace.yaml`, `turbo.json`, `nx.json`, `lerna.json` — is none itself) and sub-folders with **both** a project file and a deploy file of their own (≤ 3 deep; hidden, dependency, build, example, test, template and archive folders never count, a `docs/` one only with a site generator in its package.json; a product inside another is part of the outer one). Each is an `app` or a `site` (an Astro/Docusaurus/… dependency, or *site* in the folder's name). `AreaDiscovery { products, suggestion: [AreaDef], notice }`: the suggestion keeps B1's rules (root first, ids `app`/`site` or the folder's slug, never reserved) and is written out for Settings › Areas (`filled_in`, B5: label `area:<id>`, explicit tag prefix, a folder's own package.json/pyproject.toml/Cargo.toml as version files, a package.json `homepage` as `lookAt`); `notice` = a product at the root and one beside it, or a monorepo's root with two. `discover(cwd)` runs it on `git ls-files -z -- <pathspecs()>` (only the names it reads) at the repository's top, cached per repository and `HEAD`; IPC `areas_discover` (`command(async)`), `None` for a workspace opened on a sub-folder. | `model_catalog.rs` *(1.169.0 A1)* | The model menus' catalog. **Sources:** `codex debug models` (cached PATH, 10 s bound, parsed field by field; `visibility` other than `list` kept *hidden*, its levels still known); `https://www.plyloft.com/models.json` through `account::ReqwestHttp`, cached atomically in `model_catalog.json`; the bundled `src-tauri/catalog/models.json`. **Network and privacy:** `codex debug models` may refresh Codex's list over the network with the member's own Codex login, so it runs at start and then at most once a day (a failed read is retried, at most once a minute, when a menu asks; a failed read keeps the last good list). The site file is a plain GET with no account, token or identifier (Plyloft's user agent only): at start, then a day after a good answer or an hour after a failed one; a 404 or no network silently uses the cache, then the bundled copy. The body is checked against 1 MB after `ReqwestHttp` reads it (it reads whole; its 20 s `HTTP_TIMEOUT` bounds it). **Schema:** `schemaVersion: 1`, per vendor `efforts` and `models[{id, label, efforts?, defaultEffort?}]`; a build reads only version 1, so **the site must keep serving a version-1 file for older Plyloft** (a new shape goes in a new file or alongside). **Menus** offer only the newest version of each family (owner D2, `latest_per_family`, mirrored in `modelCatalog.ts`; `src/lib/modelFamilies.fixtures.json` holds both). **Spawn:** `fit(vendor, choice)` runs inside `settings::model_for_workspace` / `model_for_card` and `builder::builder_model`: no `--effort` on a Claude Code older than `MIN_CLAUDE_VERSION_EFFORT` (2.1.111) or of unknown version; an effort the chosen model is known not to take, or (no model chosen) one the vendor doesn't take, passes no flag; an unknown model keeps it. Every Claude spawn passes `ModelChoice::claude_flags()` (`--model`, `--effort`). IPC `model_catalog_get`, event `model-catalog-changed`. |
| `bundle_fingerprint.rs` *(Direction C Part 3 self-check SC-1)* | Native installed-app provenance. Walks the `.app` containing `current_exe` without a shell or subprocess and reproduces the bootstrap installer's path/type/POSIX-mode/file-SHA/symlink-target manifest exactly. One adversarial test runs the native and shell implementations over the same bundle and requires byte-for-byte manifest plus digest parity. Newline/CR paths, relative/missing roots, and symlink roots fail closed. |
| `installed_self_check.rs` *(Direction C Part 3 self-check SC-1)* | Observation-only P3-1 receipt authority. Defines the exact fourteen check ids, nineteen card states (twenty-one since v1.112.0: `challenger-reviewing`, `changes-requested`), bounded typed DOM/action/Intake/capability facts, and the read-only IntakeRegistry+PtyRegistry checkpoint. Card-state facts carry their observed card id and distinguish deterministic `seeded` presentation evidence from lifecycle-`reached` evidence; one session may cover multiple cards in its locked workspace while Intake continuity remains anchored to the card open at Begin. Missing store authority remains nullable instead of being inferred from rendered DOM; transition samples pair by opaque id rather than list adjacency; diagnostic reasons are backend-normalized to the shared cap. Frontend provenance/status/path fields are rejected; a claimed pass requires supporting facts; Tauri recomputes `overall`, stamps native bundle identity plus observation-count/cap diagnostics, and atomically writes schema-v1 JSON only inside the existing app-log directory. No product state or lifecycle process is touched. |
| `files.rs` | Stateless filesystem helpers: `list_dir`, `read_text_file` (rejects > 5 MB / NUL-containing files / non-UTF-8), `write_text_file` (atomic via tempfile sibling). |
| `files_order.rs` | `FilesOrderStore` over `files-order.json`. Per-workspace × per-directory custom ordering for the FilesPanel drag-drop reorder. |
| `watchdog.rs`, `memory_facts.rs` *(1.173.0 H1)* | **A frozen window says so in the log.** The frontend's heartbeat (`lib/heartbeat.ts`: IPC `watchdog_beat` every 2 s visible, 30 s hidden, no logging) and a `watchdog` thread (1 s tick, 5 s while hidden, `park_timeout`) whose pure `Decider` (injected ms clocks) logs one WARN at 10, 30 and 120 s of a visible window's silence and one INFO when it answers; posts a no-op through `run_on_main_thread` every 5 s and logs a stall past 5 s (30 s hidden: App Nap). A gap in its own ticks on the awake clock (macOS `CLOCK_UPTIME_RAW`, Windows `QueryUnbiasedInterruptTime`) is Plyloft starved — one WARN and a `busy` episode — while time on the clock that counts sleep only (`CLOCK_MONOTONIC_RAW`, `GetTickCount64`) is one INFO `the computer slept` and nobody's silence. Silence counts only while both the OS (`window_shown`) and the page (each beat) say visible; no beat 30 s after start is one WARN. `watchdog::quitting()` at the top of `run_exit_cleanup` stops the probes. Each WARN carries `memory_facts::read()` (macOS `host_statistics64`, `sysctlbyname` vm.swapusage/hw.memsize/vm.pagesize, `proc_pid_rusage` footprint of the app process only; Windows `GlobalMemoryStatusEx`, `K32GetProcessMemoryInfo`) — no child process. `run.json` (persistence map; atomic, ≤ once a minute, at once on a WARN up to 3 per freeze) carries the run to the next start, which logs `startup: the last run (since …) did not end cleanly…` when it has no `ended_at`. `watchdog::stop()` in `run_exit_cleanup`, just before `exit: cleanup done`, ends the thread and closes the marker (Hard rules 3, 4). `watchdog_status` feeds Settings › Diagnostics › Responsiveness (`lib/responsiveness.ts` words; Copy details has the watchdog's lines). Close/focus tell it the window is hidden/shown (`window_shown`). Test bridge `app.freeze` (dev builds) freezes the page or the main thread for a walk. |
| `watcher.rs` | `WatcherRegistry`: per-workspace `notify` debouncer (250 ms). `SelfWriteTracker` with 750 ms TTL prevents echo from `fs_write_text_file`. `NOISY_NAMES` excludes `.git`, `.atelier`, `node_modules`, `target`, `dist`, etc. App-state dirs are added to `excluded` at startup. Emits `fs://changed` events; the events struct is `Deserialize`-able. |
| `search.rs` | `SearchRegistry`: spawns `rg --json` per workspace, streams matches as `search://result` events, finishes with `search://done`. New query cancels in-flight `rg`. 1000-result cap. |
| `agents.rs` *(v0.3.0; expanded through agent collaboration Phase 3)* | `AgentRegistry` owns config CRUD, run lifecycle, transcript writing, cooldowns, stream parsers, and inflight guards. Oneshot runs use stream JSON (Codex `exec --json`; a Claude run's session transcript, then Plyloft's result line — no `claude -p`), capture snapshots before spawn, compute changes after exit, and create handoffs only after evidence finalization. Phase 3 classifies project-relevant oneshots as exclusive unattended writers; queued work enters the bounded writer queue on busy, while a manual immediate request returns typed busy without creating a run. `reconcile_orphans` rewrites prior pending/running runs as killed at startup. |
| `card_execution.rs` *(Direction C Part 3 P3-0B)* | Single-process per-card verification/work interlock. A short mutex serializes the opposing live-authority check and publication only; runner-backed work gets an RAII attempt-pending marker, while verification publishes a deferred `VerifyRegistry` record before releasing the gate and starts its worker afterward. Live Agent counters, active/resumable card Swarms, and the existing derived card-swarm queue are authority; stale persisted `lastRunStatus: running` alone never blocks. No lease, heartbeat, or persisted lock is introduced. |
| `card_area.rs` *(1.173.0 B3)* | A card belongs to one area. `KanbanCard.area: Option<String>` (`area`, an `AreaDef.id`; serde-default, not written when `None`): set at creation (`kanban_card_create`'s `area`, validated; the frontend's `currentAreaFor`, which B2 fills in), kept by a follow-up and a fork, changed only through `kanban_card_set_area` (while Not started with no copy, or on a card with no area or an unlisted one; a card with a copy only to an area whose branch is the one its copy was cut from, `CardAreaState.fits`; never under Held). Non-fast-forward retried once; refused → `atelier/config-next-improvement-<area>-<ts>` (`-2`… when taken; never forced) + `Settings: Next improvement direction — <area>` pull request (`PullRequests` trait, `gh` in the app); a press while it is open adds a fast-forward commit on its branch; merged settles, closed forgets; without `gh` the refusal marks this Mac read-only. Plain words in `pending.message`/`problem`, git's in `technical`/`problemTechnical`. Offline: retried by every definition refresh (`drain`, which with every press emits `ni://direction {workspaceId, area}` per area changed). **Draft** (`draft`, pure): README/package.json, a file over 64 KiB not read (`cat-file -s` first); "For whom" from the README's first prose paragraph, else package.json `description`, else empty; "What better means" never drafted; the default "What you don't want", Polish; "Where to look at it" from the area's `lookAt`, the Railway service of this repository and area (D1's Railway reading only, `deploy_map::railway_services`, cached, asked only while there is no direction or its address is empty), package.json `homepage` (not a code host) or the README — shown, never written. **Kept on this computer (E6):** `place` is the one resolver — `Repository(Target)` while the workspace shares a definition, else `ThisComputer`; `record` (every press), `view`, the draft and `direction_for` (E2's round) branch only on it. `ThisComputer`: `projects/<ws>/next_improvement/direction/<area>.json`, the same schema, atomic, no git, no queue, editable (no read-only rule; `shared: false`). On share (Share, ConvertLegacy or a later definition, i.e. any refresh with a definition where this Mac isn't read-only), `drain_with` (and a shared press) first runs `adopt_local`: each local file becomes a queue entry `moved: true` with no base under the area it belongs to (`local_area_for`: a name `resolve_area` accepts, or `default` → the only area; any other file stays, logged at debug, and `keptHere` says so (`orphan_words`) on the implicit area's view or the area a call naming none goes to), queue written first, then the file removed; an area already queued takes the local direction as its value when it differs (shared → unshared → shared), keeping its own base, `moved` and pull request (an open one is updated; a merged one clears `moved`). The publish takes a moved entry only while the branch has no file for that area or the same one (`yields`): a different one wins (`AreaOutcome::KeptRepository`, also at a press and in `effective`), the entry is dropped and `kept_repository` in the queue makes the view's `notice` say so once until `ni_direction_notice_seen`. A press after the share clears `moved` (an ordinary change: merged, C3's question). Until it moves — a read-only Mac, or before the next refresh — the local file counts as a pending moved entry (`pending_for`) in the view and in `direction_for`, so it stays shown and used until the branch has its own; a read-only Mac says `kept_until_writable_words`. IPC (`command(async)`): `ni_direction_get(workspaceId, area?)` → `DirectionView`, `ni_direction_save(workspaceId, area?, direction)`, `ni_reject(workspaceId, area?, idea, reason)`, `ni_unreject(workspaceId, area?, index, title, at)`, `ni_direction_notice_seen(workspaceId, area)`. E2 reads `direction_for`. **The direction written before the areas (1.174.0, C11):** `project_direction` is the `default` direction once the project has two or more areas (kept here; shared: the branch's with this Mac's queued change, else a local orphan); `before_areas` → `BeforeAreas {direction, areas}` (the areas without their own); `adopt_project` copies it, rejected list included, into each named area without one (kept here: atomic `save_local`; shared: a queued entry with no base and the line "Direction written before the areas", published by the caller's drain; read-only refused), with optional editor `fields` over it; the local `default` file is removed once every area took it or has a readable direction of its own (an unreadable branch file is never written over, and doesn't count; a branch's `default` is never removed). The IPC drains each area it went to (`Some(area)`). `DirectionView.beforeAreas` while the area has none. IPC: `ni_direction_project(workspaceId)` → `BeforeAreas | null` (reads only), `ni_direction_adopt_project(workspaceId, areas, direction?)` → the areas that took it (workspace lock, then `drain_with` per area, `ni://direction`). The sheet is asked through `useNextImprovement.adoptAsk` and drawn by `AdoptDirectionSheetHost` (App.tsx), so a Save from the leave guard still shows it; it shows only while its workspace is the active one and is dropped when that workspace is removed. Kept on this computer with one area, that area reads `default` until it has its own file (`local_read_for`, as `local_file_for` in the shared case); a press there writes `<area>.json` and removes `default`. `orphan_words("default")` never says "default". |
| `next_improvement_round.rs` (`next_improvement::round`) *(1.173.0 E2)* | Next improvement's **round**: one AI proposes 0–5 ideas for an area, a second opinion keeps or sets aside each, the slate waits for the person. **Hosted by the workspace, not a card**: each side is a `ChatRegistry` lane `chat::Lane::Improve { area, round, role }` (`ChatKey::improve`, `card_id` ""; `LaneHost::Workspace`, `chat::require_host` beside the unchanged `require_card`), run through the plan lanes' API (`plan_lane_open`/`plan_send`/`lane_view`/`plan_interrupt`/`plan_drop`): Claude's hidden TUI in a PTY (`spawn_chat`: cached PATH, `-p` refused, coordination `Intake`/read-only without a card) or `codex app-server` read-only — Hard rules 1 and 2 as Plan with two AIs. Folders `claude-sessions/chats/<ws>/next-improvement/<area>/round-<12 hex>/<proposer|critic>` (plain, under the session root; Codex key `ni:<ws>:<area>:<round>:<role>`); no `chat://`/`plan://` events for these lanes; the critic lane takes the Challenger model row. The proposer's Claude argv is `claude_chat_argv` + `chat::with_web_reading(args, roots)`: `WebFetch`,`WebSearch` added to `--tools` and out of `--disallowedTools`, and `--allowedTools` = `Skill,Read(//<copy>/**),Grep(//<copy>/**),Glob(//<copy>/**),WebFetch,WebSearch` (`permission_roots`: a Windows root also as `//c/…/**`), so under `dontAsk` no file outside the copy can be read and sent out through a fetch; `--strict-mcp-config` stays, so no browser or screenshot tool: ideas say they read the page's text and the code. The second opinion keeps the plain read-only profile (no web). **Slots**: a round's lane takes one of the chats' 3 sessions through `make_room` (no reservation; a running plan exchange's reservation leaves one), evicting the least recently used Ready chat; a send refused for room is retried for up to 2 minutes (`room_wait`, within the turn's net) while the line under way says "Waiting for one of Plyloft's 3 AI chats to finish", then `ROOM_WORDS`; a lane suspended after it answered is read from its record. **Code** (`code_tip`): the area's branch (`areas::resolve`, else the project's target) fetched first (`Plumbing::fetch`, bounded); the local branch when it contains `origin/<branch>` (`merge-base --is-ancestor`), else origin's; offline the last fetched tip and `codeNote` "Read the code as of <day>: …"; a plain `pinned_read::DetachedReadWorktree` under `claude-sessions/next-improvement/worktrees/`, given as `--add-dir`, removed at the round's end; a copy a quit or crash left goes at launch (`ReviewerRegistry::new`: `take_orphan_read_trees` and `retire_session_leftovers` for `next-improvement`), the area's old `round-*` lane folders before its next pass (`sweep_round_dirs`). A copy that can't be made is a `failed` round with `COPY_WORDS` and git's text in `technical`; lane errors in the registry's words ("plan session", spawn, OS) become `SESSION_WORDS` with the text in `technical` (`user_words`). **Area** as E1 (`area_key`); no direction or a blank one refuses (`NO_DIRECTION_WORDS`). **Line-up** (`plan_exchange::lineup`): the direction's `proposer` (else Claude) proposes, the other gives the second opinion; one AI ready → the same AI in a separate fresh lane (`oneAi`, `byline`). **Prompts** `resources/prompts/next_improvement_proposer.md` and `next_improvement_second.md` (`include_str!`, ≤ 8,000) with the JSON contracts; `parse_proposal`/`parse_verdicts` strict (one object; fields, S|M|L, low|some|high; one verdict per idea with a reason); a malformed answer asked again once in the same session, then status `unreadable` with the raw text; `post_check` *(E4)*, pure, after parsing and before the second opinion (and on a *Change it…* rewrite): the first 5 checked, the rest set aside "more than five"; then one reason per idea, `by: check` — title close to a rejected one (`resembles`: the same letters without small words and spaces, or ¾ of the words the same word — equal, one letter in or out from 4 letters, within 2 edits from 8 — so one different key word is a different idea), sales pressure added in title/change (`pressure_in`: countdown, only N left / N seats left, pop-up, limited time… unless taken away in its clause or named in `removes` or `saw`), blank `saw`, no `where` left and no address in `saw`, blank `check`, empty `removes`, or blank `removes` when the round's (not the current direction's) only approach is Simplify; before it, `ground` keeps `where` files in the copy (`place_in_copy`: `#L40`, `:L40`, backticks/quotes, `path (…)`, `path lines 40-60`, a leading repository name; `RoundEnv::exists`), else the copy files `saw` names, and drops sources on the product's host or its GitHub repository (`RoundInput.repo`); a blank `saw`/`check`/`removes`, no place, or files not in the copy is asked for once in the same session (`blank_problems` → `blank_retry_text`, the whole answer again; `merge_retry` keeps the complete ideas when the retry holds fewer; a retry that times out or fails leaves the first answer); Claude's `looked.pages` are only the pages it fetched; all set aside → status `nothing`, `nothing` None, phase "Plyloft's check set aside N ideas…". The prompts carry the nine anti-slop rules (E4). **Limits**: one round per area (`RUNS_WORDS`), 20 min per answer, 30 min per round (`timedOut`), *Stop* (Esc / `turn/interrupt`, `stopped`). **Phases** `look`/`read`/`second` from the proposer's own `WebFetch`/`Read`/`Grep`/`Glob` calls, emitted with the round as `ni://progress` (`RoundView`). *Change it…* (`change`) rewrites one idea in fresh lanes and asks the second opinion again; `mark` records *Make it a card* (`cardId`) and *Don't propose this…* (`dismissed`); a stopped change keeps the idea (`CHANGE_STOPPED_WORDS`). Stop, Get and Mark take the area id as given when this Mac has a round for it (`round_area`), so a held or removed area's round can still be stopped and read. Quit: `Rounds::interrupt_all` in `run_exit_cleanup` before `ChatRegistry::kill_all` (saved `interrupted`, copy removed, the driver writes nothing after; Hard rule 4); a round saved running reads `interrupted` at launch; `forget_workspace` from `workspace_delete`. **Another round** *(1.174.0 C15, NIW-4)*: `ni_round_start` takes optional `approaches`/`proposer` for that round only (`round_approaches`; kept as `approachesOverride`/`proposerOverride` when they differ from the direction's), and the direction is only read, never written, so a shared project gets no commit for a round; the last round's unanswered ideas are carried into the new slate (`carried_ideas`, `carriedFrom: <round>`), answerable while it runs, kept through Stop, an unreadable answer and a failed start, listed to the proposer as still waiting, and placed after the round's own ideas; the Inbox's News row counts them while the round proposes. **Already a card** *(1.174.0 C8, P13)*: `prepare` reads the Board from `KanbanStore::load` (no git) and `planned_cards` keeps the area's open cards (not Done, not archived, not `card_area::exempt`; a card with no area counts for every area; every card without areas) and every card made of the latest round's ideas (`Rounds::made_cards`) — those first, then by `updated_at`, at most `PLANNED_MAX` (60); titles go in as JSON strings without control or bidi characters (`data_title`, `DATA_LINE`); they reach the proposer as "Already planned as cards for …", the phase `planned` says "read the N cards already planned for …" (`Round.plannedCount`), and `post_check` sets an idea whose title `resembles` one aside as "already a card: <title>" (`by: check`, `SetAside.card {id, title}`, which the line opens), after the rejected list's reason. IPC (`command(async)`): `ni_round_start(workspaceId, area?, approaches?, proposer?)`, `ni_round_change(workspaceId, area?, ideaId, comment)`, `ni_round_stop(workspaceId, area?)`, `ni_round_get(workspaceId, area?)`, `ni_round_mark(workspaceId, area?, ideaId, cardId?, dismissed)` → `RoundView {workspaceId, area, round?, byline?, elapsedMs?}`; TS `niRoundStart/Change/Stop/Get/Mark`, `onNiProgress` (`src/ipc.ts`). |

Plyloft's own repository keeps the drain (Wait for the work, then release; Stop the work and release now). Pure words and rules in `lib/releaseSheet.ts` (node-tested). Nothing persisted. |
| `components/ReleaseProgress.tsx` *(1.174.0 DR4)* | The Release sheet following a release (release.html states 6–9): drawn by `ReleaseSheet` while its press runs, while the workspace has a recorded release through a pull request (`ship_audit.pendingRelease.pr`), or once it is done here. Steps from `PrReleaseStep` (`releaseSheet.progressSteps`), the note of what Close leaves (`progressNote`), Stop (`release_stop`; asked first from `pushed` on, `stopAsks`), Open on GitHub, Update the release (`ship_release_update`) or Continue as its one filled button, done (`releasedWords`, the release page from the pull request's address), a record with a problem (`stoppedReleaseWords`, Dismiss). The sidebar's `ReleaseStatusRow` (SidebarStatus) and the Inbox's `release_stopped` row open it; `inbox.rs` gives one `released` row per tag. Nothing persisted. |
| `kanban.rs` *(v0.6.0; +knowledge in v0.8.0 D2)* | `KanbanStore` over `<config_dir>/kanban/workspaces/<wsId>.json`. Persisted card columns are Todo, In progress, and Complete; since C5 the Board derives its columns (Needs you, Working, Waiting, Done) from each card's state and never writes `column_id` from a drag. `set_board_order` (IPC `kanban_card_set_board_order`) writes only `board_order`, leaving `updated_at`; `archive_card(…, auto)` stamps `archived_at` / `auto_archived` and, for the auto-archive (`auto_archive_eligible`: landed 14+ days ago, never archived, no worktree), re-checks under the cache lock; `restore_card` (IPC `kanban_card_restore`, async on a worker under `CARD_START_LOCK` since QoL 1.168) gives back every archived card and keeps `archived_at`; `commands::restore_card_locked` then makes a copy the archive couldn't move (`kept_copy`) the card's own again, or brings an unshipped card's copy back from the Trash on its branch (`worktrees::restore_card_copy_from_trash`, under `TRASH_GC_LOCK`), and on Free puts a card that would take a held card slot back in To do. C7: `blocked_by: Vec<String>` (`blockedBy`, the cards of the same workspace a card waits for; serde-default, not written when empty, so an old board loads unchanged) is set only through `set_blocked_by` (IPC `kanban_card_set_blocked_by`: the whole list, atomic, refusing the card itself, a stranger and a cycle with nothing written); `start_refusal` holds a Todo card while a blocker has not landed (asked by `intake_start`, `builder_start`, `kanban_card_advance_to_in_progress`); `delete_card` prunes the id from every `blocked_by`; an archived blocker holds no one. `builder_vendor_pref: Option<BuilderVendor>` (`builderVendorPref`, the Builder vendor chosen on the card before a build; serde-default, not written when `None`) is set only through `set_builder_vendor_pref` (IPC `kanban_card_set_builder_vendor_pref`, local, leaves `updated_at`); the frontend's effective vendor is `effectiveBuilderVendor` (this session's pick, then the card's, then `behavior.builderVendor`), and a live session's `builder.vendor` stays the fact. `KanbanCard` remains an additive schema with optional/defaulted run, Knowledge, review, ship, worktree, sync, and intake fields. `pr_review: Option<PrReview>` (`prReview`, Challenger on a pull request; serde-default, not written when `None`) is set only by `pr_review_create` and kept by its refresh, start and push. Tier-2 migration tests cover legacy cards and column aliases. |
| `line_comments.rs` *(SL-5)* | Comments on lines of a card's review diff, for its Builder: `KanbanCard::line_comments` (`lineComments`; serde-default, not written when empty, so an old board loads unchanged). A comment holds the path, the new-file range of its added/context lines (`newStart`–`newEnd`), the base-version range of its removed lines (`oldStart`–`oldEnd`), the quoted diff lines, the text and `sentAt`. Written only through `KanbanStore::{add,update,remove}_line_comment` / `mark_line_comments_sent` (IPC `card_line_comment_add`/`_update`/`_remove`, `card_line_comments_mark_sent`; atomic, `updated_at` left alone); sent comments are history and refuse edits. Local only — no `gh` call. The selection → range mapping and the one message are `src/lib/lineComments.ts`; Send to Builder (`src/lib/lineCommentSend.ts`) resumes a closed session (`builder_resume`) and pastes through `builderStore.deliverWhenIdle`, marking sent only once written. |
| `later_requests.rs` *(1.172.0 A1)* | What the person told a card's Builder after the first result — the result's Not right, Request changes on the review's lines, or Not right — tell the Builder in Ask about this card: `KanbanCard::later_requests` (`laterRequests`; serde-default, not written when empty, so an old board loads unchanged), each `{id, text, at, source}`, the newest 10 of at most 1,500 characters. Kept by the frontend once a message reached the Builder (`builderStore.askBuilder`'s `laterRequest`, `sendChanges`), through `KanbanStore::{add,remove}_later_request` (IPC `kanban_card_add_later_request` / `kanban_card_remove_later_request`; atomic, `updated_at` left alone; removal refused once the card has landed). Shown under What you want as "Asked later (n)" (`LaterRequests.tsx`); appended after the capped brief in `review_context::brief_of` (the Challenger's brief, together with the line comments already sent to the Builder, newest 10, in time order), in `builder_brief` (a card built again; requests only), and in the pull-request draft's material (`pr_draft::prompt`). Never mirrored to the card's issue (no `gh` call); Ship's pull-request draft may quote them. A follow-up card starts without them. |

| `proposals.rs` *(GH #104, ADR 0005; BOARDS.md §4 state C)* | The Challenger's **improvement proposal**: after the last review of a card that met friction, one more short read-only pass proposes at most one change to a repository skill or to the root instruction files. This module owns all of it and nothing else owns any of it. `met_friction` is the rule — two or more `changes_requested` rounds (`BuilderState.changes_requested_count`), a failed Verify, or at least one correction the human typed after the Builder's first answer (`BuilderState.user_corrections`, counted by `BuilderSession`'s `onData` through `builder_note_correction`, because Ply keeps no Builder transcript) — and **at most one proposal per card** (`proposedCards`). `proposal_prompt` carries exactly the card's list: the brief (`review_context::brief_of`), each round's verdict with its findings, the Verify failures' summaries, and the repository's `SKILL.md`s and instruction files. The pass runs only on a **settled** card (`card_settled`, GH #104 walk: the last review approved, or its request for changes was overruled by the human; nothing waiting) — on the Challenger's own thread from `builder::challenger_loop` when no further review waits, or from `after_human_decision` when the human accepts the Builder's pushback — and its prompt carries `## What the human decided (authoritative)`: the words of the last corrections (`BuilderState.correction_texts`, ≤ 5 × 500 bytes) and how each request for changes ended (`BuilderState.review_decisions`), with the rule that a proposal never goes against them (the words stay on the local card and are sent only to the Challenger's CLI for this pass); the answer's optional `SOURCE:` line is stored (`source`) and shown in the block. It runs through `reviewer::run_read_only_pass` — the review's own spawn, argv builder and `SpawnIntent`, so the restricted read-only profile and the exit-time kill are the review's and not a second copy — in a throwaway `DetachedReadWorktree` at the checkout's HEAD, `PASS_TIMEOUT_SECS` 5 min. **Every failure is dropped in silence**: no provider, a timeout, an unparsable answer, a diff that does not apply — one log line, no retry, nothing on the card, and Ship is never blocked. The one exception (v1.164.0 part 5 follow-up): a Claude pass stopped at Claude's trust question (`claude_session::FOLDER_TRUST_REFUSAL`) records that card as `trustStoppedCard` (additive, absent when none) and gives its claim back, and the card draws the trust step where its proposal line would; the next Claude pass that gets past the question, or any later pass of that card, clears it (one slot per workspace). `parse_answer` reads `NONE` or one `SCOPE`/`TARGET`/`REASON`/`CHANGE`/`DIFF` block; a target or any diffed path outside `.claude/skills/`, `.agents/skills/`, `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md` is discarded, as is a multi-file diff, an oversized one, and one that `applies_cleanly` (`git apply --check` in the checkout, read-only) refuses. A personal, Team or Ply target carries words only. State: `projects/<ws>/improvement_proposal.json` (atomic, `#[serde(default)]`), dropped by `current` when older than 30 days or when the target's content id moved. `approve_change_full` builds the change for the channel's **existing** paths — a skill as `project_skill_publish`'s own `LibraryChange::new(ItemKind::Skill, …)` with the diffed file patched, an instruction file as GH #101's `LibraryChange::instructions` with `bases` from the mirror's manifest — queued through `workspace_definition::app_library_change`, which holds the workspace lock and refuses a read-only collaborator. Nothing writes the checkout. IPC: `improvement_proposal_state/dismiss/approve/trust_stopped`, `builder_note_correction`; event `improvement-proposal://changed`. 54 unit tests. |

Personal prompts (`prompts/library.json`) → Global `entry_type=Prompt` Knowledge entries (the extended memory store — see `memory.rs::KnowledgeType`); per-card `knowledge` → card-linked Workspace `Note` entries (the card's inline field stays). De-dupe by title / body+card-link makes sentinel-less re-runs no-ops; nothing is deleted; the team-shared `.atelier/library/prompts.json` is untouched. Runs at `.setup`, best-effort. 3 tests incl. Tier-2 legacy-memory-file load. |

Surfaced by `OverlapWarnings.tsx` on the Run and Outcome steps. Off the main thread since 1.174.0 (B4). |
| `command_threads_tests.rs` *(1.174.0 B4, CH-1)* | Guard test: every Tauri command whose own body, or a listed helper, runs git, `gh` or another process (`SPAWNS`: `git_command`, `new_command`, `Command::new`, `crate::git::`, `crate::github::`, `crate::overlap::`, the worktree listing, the issue mirror `gh_sync_card_*`, `crate::builder::done`, `crate::inbox::assemble_feed` and more) is `#[tauri::command(async)]` (also as the shipping half of a `cfg_attr` pair), an `async fn` or hands its work to a worker.
