The transcript accumulates against protocol item ids with a 2000-entry cap (`lib/codexTranscript.ts`); completed assistant messages render through the lazy `lib/markdown` chunk. See `docs/codex-tiles/DESIGN.md`. |
| `components/EditorTabContent.tsx` | Files + CodeMirror editor, hosted only by `CodeModeHost` (moved out of the retired `RightTabs.tsx`, GH #95). |
| `components/PaletteScopes.tsx` | UX-3.6: ⌘K's Files and In files scopes — the cached file index and fuzzy match Quick Open had, and `SearchPanel` driven by the surface's field. A match opens Code mode and dispatches `atelier:goto-line` after two animation frames so the editor document swap completes first. |
| `components/RepositoryPage.tsx` · `components/RepositorySettings.tsx` *(GH #94, UX language card 2; replaced `ProjectConfigPanel.tsx` and `SharedDefinitionPanel`, deleted)* | Settings › Repository — Build & release and General, edited in place: `RepositoryPage` is the shell, the draft and the publish bar; `RepositorySettings` (`RepositoryBuildRelease`, `RepositoryGeneral`) draws the rows for `ProjectConfig` and the shared definition — version files, the verification steps (Script and SlashCommand), Detect/Re-detect (`projectConfigDetect`) and the review checklist. The local config's Reset (`projectConfigDelete`, which removes the workspace's project subdirectory) is the This-Mac page's danger zone. |
| `components/ModeHeader.tsx` *(UX-3.5, the Shell board)* | The top of every centre mode: `ScreenHeader` (crumbs `Workspace › Mode`, title, at most one primary and a `⋯`) plus the sidebar button; `MODE_LABEL` is the one set of mode words. |
| `components/WorkspaceSwitcher.tsx` *(UX-3.3)* | The sidebar's workspace switcher: a popover with recents, a search field from six workspaces, the Needs you count per workspace, and Remove behind a confirmation. |
| `components/SidebarSlot.tsx` *(UX-3.4)* | The sidebar's frame: 200–320 px, resizable and remembered on this Mac, ⌘\ hides it, and below 1000 px it floats over the stage. |
| `components/SidebarStatus.tsx` *(UX-3.8)* | The quiet status rows at the sidebar's foot — an update, a missing tool, a drain: a dot, one sentence, one action word. No floating banners. |
| `components/ShortcutsSheet.tsx` *(UX-3.7)* | ⌘/ — every shortcut, drawn from `lib/shortcuts.ts`, the list the key handler matches with. |
| `components/HistoryResults.tsx` *(v1.25.0; UX-3.6)* | ⌘K's History scope (⌘⇧H): the scope toggle and the hits; the field is the palette's. |
| `components/InboxPanel.tsx` *(UX-4.2)* | The Inbox page: a summary sentence, then Needs you · Failed · Running · Scheduled, one action a row; waiting sessions are Needs you rows (UX-4.4). |
| `components/ActivityView.tsx` *(UX-4.5)* | History: a facts block and one `ListView` of runs and ships across workspaces. |
| `components/BoardList.tsx` · `components/BoardFilterMenu.tsx` *(UX-5.6, 5.7)* | The Board's List view (the same filtered cards, grouped by attention, sortable, Ship selected) and the filter shared with the columns. |
| `components/CardStateHeader.tsx` *(UX-6.2)* | The card page's Now callout: the state's words, one primary action, at most one quiet alternative — and, on a card whose copy has changes, **Try it** as the one allowed second quiet action (Try it TI-4, D18; also in the review). |
| `components/CardDependencies.tsx` *(UX-7.3)* | The card page's Blocked by and Blocks properties, with the picker that refuses the card itself, an archived card and a cycle. |
| `components/IntakeHistorySection.tsx` *(`IntakePlan`, UX-6.4)* | A finished intake as plain text — its plan and transcript — on the card page; a live one is the terminal. |
| `components/ChatPanel.tsx` · `store/chatStore.ts` · `lib/chatView.ts` *(card chat, part 2)* | The card's **Chat** tab: a card-page section (`uiStore.cardStep` = `"chat"`, a `CardPageSection` beside the lifecycle steps; opened by the header's Chat button, one section at a time, column 2 on a wide page), mounted once first opened and then kept (hidden like every section; keyed by card). Discussion only, said in the tab. A vendor picker (`Segmented`: `chat_open`, disabled while an answer is on its way); the conversation (`MarkdownText` for a finished answer, plain while it streams; tool lines; a shell item drawn loudly; interrupted; notices; a user message `isError` marked not sent); the token line (never a cost); the composer (Enter sends, Shift+Enter a new line, never during an IME composition; enabled for idle/ready/stopped, Stop while working; a refused send keeps the draft and says why); attachments by HTML5 drop (`data-drop-zone="card-chat"`, `CARD_CHAT_DROP_KINDS`) paste (files only when the clipboard holds no text; a pasted image named "Pasted image <date> <time>") and Add files… (`ux` `FilePickButton`, a hidden file input; `accept` on Windows only), read whole (`readDroppedFile`), sent as raw bytes (`chatAttachmentAdd`), chips with name, kind, size, pages, note and remove; refusals with the backend's reason; a polite live region for each finished answer. `reason` = `FOLDER_TRUST_REFUSAL` shows `ClaudeReviewTrustStep` (subject `chat`), any other reason a warning; `attention` offers Show terminal. `chatStore` keeps `byKey["ws::card"]` = `{vendor, opened, conversations: {claude?, codex?}, attachments, draft, pendingIds}`: snapshots from `chat_messages`/`chat_open`, `chat://state` per vendor, `chat://messages` merged by id (`mergeChatMessages`), both held while a snapshot is on its way and applied after it; a send's reply goes to the vendor it was sent to; one draft per vendor; an answer still streaming is shown finished on an interrupt or once no turn runs; a send opens the shown vendor first when this session has not. |
| `components/ChatTerminal.tsx` *(card chat, part 2)* | "Show terminal": an xterm on the chat's backend-owned Claude PTY, never spawned, killed, fitted or resized here — drawn at the grid's own 120 × 40 (12 px) in a pane that scrolls both ways (Mac walk F2: a view-sized terminal made Claude repaint and garbled the first paint). Subscribes first and holds `pty://data`; takes the grid's `rows` × `cols` when they differ, writes `chat_screen`'s `formatted` (screen and modes) with the held and later stream from the grid's `offset` (`builderAttach.replayJoin`) in one direct write; the member's keys go to `pty_write`. Says that what is typed there runs with the member's rights. |
| `components/shell.css` · `components/board.css` · `components/code.css` · `components/terminals.css` *(C3, C5, C8)* | The Shell, Board, Code and Terminals surfaces' CSS, on tokens only. |
| `scripts/tokens-css.mjs` *(UX-2.1)* | Generates the token block of `src/App.css` from `docs/ux-language/tokens.json` (the Ply Design System v2's copy); `designTokens.test.ts` fails on drift. |
| *(retired, UX revamp)* `QuickOpenModal.tsx` · `SearchModal.tsx` · `HistorySearchModal.tsx` (UX-3.6: ⌘K's scopes) · `DependencyBanner.tsx` (UX-3.8: a status row) · `CardStepper.tsx` (UX-6.5: the Timeline) · `ShipAuditModal.tsx` (UX-6.7: `ReleaseFlow`) · `ReleaseFlow.tsx`, `ShipBatchModal.tsx`, `ReleaseAreaSheet.tsx` (1.174.0 DR3: `ReleaseSheet`) · `RemoveTeamSkillButton.tsx` · `MemoryPanel.tsx`, `KnowledgeEvidenceView.tsx` and their `lib/knowledge*.ts` (UX-9.5 retired Notes; UX-10.2 their code) | Gone. Every modal is a `Sheet` (UX-10.1); there is no `modal-card`. |
| `store/workspaceStore.ts` | Zustand. Workspace list, active id, missing-cwd map. |
| `store/editorStore.ts` | Zustand. `byWorkspace[wsId] = { openFiles: OpenFile[], activePath }`. Each `OpenFile` carries `content` + `initialContent` (dirty check). |
| `store/settingsStore.ts` | Zustand + `settings://changed` subscription so the main window reacts to Settings writes in real-time. |
| `store/searchStore.ts` | Zustand. Streamed match buffer per workspace. |
| `store/coordinationStore.ts` *(agent collaboration Phase 2)* | Workspace-keyed Zustand projection of backend coordination status. One global `coordination://changed` subscription refreshes the affected workspace; loading, error, and module-level empty fallbacks are stable. Tile/REPL and intake surfaces derive live warning chips from active session ids while historical warnings remain backend records. |
| `store/uiStore.ts` | Zustand session state for shell visibility, center mode, contextual Knowledge, selected-card/card-step state, search/history/settings overlays, inline card expansion, and the Code preview's per-workspace browser mode. Workspace-keyed maps stay isolated; the legacy left-rail and terminal-pane state was removed with the retired shell. |
| `store/toastStore.ts` | Zustand + the `pushToast()` helper. Auto-dismiss for info kind (6 s). Phase 3 warning toasts may carry a bounded action callback used by `Queue it`. |
| `store/agentStore.ts` *(v0.3.0; Phase 3 immediate admission)* | Zustand. `byWorkspace[wsId] = { agents, runs, loading, error, selectedRunId, cwd }`. CRUD methods wrap the IPC layer. `startRun` consumes the tagged `started | busy` response; busy creates no optimistic run and exposes the queue callback. `applyRunUpdate` / `refreshRun` drive UI updates from `agent://run-*` events. |
| `store/tileStore.ts` *(v0.5.0)* | Zustand. `byWorkspace[wsId] = { tiles: Tile[], expandedTileId, detailTileId }`. A `Tile` is `{ id, kind: 'shell' \| 'claude' \| 'agent-run', ptyId, runId?, agentId?, label, cwd, startedAt, status, pendingClose, expanded }`. Subscribers wired by `startTileSubscriptions` (called once at app boot from `App.tsx`): `agent://run-started` → `addAgentRunTile`, `agent://run-done` → `setStatus` (and remove if `pendingClose`), `pty://exit` → `setStatus(killed)` then auto-remove. `closeTile` flips `pendingClose=true`, calls `agent_run_kill` for agent tiles or `pty_kill` for shell/claude tiles, lets the exit listener finalize removal. `addShellTile` / `addClaudeTile` create user-spawned tiles whose `ptyId` is recorded by Terminal's `onPtyId` callback after spawn. Helper `writeToTilePty(tileId, data)` writes to the tile's PTY (used by drop-injection). Tiles are session-only — no persistence. |

## IPC surface

Most commands live in `src-tauri/src/commands.rs` and its child modules (`card_pr_commands.rs`, `skills_hub_*.rs`, `ply_skill_ipc.rs`); newer domains have their own modules (`card_area.rs`, `deploy_map.rs`, `next_improvement.rs`). `lib.rs::generate_handler` is the registered list. Frontend wrappers in `src/ipc.ts` (typed).

### v0.1.0 — core

| Command | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `shell_env_info` | Returns cached `{path, shell, claudePath}`. |
| `pty_spawn(cmd, args, cwd, cols, rows)` → `id` | Spawns a PTY child. |
| `pty_write(id, data)` / `pty_resize(id, cols, rows)` / `pty_kill(id)` | PTY ops. |
| `pty_session_lightness(id)` *(1.168.1)* | `"light"` / `"dark"`: the light/dark a live Claude session Plyloft themed started in (`claude_theme::register` at spawn, forgotten at exit or kill), else null. Windows' "Restart it to use the new theme" line compares with it (`lib/themeRestart.ts`). |
| `pty_console_host()` *(1.169.0)* | Windows: `"bundled"` when this run's PTYs use the ConPTY Plyloft ships (`<install dir>\conpty\`: conpty.dll, `x64\` / `arm64\OpenConsole.exe`, NuGet Microsoft.Windows.Console.ConPTY pinned in `src-tauri/windows/conpty.lock.json`, fetched and hash-checked at build time by `scripts/fetch-conpty.mjs` from `build.rs`), `"system"` when `conpty_host::ensure` fell back to kernel32's; null on the Mac. Chosen once, before the first PTY: the DLL is loaded by its path after a trial console, so portable-pty's `LoadLibraryW("conpty.dll")` gets that module, or nothing (system ConPTY). The theme-restart line shows only on `"system"`. Each PTY's OpenConsole.exe is a child of Plyloft that ends when its pseudo console closes (session dropped, tile closed, quit) or when Plyloft's handles close (a crash). |
| `workspace_list` / `workspace_create` / `workspace_delete` / `workspace_set_active` / `workspace_rename` / `workspace_cwd_exists` / `workspace_set_files` | Workspace CRUD + state. |
| `fs_list_dir(path, includeHidden)` | Directory tree node. Async (QoL 6.1, off the main thread). `.git` and `.DS_Store` are never listed; other dot-names only with `includeHidden` (Settings › Code › Show hidden files, `editor.showHiddenFiles`). |
| `fs_read_text_file(path)` / `fs_write_text_file(path, content)` | Editor I/O with size + binary guards, atomic writes. The write registers with `SelfWriteTracker`. |
| `fs_reveal_in_workspace(workspace_id, path)` *(UX-8.5)* | `Reveal in Finder` on the page Code shows for a file it cannot open (binary, over 5 MB, not UTF-8). `files::reveal_target` resolves the path (symlinks followed) and accepts only a regular file inside the workspace's own directory; `open -R` the way `worktree_reveal` spawns it — no plugin, no new capability. |

### v0.2.0 — settings, watcher, search, files-order

| Command | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `settings_get` / `settings_set` | Settings CRUD; `settings_set` emits `settings://changed`. |
| `settings_reset` | 1.176.3: a reset from the window (Reset all, Reset this page); the paths it names go back whatever wrote them, the rest merges as `settings_set` does; returns `{ before, saved }` for the Undo. Emits `settings://changed`. |
| `reveal_log_dir` | `open` the log folder in Finder. |
| `program_folders_check(value)` | 1.176.1: reads a Program folders value (`program_folders.rs`) per entry and for the list, for the Settings row and Diagnostics. |
| `fs_watch_workspace` / `fs_unwatch_workspace` | File-watcher lifecycle. |
| `search_start` / `search_cancel` / `ripgrep_available` | Find-in-files. |
| `files_order_get` / `files_order_set` / `files_order_clear` | Custom file-tree order. |

### v0.3.0 — agents and browser pane

| Command | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `agent_list(workspaceId, projectCwd)` | Merge workspace-scoped + project-scoped agents. |
| `agent_get` | Reads one agent config. Project scope lives at `<cwd>/.atelier/agents/<id>.json`; workspace scope at `<config_dir>/agents/workspaces/<wsId>/<id>.json`. (GH #171: `agent_create` / `agent_update` / `agent_delete` went with Settings › Agents.) |
| `agent_run_start(agentId, scope, workspaceId, projectCwd, promptOverride?)` | Spawn an agent run with `AgentTrigger::Manual`. |
| `agent_run_list(agentId?, workspaceId?, limit?)` | Reads runs from `<config_dir>/agents/runs/*.json`. Live `bytesWritten` synced from in-memory counter. |
| `agent_run_get` / `agent_run_kill` | Run lifecycle. Kill marks status `killed` first, then SIGTERMs. |
| `agent_transcript_read(runId, offset?)` | Reads transcript file from disk. |
| `probe_localhost_ports` | TCP-probes 13 candidate ports (3000/3001/4173/4321/5173/5174/5000/8000/8080/8081/4200/1313/9000) with a 120 ms timeout each. |
| `probe_ports` | Try it (TI-3): TCP-probes a given list — a card copy's port block — on 127.0.0.1 then [::1], 120 ms connect each, off the main thread; ports below 1024 and repeats are dropped, at most 16 tried. |

### v0.4.0 — palette helpers + notifications

| Command | Purpose |
|---|---|
| *(retired, GH #171)* `cron_next_fires` · `glob_match` | The Agents form's cron preview and glob test; gone with it. |
| `notification_send_test` | Posts a benign test notification — used by Settings → Behavior → "Send test notification" button to trigger the macOS notification permission prompt under user control. |
| `notification_access` | QoL 1.168 (F4): Plyloft's notification authorization — `not-asked` / `allowed` / `denied` / `unknown` (`notification_access.rs`: `UNUserNotificationCenter` inside an app bundle only, Windows' `ToastEnabled`). Read by `NotificationAccessRow` (Setup's Your AI step, Settings › Claude and Codex). |
| `notification_request_access` | QoL 1.168 (D6): asks macOS for permission (its prompt appears once) and returns what it settled on; on Windows it only reads. |
| `notification_open_settings` | QoL 1.168: opens the system's notification settings (scheme-checked like `microphone_open_settings`). Rust's pull-request notifications also emit `notification://sent` `{workspaceId, cardId}` so a click on one lands on the card (`lib/notifyTarget.ts`). |

### v0.5.0 — workspace styling

| Command | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `workspace_set_accent_color(id, color)` | Sets `Workspace.accentColor` (`Option<String>`, hex like `"#7c3aed"`) and persists via `WorkspaceStore::save_atomic`. `color: null` clears the accent. Frontend caller is the workspace icon picker, opened from the switcher's ⋯ › Icon and colour. |

### v0.6.0 — Kanban

| Command | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `kanban_load(workspaceId)` | Reads `<config_dir>/kanban/workspaces/<wsId>.json`. Empty for a missing file; a newer Plyloft's is refused. 1.175.0: a file that can't be read opens from `<wsId>.json.bak` when that is a board with cards (the unreadable bytes are kept as `<wsId>.json.unreadable-<ms>`), else it is set aside and the board is empty; `board_unreadable_notice` says which, once. Round 4: a restore also writes `<wsId>.json.restored` (the backup's time; whether it was said and GitHub read), so a relaunch still holds a GitHub Issues board's writes until its first read; it is not a board, and is removed once nothing is owed. |

### v0.7.0 — Quick Open file walker

| Command | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `fs_list_files_recursive(cwd, includeHidden?)` → `{paths: string[], truncated: bool}` | Recursive walker (the `ignore` crate) capped at 10,000 files / depth 12. QoL 6.1: follows the project's `.gitignore` files (nested, `.git/info/exclude`, global excludes; also outside a git repo), skips the noisy dirs and card worktrees, and dot-names unless `includeHidden`. Async. Returns relative paths with forward-slash separators. Used by Go to file (⌘P): `PaletteScopes.tsx` caches it per workspace and walks again when `fs://changed` reports a created/removed file, when Show hidden files changes, or on Re-scan. |
| `fs_create_file` / `fs_create_dir(workspace_id, dir, name)` → `String` *(QoL 6.3)* | New file (empty, `create_new`) or folder in `dir`, never over an existing entry; `dir` must be the workspace folder or inside it (`files::dir_in_workspace`). Names are one segment (`files::valid_entry_name`). |
| `fs_rename_entry(workspace_id, path, new_name)` → `String` *(QoL 6.3)* | Rename in the same folder; refused over another entry (a change of case alone is allowed), on the workspace folder itself and on `.git`. The entry's folder is resolved, the entry itself is not (a link is renamed, not its target). |
| `fs_trash_entry(workspace_id, path)` → token; `fs_undo_trash(token)` → `String` *(QoL 6.3)* | Move to the Trash / Recycle Bin through `trash::trash_all`; the token (in-process only) lets Undo put it back with `trash::restore`, never over a name taken since. |
| `fs_reveal_entry(workspace_id, path)` *(QoL 6.3)* | Reveal a file **or folder** of the workspace (`files::reveal_entry_target`). |
| `fs_open_dropped` (raw body = the file's bytes; headers `x-ply-workspace-id`, `x-ply-file-name` percent-encoded, `x-ply-expected-len`, `x-ply-modified-ms`?) → `{path, inside}` *(QoL 6.4; raw since 1.172.2, D3)* | A file dropped on the editor: `files::locate_dropped` finds the same file in the workspace (name, size, bytes; modification time breaks a tie) → `inside: true`; else the bytes are kept as `<app_cache>/dropped/open-<uuid>/<name>` (`cache_retention` prunes the folder) and the editor opens it read-only (`ReadOnlyPlace` `outside`). `onDragDropEvent` is not used: with `dragDropEnabled: false` the runtime never installs the handler that emits it. |
| `fs_copy_into_workspace(workspace_id, source, name)` → `String` *(QoL 6.4)* | "Copy into the project": a copy `fs_open_dropped` kept (only under the app cache's `dropped/`) into the workspace folder, `name 2.ext`… when taken, tempfile + no-clobber rename. |

### v0.7.3 — external file drop materialization

| Command | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `fs_save_dropped_file` (raw body = the file's bytes; headers `x-ply-file-name` percent-encoded, `x-ply-expected-len`?) → `String` *(raw since 1.172.2, D3: no JSON number array; a JSON body is refused; off the main thread)* | Writes bytes from an external Finder-drop (browser File API doesn't expose `.path`) to `<app_cache>/dropped/<uuid>-<safe_name>`. Returns the absolute path so `Terminal.tsx` can shell-quote and paste it into the PTY. Sanitizes the basename to alphanumeric + `.`, `-`, `_`. GH #230: called through `lib/droppedFile.ts::saveDroppedFile`, which reads the `File` whole first (length = `File.size`, a PNG's chunk chain reaches `IEND`, a rejected read is incomplete; one re-read after 500 ms, then refused); bytes that arrive at any length but `expectedLen` are refused, and the write is a tempfile renamed into place. |

### v0.8.0 — Prompts library + custom templates

| Command | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `prompts_list` / `prompts_create` / `prompts_update` / `prompts_delete` | CRUD over `<config_dir>/prompts/library.json`. Flat global store. `prompts_create` assigns UUID + timestamps; `prompts_update` preserves `created_at`. |
| `templates_list` / `templates_create` / `templates_update` / `templates_delete` | CRUD over `<config_dir>/templates.json`. `UserTemplate { id, label, description, tiles: [{kind: shell|claude}], createdAt, updatedAt }`. Surfaced in the optional Terminals tile-layout picker alongside built-ins. |

### v0.9.0 — Diff reviewer

| Command | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `snapshot_get_changes(runId)` → `Option<ChangesManifest>` | Returns the persisted change manifest for a finished run, or `None` if the snapshot was never captured (legacy run, REPL run, or run still in flight). Frontend `RunChangesPanel` renders the changes inline using CodeMirror `@codemirror/merge`. |

### v0.10.0 — Command Blocks

| Command | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `shell_integration_script(shell)` → `String` | Returns the OSC 133 shell integration script for `"zsh"`, `"bash"` or `"powershell"` (pwsh 7 and Windows PowerShell 5.1). Surfaced in Settings → Editor and terminals → Command blocks with a copy-to-clipboard button. User pastes into `~/.zshrc`, `~/.bashrc` or the file `$PROFILE` names; once sourced + the setting toggled on, terminal panes track command boundaries via OSC 133;C / 133;D (`lib/commandBlocks.ts`). |

### v1.6.0 — Kanban run_history

| Command | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `kanban_clear_run_history(workspaceId, cardId)` | Wipes the `runHistory: Vec<RunHistoryEntry>` array on a single card. Atomic write through the existing `KanbanStore::cache` mutex. UI surfaces this behind a "Clear history…" button on the card's run-history accordion (More about this card), beside its "Record this card's runs" switch (1.172.0 D1; before, both were in the card's ⋯). Read access reuses `kanban_load` (cards already carry their `runHistory` field). |

`RunHistoryEntry` (in `kanban.rs`): `{ run_id, status: AgentStatus, ended_at: i64, files_changed_count: u32, top_paths: Vec<String> (≤10, each ≤200 chars middle-truncated), swarm_id: Option<String>, swarm_template_id: Option<String>, swarm_collisions: u32, agent_config_id: Option<String>, legs: u32 (>1 only for coordinator-mode aggregations) }`. Caps: 5 entries / card (FIFO eviction), 10 paths / entry, 4 KB / serialized entry (over-budget falls back to counts-only). Two new `KanbanCard` fields are additive with `#[serde(default)]`: `run_history` and `suppress_run_history: bool` (per-card opt-out).

Wire-up: `agents.rs` PTY exit listener appends an entry for oneshot runs (skipped when `swarm_id.is_some()` — the swarm path aggregates); `swarms::on_swarm_run_done` calls `append_kanban_history_for_swarm` once per `Running → terminal` transition with `legs = run_ids.len()`. Both paths are best-effort log-and-continue. See `docs/STATUS.md` v1.6.0 section for the full design rationale (split from `card.knowledge` to avoid the prompt-feedback loop in `kanbanStore.moveCard`).

### v1.61.0 — onboarding (Phase 5)

| Command | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `onboarding_take_welcome()` → `bool` | One-shot welcome flag. The `.setup` import drain writes a `welcome_pending` sentinel when `scripts/onboard.sh` pre-registered workspaces this launch; the first caller consumes (deletes) it and gets `true`, everyone after gets `false`. The frontend (`App.tsx` mount effect) uses it to fire a single welcome toast. |

(Brings the IPC surface to 165 commands.)

### v1.62.0 — branch-protection awareness (Phase 6)

| Command | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `codeowners_check(workspaceId, absPath)` → `{ flagged, owners }` | READ-ONLY: does editing `absPath` need a CODEOWNERS reviewer? Backend makes the path cwd-relative (outside-cwd ⇒ not flagged), reads `<cwd>/.github/CODEOWNERS`, returns the owners of the last matching rule (`codeowners.rs`, last-match-wins). Missing file ⇒ not flagged. Powers the editor ribbon. |

(Brings the IPC surface to 167 commands.)

### v1.65.0 — Inbox (UX revamp step 1)

| Command | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `inbox_feed()` → `InboxFeed` | READ-ONLY: the cross-workspace derived event feed (`inbox.rs`, docs/ux-revamp/STEP1.md). Reads every workspace's board through the managed `KanbanStore` (cold workspaces included), one mtime-windowed scan of `agents/runs/`, and TTL-cached `gh` review-requested PRs. Returns events (stable ids, `seenAt` joined) + the per-workspace 4-week cost rollup. |
| `inbox_mark_seen(eventIds)` → `usize` | Marks events seen for the current user (`identity.userEmail` \| `"local"`) in `inbox/seen.json`. seen ≠ resolved — rows dim; they leave the feed only when the underlying state changes. |
| `inbox_mark_all_seen(expectedSetSha256, expectedUserSha256)` → `InboxMarkAllSeenResult` | Recomputes through the same managed feed/cache, checks the displayed user/set and optional immutable process constraint, then atomically marks exactly the matching unseen set. Returns the pre-write proof and marked count. |

(Brings the IPC surface to 170 commands.)

### v1.77.0 — GH-backed card delete

| Command | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `kanban_card_delete_github(workspaceId, cardId)` | The "Close issue & delete" dialog action for GitHub-backed cards (the hard-delete `kanban_card_delete`'s v1.57 guard refuses). Closes the backing issue via `gh issue close`, then hard-deletes the card locally. Tolerates an already-closed or vanished issue (the "issue missing" badge case); any other close failure (offline, permissions) aborts with **no local mutation**. The delete sticks because `github_sync::reconcile_plan` (v1.77.0) no longer creates cards for closed issues with no local card — reopening the issue on GitHub is the recovery path that recreates the card. NOT propagated to teammates: their local card survives (reconciled to Complete). |

### GH #116 — a deleted card takes its Builder worktree with it

| Command | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `kanban_card_worktree_state(workspaceId, cardId)` | Read-only: the card's Builder worktree as a delete would see it — `disposition` (`keep` / `remove` / `ask`, from the pure `worktrees::worktree_disposition`), the folder, its branch, the dirty-file and local-only-commit counts, whether a Builder session is live, `unreadable`, `busy` (a Verify job or the card's linked agent run working in the folder, which refuses the delete), and the card's own `atelier/card-…` branch with its local-only commit count — what `everything` drops even when the folder proves no branch. Git-ignored files count as work in the dirty count; only the build caches `strip_caches` removes are discounted. The confirm sheet reads it before it opens; no destructive button is live until it lands. |
| `kanban_card_delete(workspaceId, cardId, worktree?)` | Gains the sheet's answer (`auto` / `keep` / `everything`) and returns a `CardDeleteOutcome`. After the row is gone it ends the card's live Builder session (`BuilderRegistry::end_session` — the app-exit PTY kill, Hard Rule #4), drops its verify logs, and takes the folder off its path and drops its `atelier/card-…` branch when that loses nothing (`auto`), or on an explicit `everything`. A removal is an atomic rename into the `.trash/` of the worktree's root (`.plyloft-worktrees/`, or legacy `.atelier-worktrees/`) — never an outright delete, so no write can race it — and the 7-day GC frees it, except when something wrote to the staged copy after the delete judged it clean: an automatic removal marks its entry `<entry>.clean`, and at expiry the sweep compares the kept index with the tree (`diff-files`, `ls-files --others`, and an `--ignored --directory` pass minus the cache paths — none needs the HEAD a pruned entry has lost) and keeps a changed one, or one it cannot read. An automatic removal stages its entry already marked `<entry>.kept` — written before the rename, so the entry is never visible without it — and lifts that mark only once the staged copy reads exactly as the decision read it. Anything else keeps the entry, and its branch, for good: `worktree_kept_list` / `worktree_kept_delete` are how the Worktrees panel shows those entries and how the human removes one. `Delete everything`, the archive, the panel and Sync write no mark and expire as before. A failure is reported in the outcome, never raised: the card is already deleted. On a worker, holding `CARD_START_LOCK` across the card snapshot, the row removal and the cleanup — the same lock `builder_start` and `builder_resume` take, so no session can be spawned into a folder on its way out. |
| `kanban_card_delete_github(workspaceId, cardId, worktree?)` | Same tail after the issue close. Also on a worker now; the lock is taken after `gh issue close`, so no card start queues behind a network call, and the card is read again under it. |

### GH #66 — claim state on GitHub-backed cards

| Command | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `kanban_card_claim(workspaceId, cardId, confirmed?)` → `ClaimOutcome` | Changed: reads the issue's live assignees and stores them as `KanbanCard.gh_assignees`. Returns `{claimed, holder, card}`, where `holder` is `unclaimed` / `mine` / `others{logins}` before the call. When someone else holds the issue, it assigns `@me` only if `confirmed`. Runs on the blocking pool. |
| `kanban_card_claim_state(workspaceId, cardId)` → `ClaimOutcome` | Re-reads the live assignees and stores them on the card without assigning anyone, then emits `kanban://changed` when they changed. The frontend calls it before a start that creates a worktree, because the synced list can predate a claim made on another Mac. |
| `github_current_login(fresh?)` → `string \| null` | This Mac's GitHub login, for the card claim chip. Display only. It uses the 60-second `current_login` cache unless `fresh` is set, which Sync passes to bypass it. |

`advance_card_to_in_progress` (card drag, Run, Start build) now pushes
`status:in-progress` through `gh_sync_card_edit` after the worktree exists. A
failed push sets `sync_pending`. Its claim gate stores the live assignees and
names the other holder when it refuses. `builder_resume` pushes the same label
on a background thread. Reconcile treats `gh_assignees` as GitHub-owned and
refreshes it even on a `sync_pending` card (`ReconcileAction::SetAssignees`).

### N14 — teammates on GitHub-backed cards

| Command | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `kanban_card_linked_pull_requests(workspaceId, cardId)` → `LinkedPullRequest[]` | Item 4: the pull requests linked to the card's issue — GitHub's own closing list (`closedByPullRequestsReferences`, open, closed or merged: "Closes #21" and Development links), plus a link made by hand from the issue's last 25 connected/disconnected timeline events, deduped — from one bounded `gh api graphql` read, as the workspace's GitHub account. Same repository only; the card's own ship PR (`pr`, or the number in `pr_url`) is left out. `{number, url, state: open/closed/merged, draft, author}`. Not stored: the card page reads it on open and after each Sync. Team-gated like Claim. |
| `kanban_open_pr_links(workspaceId)` → `OpenPrLink[]` | U28: one `gh pr list` read for the Board: which issues an open pull request closes (`closingIssuesReferences`). Team-gated; display only and not stored. |

`KanbanCard.gh_author` (`ghAuthor`, item 6): who opened the backing issue,
GitHub-owned like `gh_assignees`. `gh issue list/view --json` now asks for
`author`; reconcile sets it on create and patches it (also on a
`sync_pending` card, through `SetAssignees`), and Claim records it from its
own issue read. An answer without an author never clears it. Additive
`#[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]`, no schema bump.

| `activity_feed()` | v1.80.0 (Part 2 P2-2): the Activity view — telemetry's single disclosure home. Cross-workspace runs/swarms/ships/verify + 4-week cost, windowed 28 days, capped at 200 rows (`truncated` flag). Pure view in `inbox.rs::assemble_activity`; absorbs the retired SwarmTab run history. No seen markers — history, not notifications. |

(IPC surface as of v1.86.0: **183** registered commands — +`card_overlap_check` (v1.86.0) — +`license_get`/`license_set`/`license_clear` (v1.85.0) — 180 as of
v1.80.0, minus `github_review_requested` (v1.84.0: its only FE consumer
died with WorkspaceDetailPane; the Inbox feed reaches
`github::pr_list_review_requested` directly in Rust). The count is
measured from `lib.rs::generate_handler`.)

### 1.158.0 — Plyloft account, plans and team billing (launch A1–A3, G1–G4, T1–T3)

Contract with the server: [LAUNCH_API](plans/LAUNCH_API.md). Errors from the
account and billing calls are `AccountError {code, message}`.

| Command | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `account_get()` → `AccountInfo` | The account as this profile has it (signed in, login, plan, team repo/role, `exp`, period end, status, `cancelAtPeriodEnd`, comp, device, last checked, offline, `lastKnownPlan`, `team {state, device, teamRepo, role}`). No keychain, no network. |
| `account_sign_in_start()` → `SignInStart` | Starts GitHub's Device Flow (no scopes) and returns the code and URL. Polling runs on its own thread and reports on `account://sign-in`. |
| `account_sign_in_cancel()` | Stops a running sign-in. |
| `account_refresh()` → `AccountInfo` | `GET /entitlement` now (Settings › Plan's Check now). |
| `account_use_team()` → `AccountInfo` | 1.178.0: **Use Team on this computer** — `POST /session/use-team`; the computer that had Team stays signed in, on Free. Typed errors `no_team`, `rate_limited`, `network`, `unauthorized` (signed out: no request). |
| `account_sign_out()` → `AccountInfo` | `DELETE /session` when reachable, then removes the keychain entry and the entitlement. |
| `account_dev_skip_sign_in()` → `bool` | Debug builds: `PLYLOFT_DEV_SKIP_SIGNIN=1` answers true, for the walks. Release builds always answer false. |
| `account_dev_load_entitlement(token)` | Debug builds only (compiled out of release, with its registration): loads a `PLY2-` token minted with the dev keypair, for the walks. |
| `account_checkout(plan, interval, seats?)` | `POST /checkout`, then opens Stripe Checkout in the browser (Create team; `seats` 2–50). Team only since 1.178.0: any other plan is refused. `already_subscribed` opens the Portal. |
| `account_portal()` | `POST /portal`, then opens the Customer Portal (Manage subscription, Add seats). |
| `plan_gates_get()` → `PlanGates` | Every plan decision at once: plan, team access (`TeamAccess`, including `signed_out`, `team_elsewhere`, `team_to_choose`) and its refusal. No keychain, network or subprocess. |
| `team_billing_get()` → `TeamBilling` | Where Create team stands (`create_stage`) and the server's team (seats, holders, a lapse). No request when signed out. |
| `team_checkout_wait()` → `paid` / `pending` / `cancelled` · `team_checkout_wait_cancel()` | After Checkout, polls the plan for up to two minutes. |
| `team_bind_current()` | "Finish creating your team": binds this Mac's team repository to the paid plan (`POST /team`). |
| `team_seat_give(login)` · `team_seat_free(sub)` | Owner-only. Gives a seat to a listed member who holds none, or frees a seat held by someone no longer in the team. Nothing changes on GitHub. |
| `team_member_ids()` | The members' GitHub ids, so Members matches seats by id, never by login. |
| `team_member_states()` | N14 items 1 and 9: Joined, Invited (with expiry) or No access on the team repo and team workspaces, as the team's account. Owner-only. |
| `team_member_remove(login)` | N14 item 3: removes GitHub access on every team repository, then the manifest entry, then frees the seat. Owner-only. |
| `card_review_followup_message(rejections)` | G4: the words a follow-up swarm used to get, sent instead to the card's Builder session. |
| `intake_ended_cards()` → `{ended, live}` | Walk fix F14: intakes whose conversation ended while the card still reads as planning, and the live ones. |

Removed: `license_set`, `license_clear` (the Licence page and the "Paste a team
key" path are gone). `license_get` remains, for Diagnostics.

### QoL block 14 — Team and pull requests

| Command | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `kanban_github_refresh(workspaceId)` → `ReconcileSummary` | 14.1 (C19): the Board's periodic refresh of a GitHub-backed board, read-only with one exception since 1.175.0: on a board that was opened from its last saved copy, the first read that goes through is followed by the one-shot writes that were refused while the board was held — this computer's claim taken off an issue, a card's outcome comment (`github_sync::refresh_then_send_held` → `send_held_writes`; nothing else, and nothing on any other board). It runs every 3 min while the Board shows and on focus (`lib/boardFreshness.ts`). `github_sync::refresh`: one read of the issues (`read_issues`, 1.175.0: the open ones, the closed ones when a card needs them, and up to 10 by number when a list was cut), `reconcile_plan` applied under the board pass lock without its `write_back` — no push, labels, skill-issue tidy or marker write-back (a card whose notes already are the unmarked body is `already_adopted`, so the next Sync writes the markers); no definition read, fetch, prune or trash sweep (those stay with `kanban_github_reconcile`). Team plan gated, in the workspace's GitHub account; `kanban://changed` only when a card changed. Also asked by the window, without the Board on screen, for a restored GitHub Issues board until its first read goes through (`kanbanStore.ts::readRestoredBoard`). |
| `team_workspace_list_remove(repo)` → `bool` | 14.4 (G25): the inverse of `team_workspace_list_add` — `team_admin::manifest_without_workspace` published as the same one-file `team.json` commit to the team repository, owner-gated with a fresh identity. Members' GitHub access, clones and workspaces stay. |

`GhPr` (review-requested pull requests) carries `created_at`, so the Inbox's
`pr_review` row has its time. The Inbox row's Review it with the Challenger
opens the Board's sheet through `boardStore.prReviewAsk`; Give everyone access
(Members) reads `team_invite_plan` per member and sends `team_invite_apply`
per member with its digest — no new team command.

### N14 F8 — Put back in To do

| Command | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `kanban_card_put_back(workspaceId, cardId)` → `KanbanCard` | The card ⋯'s **Put back in To do**, for a card In progress or waiting to ship that has not landed (`put_back.rs`). On a worker under `CARD_START_LOCK`, one step: refuses an archived, landed or To do card, an open pull request ("This card has an open pull request — merge or close it first, or archive the card."; the PR is never touched), on a GitHub Issues board a card with no worktree on this Mac, and work it can't stop (Verify, an agent run, a setup, a swarm, a Challenger review — `put_back_busy`); then ends the Builder session and, while both build it, both lanes (Stop both's set-aside of Codex's build), ends a live intake (`cancel_intake_recorded`), and writes the card To do with no run, checks, review or session recorded. The branch, the worktree, the snapshots and the review rounds stay; `worktrees::reuse_card_worktree` makes the next Start build continue in that worktree (whatever the title says now); a recorded worktree that is gone gets its branch checked out again where the card's worktree lives (`worktrees::reattach_card_branch`, set up as new), and only with no branch left is it made afresh — the old handle cleared first, so the card never records another path or branch than the Builder's. The brief opens with `CONTINUES_KEPT_WORK` only when the recorded branch is the one started on. Also refused: Complete with no worktree, and a Build with both comparison under way. A live intake is ended whenever the intake registry holds the card; a write that fails after stopping says the sessions were stopped. A closed pull request's `pr_url` stays on a put-back card. Resume on a To do card is refused with `RESUME_IN_TODO` ("This card is in To do. Start build picks it up again from its branch."). A To do card is not active (`plan_gates::card_is_active`), so on Free the slot is free. The team gate is a column move's; on a GitHub board the label is pushed. |

### Challenger on a pull request

| Command | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `pr_review_list(workspaceId)` → `PrList` | The workspace's GitHub repository (from origin) and its open pull requests (`gh pr list --state open --limit 50`, as the workspace's GitHub account), each with whether it is the member's and the card that already reviews it. Read-only. |
| `pr_review_create(workspaceId, input, challenger)` → `PrReviewCreated` | Makes the card "Review: <title>" for pull request `input` (a link or a number of this repository) and starts the review by `challenger` (`claude` / `codex`); `existing` when a card already reviews it (returned unchanged); `reviewError` when the card was made but the review didn't start. Refusals: no GitHub origin, another repository, merged or closed, the Free slot, a read-only GitHub board. |
| `pr_review_refresh(workspaceId, cardId)` → `KanbanCard` | Reads the card's pull request again (head, state, title); a failed read is kept on the card as `prReview.readError`. |
| `pr_review_start(workspaceId, cardId)` → `KanbanCard` | Review / Review again: the pull request's current head, on the card's branch brought there, after asking the Free slot under `CARD_START_LOCK`; refused on a merged/closed pull request, on a head already reviewed with a verdict, and when an unpushed fix has new commits on top (Bring in the new commits first). |
| `pr_review_merge_head(workspaceId, cardId)` → `KanbanCard` | Bring in the new commits: the member's own pull request's new commits merged into the card's branch under an unpushed fix (a merge commit; a conflict is aborted and said); the head they came with becomes the one the fix is built on. Refused while a Builder session is open, on anyone else's pull request and on a rewrite. |

### N19 — Follow up a shipped card

| Command | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `kanban_card_follow_up(workspaceId, originalCardId, cardId, title, notes)` → `KanbanCard` | **Follow up** on a card that has landed (shipped, or its pull request merged). The normal new-card path — `team_gate_github_card_write`, provenance stamped, To do (no Free slot), an issue on a GitHub Issues board (`gh_sync_card_create`) — plus `KanbanStore::create_follow_up`, which, in one write under the board lock, refuses a card Follow up may not start from (`kanban::follow_up_allowed`: landed, or a GitHub card in Complete — a done issue; else `FOLLOW_UP_NOT_SHIPPED`) or a duplicate id, sets the new card's `followsUp` and appends to the original's `followedUpBy` (`CardFollowLink { cardId, title, at }`, local only, never synced). Nothing else on the original changes, `updatedAt` included. The title and brief come from `lib/followUp.ts::followUpDraft`; the new card has no branch, so its start cuts a new one from the target. `ensure_follow_up_card` (Ship with follow-up) records the same link through `KanbanStore::link_follow_up` (idempotent, no landed check). |

### Agent collaboration Phases 2–3 — coordination

| Command | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `coordination_status(workspaceId)` | Read-only active/recent session and live-warning projection. |
| `coordination_handoff_recent(workspaceId, limit)` | Read-only bounded mechanically derived handoff history. |
| `agent_run_request(...)` | Immediate oneshot admission with a tagged `started` or typed `busy` result. |
| `agent_run_queue(...)` | Explicitly enqueue the same manual intent after the user chooses `Queue it`. |

### Agent collaboration Phase 4 — independent reviewers

| Command | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `reviewer_capability_probe(provider)` | Explicit quota-bearing behavioral write-denial probe for the current Claude/Codex executable and version. |
| `reviewer_run(workspaceId, cardId, provider)` | Run a proved reviewer against a pinned detached worktree and return its revision-bound artifact. |
| `reviewer_list(workspaceId, cardId)` | Read prior review records with live branch-drift validity. |
| `builder_review_again(workspaceId, cardId)` → snapshot n | GH #58: re-run the Challenger on the current build snapshot in smaller passes when its review is incomplete, verdict-less or failed. Refuses a running review, a changes-requested verdict (address or override it) and a worktree past the snapshot. |
| `builder_verdict_delivered(workspaceId, cardId, snapshotN, at)` | GH #135: the Builder received the verdict of the review that settled at `at` on `snapshotN`, so `BuilderState.undeliveredVerdict` is cleared. The card records that field when a review settles, so a verdict is not lost when the Run step is unmounted; a stale delivery (a newer review settled since) clears nothing, and clearing nothing is not an error. |

### Agent collaboration Phase 5 — independent discussions

| Command | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `discussion_prepare(workspaceId, cardId?, problem, synthesizerProvider)` | Resolve exact Git input, capture excluded dirty paths, verify current provider capability, seal and atomically persist the discussion before any paid leg. |
| `discussion_launch(discussionId)` | Open the prepared five-leg pipeline and schedule its process-owned worker. |
| `discussion_list(workspaceId)` | Read durable discussion views with live revision-staleness and derived pause/provider state. |
| `discussion_resume(discussionId)` | Resume only incomplete legs from a paused or startup-interrupted record. |
| `discussion_retry(discussionId, leg)` | Retry exactly one failed leg, then continue only incomplete dependencies. |
| `discussion_abort(discussionId)` | Persist terminal abort, then kill any active managed collectors owned by the discussion. |

### Improvement proposals (GH #104, ADR 0005)

| Command | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `improvement_proposal_state(workspaceId)` | This workspace's proposal, or `null`. The **one** reader: the 30-day and target-changed rules are applied here, so the block, the Inbox row and the Ship line cannot disagree. `canApprove` is false for a read-only collaborator, for a workspace with no shared definition, and for a words-only proposal. |
| `improvement_proposal_trust_stopped(workspaceId)` | The card whose last proposal pass stopped at Claude's trust question, or `null` (a file read). That card shows the trust step where its proposal would appear. |
| `improvement_proposal_dismiss(workspaceId)` | `Dismiss`: forget it. The card stays remembered, so no second pass runs for it. |
| `improvement_proposal_approve(workspaceId)` | `Approve and publish`: the change goes onto the integration branch through the shared channel's existing paths (a skill as any skill update; an instruction file never overriding, per GH #101), then the proposal is forgotten. Nothing writes the checkout. |
| `builder_note_correction(workspaceId, cardId)` | The human typed a correction into the Builder session after its first answer — the friction rule's third condition, counted by the window because the Builder is the vendor's own TUI in a PTY and Ply keeps no transcript of it. |

### Agent collaboration Phase 1C — instruction convention (GH #101: now the Library's Instructions page)

| Command | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `library_instructions_view(workspaceId)` → `InstructionsView` | Read the Instructions page (GH #101; replaced Phase 1C's `instruction_files_check`). Read-only, on the blocking pool: a shared workspace may build its mirror first; the fixed-root `AGENTS.md` / `CLAUDE.md` diagnosis is `instruction_files::inspect`. |
| `library_instructions_set_up(workspaceId, step)` → `bool` | Run the page's one step (replaced `instruction_files_apply`). A shared workspace publishes both files in one commit through the library channel and writes nothing to the checkout (`true`); a workspace without a shared definition writes the checkout, fingerprint-gated (`false`). |

### Direction C Part 3 P3-0A — card Ship preflight

| Command | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `card_ship_preflight(workspaceId, cardId)` → `TargetDirtyState?` | Free-tier, read-only inspection of the target workspace checkout. An observed clean checkout returns `{totalCount: 0, paths: [], truncated: false}`; dirty returns the exact total plus at most 100 display paths; unavailable Git state returns `null`. `land_card_local` repeats the strict check and emits the same typed payload before mutation. |

### Direction C Part 3 installed self-check SC-1/SC-2

| Command | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `installed_self_check_intake_authority(cardId, observedPtyId)` → `IntakeAuthorityCheckpoint` | Read-only comparison of the observed opaque PTY id with both the card→PTY IntakeRegistry mapping and live PtyRegistry ownership. |
| `installed_self_check_write_report(draft)` → `InstalledSelfCheckReportReceipt` | Validates one exact typed P3-1 observation draft, enforces its workspace plus Intake-card scope, fingerprints the containing installed bundle off-thread, recomputes status, and atomically writes to the fixed app-log directory. Accepts no destination/bundle path or authoritative report field. |

(IPC surface as of Part 3 self-check SC-2: **204** registered commands,
measured from `lib.rs::generate_handler`. As of v1.109.0: **226** — the
Codex tile cycles, the six `builder_*` commands and the two
`card_plan_challenge_*` commands.)

### #47 — dictation (Codex and shell tiles)

| Command | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `dictation_status()` → `DictationStatus` | Resolved `whisper-cli` path and each model's file state (present, size, downloading). |
| `dictation_transcribe(wav, requestId)` → `string` | Transcribe a 16 kHz mono WAV with the model and language in Settings; rejects with `{kind, message}` (`whisper-missing`, `model-missing`, `timeout`, `cancelled`, …). |
| `dictation_cancel(requestId)` → `bool` | Kill that request's whisper-cli group. |
| `dictation_model_download(model)` → `DictationStatus` | Download a model; emits `dictation://download-progress` `{model, received, total}`. |
| `dictation_model_download_cancel(model)` → `bool` | Kill the download. |
| `pty_prompt_idle(id)` → `bool?` | Whether the PTY's own child holds the terminal foreground with its line editor active (shell editing its prompt). |
| `pty_prompt_state(id)` → `"running" \| "editing" \| "canonical"?` | GH #70, the shell tile header: a job holds the foreground; the shell's line editor has the prompt; or the shell holds the foreground with canonical input on (a builtin `read`, or a shell with no line editor — indistinguishable, so the header lets output quiet decide). `null` when the PTY cannot answer at all. |

### Card chat — part 1 (backend; the Chat tab is part 2: `ChatPanel`, wrappers in `ipc.ts`)

| Command | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `chat_open(workspaceId, cardId, vendor)` → `ChatSnapshot` | Makes `claude` or `codex` the card's active chat vendor and returns that conversation (`{vendor, status, reason?, attention?, messages, usage, attachments, ptyId?}`). Starts nothing; suspends a live session of the other vendor. |
| `chat_messages(workspaceId, cardId, vendor?)` → `ChatSnapshot` | The conversation from Plyloft's own `chat.json` (default: the active vendor). |
| `chat_send(workspaceId, cardId, text, attachmentIds?)` → `ChatMessage` | Sends to the active vendor, starting or resuming its session; refused while it answers. |
| `chat_interrupt(workspaceId, cardId)` | Stop: Esc to Claude, `turn/interrupt` to Codex. |
| `chat_screen(workspaceId, cardId)` → `{ptyId, formatted, offset, rows, cols}?` | "Show terminal"'s first paint: the live Claude session's PTY id, its screen and terminal modes as escape sequences that redraw it, the `pty://data` offset they stand at and the grid's size; then follow `pty://data` (interactive: the member's keys go to `pty_write`). |
| `chat_close(workspaceId, cardId)` | Ends the session; the history stays and the next send resumes it. |
| `chat_attachment_add` (raw body = the file's bytes; headers `x-ply-workspace-id`, `x-ply-card-id`, `x-ply-file-name` percent-encoded) → `Attachment` | Checks kind (extension + signature) and size, stores it atomically, extracts a PDF's / .docx's text. |
| `chat_attachment_list(workspaceId, cardId)` → `Attachment[]` | The card's attachments. |
| `chat_attachment_remove(workspaceId, cardId, attachmentId)` | Removes the record and its files. |

### Plan with two AIs — part 1 (backend; `docs/plans/PLAN_WITH_TWO_AIS.md`)

| Command | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `plan_exchange_start(workspaceId, cardId, preferred?, ask?, attachmentIds?)` → `PlanExchangeSnapshot` | On a worker, under `CARD_START_LOCK`: the drain guard, `start_refusal`, the offer rule (To do, no Builder, not archived, no intake), P2, P3, the line-up from `doctor::vendor_ready`, room for two; ends the card's idle Chat session and starts the proposer. |
| `plan_exchange_get(workspaceId, cardId)` → `{current?, earlier[], readOnly, runningElsewhere?, lanes[], waitingForPrompt}` | The card's exchanges from `plan.json`. |
| `plan_exchange_stop(workspaceId, cardId)` | Interrupts the turn → `paused{stopped}`. |
| `plan_exchange_retry(workspaceId, cardId)` | Drain guard, `start_refusal`, P2, room for two; the paused turn again (`retry`, `reseed` or `full`). |
| `plan_exchange_more(workspaceId, cardId, note?)` | Another round (refused at 3). |
| `plan_exchange_accept(workspaceId, cardId, editedText?)` → `KanbanCard` | `apply_two_ai_plan` + the chat files copied into the card's attachments; both sessions end. |
| `plan_exchange_discard(workspaceId, cardId)` | Ends both sessions; writes nothing to the card. |
| `plan_exchange_screen(workspaceId, cardId)` → `ChatScreenSnapshot?` | The view-only paint of the exchange's Claude session. |
| `plan_exchange_open_cards()` → `PlanOpenCard[]` | Every workspace's cards whose latest exchange runs, waits for the member or is paused; the first call after a launch reads each card's `plan.json`, later ones answer from memory (PLAN_WITH_TWO_AIS §2, *Outside the Chat*). |

Events: `plan://state` (the current exchange without its messages, the lanes' status, tokens and PTY ids), `plan://messages` (`{workspaceId, cardId, exchangeId, role, vendor, messages}`).

### Terminals block — tile names, Resume…, History › Sessions (unreleased)

| Command | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `terminal_tile_title(ptyId)` → `TileTitle?` | A live tile's title from the CLI's files (following a `/clear`, finding a Codex tile's session): `{rowId, title, titleSource, cliSessionId?, followed, conversationNamed}`. |
| `terminal_tile_rename(ptyId, title)` / `terminal_tile_auto_name(ptyId)` | The member's name, kept over every later title / dropped again. |
| `terminal_tile_name_passed(ptyId, name)` | The member's name was typed into the CLI as `/rename` and submitted (`lib/cliRename.ts`: the Enter its own write 400 ms later): the row's `passedName`. |
| `tile_screen_block(text)` → `{kind, resetsAt?}?` | What a tile's drawn screen waits on (trust, bypass, usage limit). |
| `terminal_conversations(workspaceId)` → `Conversation[]` | Resume…: the project folder's Claude and Codex conversations (≤ 50, newest first; title, `updatedAt`, `live`, `livePtyId?`, `opening?` — QoL 1.168: the first message's ≤ 60-character title when the name shown is another). |
| `terminal_resume_check(kind, cliSessionId, cwd)` → `ResumeCheck` | `ready` \| `inTile {workspaceId, ptyId}` \| `elsewhere` \| `folderGone` \| `conversationGone`. |
| `terminal_sessions_history_all()` → `{sessions, unreadable}` | History › Sessions: every workspace's rows with `workspaceId`, `resumable` and `gone?` (`folder` \| `conversation`). |
| `terminal_reopen_offers()` → `{[workspaceId]: ListedSession[]}` | QoL 1.168 (D11): per workspace, the rows open at the last quit (`terminal_sessions::mark_quit` at `run_exit_cleanup`, before any PTY is killed) with a conversation, in opening order. Read once at launch (`store/reopenStore.ts`). |
| `terminal_reopen_dismiss(workspaceId)` | The offer was taken or put away: every `openAtQuit` of the workspace cleared. |
| `terminal_link_target(workspaceId, cwd, path)` → `string?` | QoL 1.168 (D6): a path printed in a tile, as Code opens it — a file inside the workspace after links are followed (`files::terminal_link_target`), spelled under the workspace folder; else null. |

### Events emitted by the backend

- `chat://state` — `{workspaceId, cardId, vendor, status idle|starting|working|ready|stopped, reason?, attention?, usage, ptyId?}` — a card chat's state (part 1).
- `chat://messages` — `{workspaceId, cardId, vendor, messages}` — added or changed chat messages, by `id` (streamed Codex text at most every 150 ms).
- `pty://data` — `{id, data}` — every chunk read from a PTY's stdout.
- `pty://exit` — `{id, code}`.
- `fs://changed` — `{workspaceId, path, kind}` — file watcher.
- `search://result` / `search://done` — streaming search.
- `settings://changed` — full Settings payload broadcast.
- `window://drop` — `{paths, position, windowLabel}` — native file drops.
- `agent://run-started` — `{runId, agentId, workspaceId, trigger}` — `trigger` ∈ `manual` / `swarm` (`scheduled` / `event` appear only on old run records, GH #171).
- `agent://output` — `{runId, agentId, data, offset}` — agent transcript chunk; `offset` is monotonic per run for replay-deduping.
- `agent://run-done` — `{runId, agentId, status, exitCode, endedAt}`.
- `discussion://changed` — `{discussionId}` after a background discussion
  worker exits; consumers reload the atomic record for authoritative state.
- `account://changed` — `AccountInfo` (1.158.0) after any refresh, sign-in,
  sign-out, Use Team, a silent reclaim or `unauthorized`.
- `account://sign-in` — `{attempt, …SignInProgress}` while a Device Flow sign-in
  runs.
- `improvement-proposal://changed` — `{workspaceId}` (GH #104) after the
  Challenger's proposal pass wrote one; consumers re-read
  `improvement_proposal_state` rather than deriving it. Replaced the retired
  `knowledge://proposal-updated`.

## Data flow

### Workspace creation
User picks Add a project… in the sidebar's switcher (or ⌘K, or the zero-project screen) → the Add a project sheet (`AddProjectSheet`, QoL 7.2) opens with Setup step 8's ways (a folder, from GitHub, new) → `useWorkspaces.create(cwd)` → `invoke("workspace_create", ...)` → Rust generates a UUID and atomically writes `workspaces.json` → the store activates the workspace → `App.tsx` adds it to `warmed` → `NewWorkspaceShell` stages the warmed hosts. Terminals are created only when the user chooses a tile action or template; no process is resurrected from a session record.

### Manual agent run
User picks `Run agent: <name>` in the command palette → `useAgents.startRun` → `agent_run_request`
IPC. `AgentRegistry::start_run` validates pre-flight, then
`PtyRegistry::spawn` asks the coordination owner for exclusive admission
before child creation. A free worktree creates the run/PTY lifecycle and emits
the existing `agent://run-*` events. A busy worktree returns a tagged conflict
without a run; the warning toast offers `Queue it`, which invokes
`agent_run_queue`. The queued closure revalidates state and creates no run,
snapshot, transcript, or session until a later pump acquires admission.

### Scheduled and triggered agents — retired (GH #171)
Scheduled (cron), file-changed and agent-finished agents no longer run. The
30 s thread that fired them is now `agents::start_runtime_tick` and does only
the swarm wall-time check and the App Nap release; `start_event_listeners`
(the `fs://changed` and `agent://run-done` listeners that started triggered
runs), `parse_cron`, `matches_glob`, the cooldown map and the Inbox's
`Scheduled` rows are gone. An old config's `schedule` / `triggers` still load
and are never acted on (`agents::tests::scheduled_and_triggered_agent_never_starts_a_run_on_its_own`).

### Reconciliation on launch
`AgentRegistry::new` → scans `<config_dir>/agents/runs/*.json` → any record stuck in `pending`/`running` is rewritten to `killed` with `endedAt: now`, `error: "process orphaned by app exit"`, and a marker line appended to the transcript. Survives force-quits / SIGKILL.

### Tile lifecycle (v0.5.0)
Three paths add a tile to `tileStore`:
1. **User spawns shell or claude** — `AppToolbar`'s `+ shell` / `+ claude` button (or `Cmd-T` / `Cmd-Shift-T` / Cmd-K palette) → `tileStore.addShellTile(wsId, cwd)` / `addClaudeTile(wsId, cwd)` creates a `Tile` with `ptyId: null`, status `running`. `TileGrid` mounts a `Tile` for it; `Tile`'s body mounts a `Terminal`, which spawns its PTY and reports the id back via `onPtyId` → `tileStore.setPtyId`.
2. **Agent run starts** — `agent://run-started` listener (registered by `startTileSubscriptions` at App boot) → resolves the run record + agent name + workspace cwd → `addAgentRunTile(run, agentName, cwd)`. The tile carries the run's `ptyId` directly so Tile actions (kill, write) work without the resolution dance.
3. **Tile already exists** — `addAgentRunTile` is idempotent against `runId` (returns the existing tile if found), so the listener can fire again without duplicating tiles.

Closing a tile: `closeTile(tileId)` flips `pendingClose=true`, calls `agent_run_kill` for agent-run tiles in `running` state (or `pty_kill` for shell/claude / non-running agent tiles), then waits for the matching exit event to finalize removal. The removal is gated on `pendingClose` so the listener doesn't reap tiles the user didn't ask to close (e.g., a user typing `exit` in a shell tile flips status to `killed` and auto-removes after a 1.5s status-pill display delay; an agent finishing on its own flips status to `done` and stays until the user closes it).

### Knowledge (GH #95)
Knowledge is a centre mode. Like Code it is a **sticky host**: `NewWorkspaceShell` mounts one per warmed workspace the first time that workspace shows it, then only CSS-hides it (`.center-mode-sticky`, `visibility`), so an unsaved note draft and CodeMirror's undo history survive mode flips and workspace switches; a workspace never opened in that mode mounts nothing. Because a hidden host can hold a `.modal-backdrop` (an `EditorPanel` confirm), the ⌘K/⌘P/Esc guards use `lib/visibleModal.ts::visibleModalOpen` — a backdrop blocks only with a client rect and a computed `visibility` other than `hidden`. ⌘⇧E, the sidebar entry under the modes and the palette's **Open Knowledge** call `openCenterMode(ws, "knowledge")`. It has no ⌘⌥ digit; ⌘⌥1–5 are unchanged.

### Shell visibility
The navigation sidebar and mode-switched center are the current shell. `gridVisible` is forced on by `openCenterMode`; there is no right column. The retired `AppToolbar.safeToggle` three-independent-column model is no longer product navigation. `react-resizable-panels` still scopes saved sizes by the effective visibility tuple.

│   │                                                at; cleared once offered, put away or resumed),
│   │                                                account? (1.179.0: the Claude account it ran on;
│   │                                                absent = Main, not written while absent) }] }.

Note on v0.5.0 tiles: the `Tile` runtime objects are **session-only** — held in `tileStore` zustand state, not persisted. Tiles die on app quit by design, just like terminal panes did in v0.4.0. PTY processes are killed via `RunEvent::ExitRequested` regardless.

All writes are atomic (tempfile + rename within the same parent dir). Schema additions stay backward-compatible via `serde(default)`. Both the App-Support `agents/` directory and per-project `.atelier/` are excluded from the file-watcher (the former via `add_excluded`, the latter via `NOISY_NAMES`).

## Key UX decisions

- **Resizable panel layout**, not draggable rearranging. `react-resizable-panels` uses shell-visibility-scoped `autoSaveId` values for size persistence.
- **Workspaces "warm" lazily.** First activation stages its `NewWorkspaceShell` hosts; subsequent visits are instant. Quit kills all warmed PTYs.
- **Terminal tiles don't unmount on mode/workspace switch.** Warmed tile-grid hosts stay mounted and use `visibility: hidden`; PTYs keep running in the background.
- **Agent runs survive workspace switches** the same way — the agent store updates per-workspace slices independently of which slice is currently rendered.
- **Cmd-W never hides the window.** It's swallowed at the React layer when no editor tab is open, so power users can't accidentally lose their session.
- **Editor is single-document at a time.** One CodeMirror EditorView per Code host lifetime; switching tabs swaps the document via Compartment reconfiguration. Its ref-owned container stays rendered so the EditorView never loses its DOM host.
- **Agent kill semantics preserve `killed` over `error`.** When the user clicks kill, `kill_run` sets status to `killed` and persists FIRST, then SIGTERMs the PTY. The exit listener reads the existing record, sees `killed`, and only updates `endedAt` / `exitCode` / `pty_id` — it does not flip back to `error` despite the non-zero exit.
- **Allowed-tools is informational in v0.3.0.** It's passed to `claude --allowedTools`, but Atelier doesn't sandbox the run further. Documented in the AgentForm UI.
- **Self-write suppression is editor-only.** Agent writes legitimately fire the external-write reload UX — that's the whole point.
- **(v0.5.0) Tiles unify three concepts.** Ad-hoc shell, ad-hoc claude, and in-flight agent run tiles all render through the same `Tile` component. The shape of a session — visual chrome, status pill, kill semantics, drop target — is identical across all three. The differences are limited to: the body component (`Terminal` vs `AgentRunView compact`), and the kill path (`pty_kill` vs `agent_run_kill`).
- **Navigation owns the shell.** The sidebar selects global/scoped modes; the center owns the chosen workflow; the right side is contextual Knowledge only. The retired AppToolbar and free-standing right-tab navigation do not compete with this hierarchy.
- **One workspace shell.** `experimentalLayout` is ignored when decoding old settings (GH #175 removed the field) and never changes runtime UI. This prevents stale navigation, shortcuts, and commands from competing with the current product.

## Local dev

```bash
source $HOME/.cargo/env
npm install                  # first time only — also pulls puppeteer-core for Tier 1.5
npm run tauri dev            # Vite + cargo watch — fast iteration
./scripts/install-local.sh   # unsigned recovery/bootstrap; external interactive Terminal only
npm run tauri build          # release .app + .dmg only
cargo test --workspace       # complete Rust workspace
./scripts/ppvp.sh            # required eight-step post-production verification gate
```

Two iteration loops, two trade-offs:
- **`npm run tauri dev`** — instant Rust HMR, instant Vite HMR. Best for tight UI iteration. Doesn't catch macOS-launch-only bugs (Gatekeeper, app bundle plist), and dev React leaks errors to the console rather than crashing.
- **`./scripts/install-local.sh`** — external-Terminal-only recovery/bootstrap for an unsigned local build. It rebuilds, gracefully quits Plyloft, performs a staged verified replacement at `/Applications/Plyloft.app` with rollback, and observes one relaunch. Ordinary installed delivery uses the signed updater/release path; never launch this script from Plyloft or a Plyloft-owned child.

Build artifacts:
- `src-tauri/target/release/bundle/macos/Plyloft.app`
- `src-tauri/target/release/bundle/dmg/Plyloft_<version>_aarch64.dmg`

Verify before shipping a change touching PTYs or agents:

```
1. ./scripts/ppvp.sh                                 # Tier 1 + 1.5 — automated build + type + test + runtime smoke
2. ./scripts/install-local.sh OR npm run tauri dev   # actual UI exercise
3. Spawn a claude tile, confirm Max-plan auth (no API key prompt)
4. Create an agent and click ▶, confirm run completes `done`/exit-0
5. Cmd-Q, ensure no orphan claude/zsh processes (`pgrep claude`)
6. For new persisted-JSON fields, write a Tier 2 schema-migration test (see docs/PPVP.md)
7. For new zustand selectors, do NOT return fresh `[]` / `{}` literals — see docs/PATTERNS.md
```

For changes touching agent runs specifically, also exercise:

```
8. Plant an agent with cron */2 * * * * and a `**/*.md` glob trigger; wait a minute and touch a .md file; verify NO run starts (GH #171)
9. Run it from ⌘K › `Run agent:`; verify the manual run starts
10. Force-quit atelier with kill -9 mid-run; relaunch; verify reconciliation rewrites the run to `killed`
```

For changes touching the v0.5.0 tile grid or sidebar specifically:
```
11. Spawn 8+ tiles, observe grid auto-tiles to 4×2 / 3×3 / etc.; xterm canvases all visible and responsive
12. Click a tile's expand (⛶), confirm siblings hide via `display: none` (NOT unmount — PTYs must stay alive)
13. Press Esc, confirm expanded tile collapses back to grid; all sibling PTYs intact (typing in them resumes)
14. Drag a file from FilesPanel onto a tile body; absolute path is shell-quoted and pasted into the PTY
15. Toggle each of the three column toggles; confirm "at least one visible" lock prevents an all-hidden state
16. Load a settings fixture with `experimentalLayout: false`; confirm the current shell still mounts and all warmed PTYs survive workspace/mode switches
```

## Appendix: What changed v0.6.0 → v0.11.0 (data flow)

### Stream-json parser (v0.9.0 G1)

Oneshot agent runs spawned `claude -p X --output-format stream-json --verbose` until the `claude -p` follow-up to PR #374; a Claude oneshot run is now an interactive session collected through its transcript, and the parser reads the transcript's lines as they grow (they have the same `assistant` / `user` shapes; the model and per-message usage come from the messages, summed when Plyloft's result line lands). Codex runs are unchanged. The PTY data listener routes bytes through a `StreamParserState` per run:

1. Bytes arrive in arbitrary chunks → `feed(&[u8])` appends to a per-run line buffer.
2. Each complete `\n`-terminated line is parsed as JSON (`serde_json::from_slice`).
3. Event dispatch on `type` field:
   - `system` (subtype `init`) → captures `model`
   - `assistant` → walks `message.content[]`, extracts `text` blocks → returned as text bytes for the transcript
   - `user` → tool results, summarized as `[tool result returned]`
   - `result` → captures `usage` (`input_tokens`, `output_tokens`, `cache_creation_input_tokens`, `cache_read_input_tokens`) + `total_cost_usd`
4. Returned text bytes are written to `<run_id>.log` and emitted as `agent://output` (so AgentRunView still renders a clean transcript, not raw JSON).
5. Exit listener reads the parser state, applies usage/model/cost to the AgentRun JSON, persists.

Unparseable lines pass through verbatim (claude's startup banner, stderr).

### Snapshot lifecycle (v0.9.0 F1)

For oneshot runs only:

1. **Pre-spawn:** `SnapshotsStore::capture_before(runId, cwd)` walks cwd respecting `WALK_SKIP_NAMES` + dotfile exclusion + `MAX_FILE_BYTES` (100 KB) + `MAX_FILES` (500) + `MAX_DEPTH` (12). Copies qualifying files to `snapshots/<runId>/before/<rel-path>`. Best-effort: failure logs and the run continues.
2. **Post-exit:** `compute_changes(runId, cwd, ...)` re-walks cwd, diffs against `before/`, builds a `ChangesManifest { changes: Vec<FileChange { path, kind, before, after }> }` with `kind` ∈ `added | modified | removed`. Manifest written atomically to `snapshots/<runId>/changes.json`. The `before/` directory is then deleted — the manifest carries embedded content for the diff (bounded by diff size, not workspace size).
3. **Frontend read:** `RunChangesPanel.tsx` calls `snapshot_get_changes(runId)` and renders changes as a list. Click-to-expand shows side-by-side diff via `@codemirror/merge` (modified) or single-pane `EditorView` (added/removed).

### Card-driven prompt construction (v0.7.4 + v0.8.0 D2)

When a Kanban card with an attached agent moves Todo → In Progress:

```
promptOverride = [card.title, card.notes, card.knowledge]
  .filter(present)
  .join("\n\n");
```

Sent as `agent_run_start({ ..., promptOverride })`. The agent's stored `promptTemplate` is intentionally bypassed for card-driven runs — this lets the user create a "scratch" agent with no template and drive everything from cards.

For manual runs (the palette's `Run agent:`), the agent's `promptTemplate` is used. Backend pre-flight in `start_run` rejects empty oneshot prompts before spawn with a clear error message.

### Drag-drop into terminal panes (v0.7.3)

Two source paths handled by `Terminal.tsx::handleHtmlDrop`:

1. **Internal (FilesPanel → tile):** `dataTransfer` carries the path under `application/x-atelier-path` (or `text/plain` fallback). Read it, shell-quote, write to PTY.
2. **External (Finder → tile):** `dataTransfer.files` has File objects. Browser File API doesn't expose `.path`, so we round-trip the bytes through `fs_save_dropped_file` (the bytes as the raw request body, the name in a header) which writes to `<app_cache>/dropped/<uuid>-<safe_name>` and returns the resulting absolute path. The path is then shell-quoted and pasted into the PTY.

The legacy `atelier:window-drop` listener (Tauri WindowEvent::DragDrop) stays in place as harmless dead code under `dragDropEnabled: false`. It would resume working if the setting is ever flipped.

### Navigation sidebar (v1.82.0)

`WorkspaceSidebar` is a single navigation column (UX-3.3). The switcher at its top holds the workspaces; below it an app section (Search, Inbox, History) and the current workspace's section under its name — Board · Code · Terminals · Library. Rows are 32 px with a Lucide icon; the selected one is a `--selected` fill. Under Terminals, one row per live session from `sessionRows` (8 at most, then Show all); a click reopens a Builder's card or opens Terminals with the tile expanded, and never touches a PTY. Status rows, "N ready to ship" and Settings live in the foot. This replaced the v0.7 multi-expand model, the v1.40 switcher/detail-pane model and the v1.82 one-row-per-workspace list.

### Run Detail retirement

The old `RightTabs::RunDetailContent` and `RunListItem` fallback are retired. Live runs are reached through Terminals and the sidebar session rows; completed run history is in Activity. No current right-panel component consumes `selectedRunId`.

### Command Blocks (v0.10.0)

When `behavior.commandBlocksEnabled` is on AND the user has sourced the OSC 133 shell integration script:

1. Shell emits `OSC 133;A` (prompt start), `OSC 133;C` (command exec), `OSC 133;D;<exit>` (command done).
2. `Terminal.tsx` registers `xterm.parser.registerOscHandler(133, ...)` *unconditionally* (so toggling the setting doesn't tear down the PTY). Handler tracks one open `CommandBlock` at a time, accumulates closed blocks per pane (cap 200).
3. `CommandBlocksIndicator` overlay (bottom-right of pane) renders only when both setting on AND blocks > 0. Shows `✓ N · ✗ M`; click expands a popover with the most recent 15 commands (exit code + duration + clock time).

### v0.11.0 hardening (data flow deltas)

- **Transcript cap.** Per-run `<runId>.log` files are capped at 50 MB (`TRANSCRIPT_MAX_BYTES`). The PTY data listener now consults a pure `cap_decision(written, incoming, cap, truncated)` helper before writing; once the cap is crossed, a single marker line is appended and further bytes drop. The `bytes_written` counter and `agent://output` offsets stay accurate (the marker is not counted).
- **Inflight guard.** `AgentRegistry::inflight: Arc<Mutex<HashSet<String>>>` (agent ids). `start_run` checks at the top and returns an error when the same agent is already running. Cleanup is RAII (`InflightGuard` Drop) on error paths; on the success path the guard is disarmed and the PTY exit listener removes the slot.
- **Reconcile_orphans.** Now returns `Vec<String>` of run ids it rewrote from pending/running → killed. `lib.rs::setup` calls it explicitly after both stores exist, then passes the ids to `KanbanStore::reconcile_orphan_runs(&[String])`, which clears `linkedRunId` and sets `lastRunStatus = failed` on matching cards.
- **shell_env refactor.** `OnceLock<ShellEnv>` → `RwLock<Arc<ShellEnv>>` so the cached state can be replaced via `re_probe()` (no app restart). New fields: `claude_version: Option<String>` (probed via `claude --version`, parsed for first `\d+\.\d+\.\d+` token), `probe_error: Option<String>` (captures `bash -lc` failure or empty PATH). New `ShellEnv::supports_stream_json()` gates on `MIN_CLAUDE_VERSION_STREAM_JSON = (1, 0, 0)`.
- **Stream-json fallback.** `agents.rs::start_run` no longer unconditionally passes `--output-format stream-json --verbose`; the args + the `StreamParserState` are gated on `shell_env::get().supports_stream_json()`. Old CLIs run in plain text mode (no token / cost / model tracking, but the run still succeeds).
- **Command Blocks v2.** Each `CommandBlock` now carries an `IMarker` anchored at the `OSC 133;C` row and an `IDecoration` registered at column 0 of that row when the `D` event fires. Decoration's `onRender` paints `✓` or `✗` with a tinted background. Popover items are buttons that call `term.scrollLines(marker.line - viewportY)` to jump to the block. Markers + decorations are disposed when blocks roll off the cap or on terminal teardown.
